# Review 7: the ownership protocol enumerated, and the base under it

Reviewer: Claude (Opus 5.5) in Claude Code, 2026-10-07. Subject: `bead` at e9eed92 on `main`.
Finding ids: **G** (not used in `FINDINGS.md`). Tests: `tests/test_review7.py`. Nothing else in the
repository was modified and nothing was committed.

65 findings: 8 High, 31 Medium, 26 Low. G01-G62 were each reproduced by at least two independent agents and checked once
more by a third (see "How this review was run"); G63 and G64 were reported by one agent and
reproduced by me, G65 is mine; every one has a test that fails on e9eed92. None of them is a §2
decision; where a finding touches one, it is a problem *with* the decision and says so.

The short version. The ownership protocol itself held: an enumerated sweep of 3,300 schedules
(claim, release, takeover, close, delete, three kinds of `update --claim`, and sync, at seven fault
boundaries, each followed by every ordered pair of four follow-ups, on three hosts, with and
without `git gc` on the remote, sequential and concurrent) found one defect, a retried `close`
that never publishes (G12). The base it stands on did not hold. A repository you clone can make
bead run a command (G01) or attach your checkout to another project's store and publish that
store to a remote of its choosing (G02). Many ordinary git setups give bead a different remote or
workspace than git uses (G03, G04, G05, G13, G20-G26), and two refs or two workspaces for one
tracker is exactly where online claims stop being exclusive (G03, G04 and G13 produce two
confirmed owners of one issue). The command receipts that make retries safe are dropped too early
and kept too long (G07, G08), and push outcomes are read from free text (G11, G18).

## Verdict: what must still be true before other people run this on their own repositories

Ranked. Each item names the findings that must be closed for it to hold.

1. **A repository you clone cannot run commands through bead, read or publish another project's
   tracker, or plant a credential in a file bead tells you to commit.** Today a committed
   `.beadroll.json` with `"remote": "--upload-pack=…"` runs that command on the first `claim` or
   `sync` (G01). A committed `.beadroll.json` can name another repository's workspace id (a public
   function of its origin URL) plus a remote of its own: `bead ready` in that checkout lists the
   other project's issues, `bead sync` publishes them to the committer's remote, and the other
   project's own checkouts then coordinate through that remote (G02). `init --tracked --remote
   https://TOKEN:x-oauth-basic@…` writes the token into the file it tells you to commit (G06; G39
   for `transport::` URLs). The tracked config is untrusted input and has to be treated as such.
2. **bead coordinates through the remote, the ref and the workspace git would use, from every
   checkout of the repository, whatever the locale, ssh setup or git configuration.** An ssh alias
   origin (`work:org/repo.git`, `gh:org/repo`) gives every clone and worktree its own tracker and a
   remote that does not exist (G05). A `meta.json` bead cannot read silently moves coordination to
   origin, where a second machine then gets a confirmed claim on an issue another machine already
   holds (G03). Re-running the documented `init --tracked` drops `ref` and `where`: two refs, two
   owners (G04). A non-UTF-8 locale reads `.beadroll.json` differently: two refs, two owners (G13).
   Per-repository and `includeIf` transport settings (CI tokens, multi-account ssh keys) never
   reach bead's private repository (G20); `pushurl` is ignored (G22); a relative remote depends on
   the cwd (G21); an older worktree or bisect is a different workspace and is told to `init` a
   second one (G23); a rename splits the tracker (G24); the first remote ever used outranks a moved
   origin (G25); a SHA-256 project cannot sync (G26); a translated git cannot do the first sync of
   any tracker (G17); the user's own pre-push hooks run on bead's journal pushes and can wedge every
   claim (G19); a half-created `sync.git` is never repaired (G27); a relative or `~` `BEAD_HOME`
   puts the store inside the checkout (G35).
3. **Every command's answer matches what the remote holds.** The enumerated sweep shows the core
   protocol does this. The edges do not: a settled receipt answers a later, deliberate repeat of the
   same command (G07, "Claimed" while nobody holds the issue); receipts are dropped before the
   command that drops them went through (G08, against §2's own wording); a recognised retry reports
   success after the claim moved (G10); text a server hook prints decides the push outcome, so an
   applied release answers `NOT_OWNER` and a bundled edit is applied twice (G11); a retried owner's
   close answers success and publishes nothing (G12); an ordinary lost race after a settled receipt
   crashes (G09); unlocked readers delete pending attempts and receipts (G28); an OSError after
   acceptance is reported as "nothing half-done" (G29); a superseded close says "Closed" (G65); with
   `--json` an unpublished close looks published (G37).
4. **A definite refusal is reported as one, with git's reason, and several agents on one machine
   do not trip over each other.** A declining hook costs twelve rounds and ends `SYNC_CONFLICT` "the
   remote journal kept moving"; a 403 or a read-only key ends "the push got no answer" and leaves a
   claim `UNRESOLVED` forever (G18). Concurrent bead processes on one machine, the tool's own use
   case, fail 15 of 21 commands with a false `REMOTE_UNREACHABLE` because they share
   `refs/beads/remote` (G16). G58 and G41 are the same family.
5. **Imported dependencies follow causality, not clocks.** F09's fix is incomplete: whether a
   retracted dependency comes back depends on which of two concurrent events has the earlier
   timestamp (G14). New and renamed keys capture other records' pending dependencies (G15, G42); a
   duplicate id in one import silently becomes two issues (G33); a self-dependency imports (G46).
6. **Import and export round-trip with `br`, and a damaged store is reported, not worked around.**
   bead cannot import its own export when text holds U+2028 (G31); it exports values `br` refuses
   (G32, G43, G44); one deeply nested import bricks every Python 3.12/3.13 machine of the crew
   (G30); non-UTF-8 git output (a branch name) is a traceback (G34); an unreadable journal directory
   is taken for an empty one, which forks the machine's own journal (G52), after which every
   `create` is a traceback and every other write is silently inert (G64); a hung-up terminal turns a
   committed command into exit 120 (G36); line-level integrity reports vanish (G63); an import
   whose comments have the wrong shape is a traceback (G38).
7. Then the Low items: the store's file modes (G47, table below), temporary-file leftovers (G48),
   tracebacks on malformed local state (G49, G55, G56, G57), lost machine aliases (G50), ignored
   config symlinks (G51), misleading advice (G53, G62), terminal output (G59, G60), the merged check
   (G61), invisible pending dependencies (G45), and URLs with `@` in the path (G40).

What does not need to move first: the ownership protocol under the enumerated faults (one defect,
G12), the shipped suites on native Linux and on Python 3.12, human output on a PTY, and remote
maintenance (`git gc`) between and during operations. Each is a verified property below.

## The ownership sweep

**What it enumerates.** Three machines (m1, m2, m3) and an observer that only reads the remote,
over one local bare remote. Two actors on m1 (threads `m1-a1`, `m1-a2`), one each on m2 and m3.
Issues Q (an old parent), X (the target, a child of Q), Y, Z and P (a new parent).

- **Operations** (by `m1-a1`): `claim X`; `release X` (A1 holds X); `takeover X` (m2 holds X);
  `close X` and `delete X` (A1 holds X); `update X --claim --title … --add-label …`;
  `update X --claim --parent P`; `update X --claim --status closed`; `sync` (m1 has a local
  comment to publish and m2 has just published a claim m1 must copy in).
- **Fault boundaries:** `BEAD_TEST_CRASH_BEFORE_PUSH`; `BEAD_TEST_CRASH_AFTER_PUSH`; a pre-receive
  hook that stalls the first push past `BEAD_NET_TIMEOUT=1` and lets later pushes (the fence)
  through, so the fence wins (*timeout-fenced*); the same but later pushes wait behind the first,
  which lands (*timeout-landed*); SIGKILL right after `confirm_ownership` and before `drop_pending`
  (*kill-confirm*); SIGKILL in the middle of writing the pending attempt file, half its bytes under
  the temporary name (*kill-write-pending*); the same for the confirmed attempt file in `claims/`
  (*kill-write-confirm*). close, delete and sync publish through `synchronise`, which has no crash
  hook; for them the two crash boundaries are reached by a wrapper that exits right before (98) or
  right after (99) the first successful `git push`. The kill and write faults are injected by
  wrapping module globals of an unmodified `bead` (`runpy`), as `test_protocol` already does.
  close/delete never confirm or write an attempt, so those three boundaries do not apply to them;
  a sync copies other machines' attempts, so *kill-write-confirm* applies to it. That leaves 55
  (operation, boundary) cells.
- **Follow-ups:** R, a retry of the same command by the same actor; D, a different command by the
  same actor (`claim Z`); O, a command by the other actor on m1 (`claim X`); S, a `sync` on m2.
  Every ordered pair of two: 12 per cell, so **660 schedules**. Then two full sync rounds.
- **Checks after every step:** each machine's local `show X` counts no confirmed claim the remote
  never accepted; generations never go back on any machine; no `lost_claims`; and the command's
  answer matches what the remote holds right after it (a successful claim, takeover or claiming
  update is held by that actor on the remote, with its bundled title, labels or parent; a refusal
  rests on the remote's state or on this machine's own unpublished writes; a release leaves no claim
  of that actor; a published close or delete is on the remote; a sync's export equals the
  remote's). **After settling:** identical exports, `show` of all five issues and `claims` on the
  three machines; no `waiting_events`, integrity entries or pending attempts; no `forked_journals`
  in any sync result; no process left behind.

**Results.**

| host | mode | schedules | pass | fail |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| macOS 26.6, Python 3.14.7, git 2.55.0 | sequential | 660 | 650 | 10 (all G12) |
| Linux 7.0 (linux-1), Python 3.12.3, git 2.43.0 | sequential | 660 | 650 | 10 (all G12) |
| Linux 7.1 (linux-2), Python 3.14.7, git 2.55.0 | sequential | 660 | 650 | 10 (all G12) |
| linux-1, `git gc --prune=now` on the remote after every accepted push | sequential + gc | 660 | 650 | 10 (all G12) |
| linux-1, the two follow-ups run at once | concurrent | 330 | 326 | 4 (all G12) |
| linux-1, concurrent + gc after every push | concurrent + gc | 330 | 326 | 4 (all G12) |

3,300 schedules; per sequential run 12,240 bead commands besides the observer's. The failing set is
identical on all three hosts: close and delete × crash-before-push × {RD, RO, RS, OR, SR}, i.e. every
pair in which the retry R runs before anything else of m1's publishes (in DR the different command
publishes the close first and the retry is then right). That is G12.

Every applicable boundary fired, identically across its 12 schedules (exit status of the operation
under fault, linux-1 sequential run):

| op | crash-before-push | crash-after-push | timeout-fenced | timeout-landed | kill-confirm | kill-write-pending | kill-write-confirm |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| claim | 98 | 99 | 0 | 1 UNRESOLVED | -9 | -9 | -9 |
| release | 98 | 99 | 0 | 1 UNRESOLVED | -9 | -9 | -9 |
| takeover | 98 | 99 | 0 | 1 UNRESOLVED | -9 | -9 | -9 |
| close | 98 | 99 | 0 | 0 | n/a | n/a | n/a |
| delete | 98 | 99 | 0 | 0 | n/a | n/a | n/a |
| update-edits | 98 | 99 | 0 | 1 UNRESOLVED | -9 | -9 | -9 |
| update-parent | 98 | 99 | 0 | 1 UNRESOLVED | -9 | -9 | -9 |
| update-closed | 98 | 99 | 0 | 1 UNRESOLVED | -9 | -9 | -9 |
| sync | 98 | 99 | 1 REMOTE_UNREACHABLE | 1 REMOTE_UNREACHABLE | n/a | n/a | -9 |

(close and delete exit 0 under both timeouts because the close is recorded locally and the publish
failure is a warning, which `--json` drops: G37.) Follow-up answers over the 660 schedules: R 302 ×
0, 10 × `NOT_OWNER`, 10 × `VALIDATION`, 8 × `ALREADY_CLAIMED`; D 330 × 0; O 102 × 0, 148 ×
`ALREADY_CLAIMED`, 80 × `VALIDATION`; S 330 × 0. I checked every non-zero retry by hand: each is the
designed answer (an attempt that never landed loses to the other actor; after a different command
that went through, or an operation that had already completed, the retry is a new command, §2).

**Verified property** (`tests/test_review7.py`, `Sweep`): for every schedule, at most one confirmed
owner, generations monotonic, no machine counting a claim the remote never accepted, identical
exports, `show` and `claims` everywhere after settling, no waiting events, integrity entries,
pending attempts or forked journals, and each command's answer consistent with the remote, except
the ten G12 schedules. The module carries the driver (`Run`, `run_schedule`, `schedules`); by
default it runs a ten-schedule slice that touches every operation and every boundary (95 s);
`BEAD_SWEEP=full` runs all 660 and fails on anything but the G12 ten; `BEAD_SWEEP_GC=1` and
`BEAD_SWEEP_CONCURRENT=1` select the variants. The parallel runner is in my scratch directory
(`review7/run_sweep.py`).

A methodological note: the first concurrent run reported 95 failures. 91 were my judge's, not
bead's: it compared each concurrent command with the remote *after both*, so a sync that
legitimately ran before the other machine's push looked stale. With the judge accepting the remote
either before or after the pair, the rerun left only G12.

## Findings (65 findings: 8 High, 31 Medium, 26 Low)

Ranked by the brief's scale, with security first. Ids were assigned before the last calibration, so
they are not strictly in rank order; the list is. Every finding has at least one test in
`tests/test_review7.py` that fails on e9eed92 because of the bug (an assertion with the finding's
message, not a harness error) and passes with its diff; each diff below was generated with
`diff -u` against e9eed92 and applies with `patch -p1`. Diffs within one area overlap in places
(they were written to be reviewed one at a time); combined versions that apply together are in my
scratch directory (`final/receipts-combined.diff`, `dangling-combined.diff`, `urls-combined.diff`,
`git-env-combined.diff`, `push-outcomes-combined.diff`), each with the shipped suites run against it.
G08's diff subsumes G09's (they change the same lines).

### High

#### G01 · High · security — A tracked .beadroll.json `remote` beginning with `-` reaches git as an option, so `--upload-pack=<cmd>` runs an arbitrary command on claim/sync

**Mechanism.** ws.remote() (bead:162-173) returns the config/meta/origin value after only without_password() (bead:107-120), which leaves a value with no scheme:// unchanged; checked_config (bead:179-187) only requires `remote` to be a str. Link.fetch builds run_git(["fetch","--quiet","--no-tags", self.url, f"+{ref}:{REMOTE_REF}"]) (bead:1624) and Link.publish builds run_git(["push","--quiet", self.url, f"{commit}:{ref}"]) (bead:1772), both with self.url as a positional and no `--` separator. git parses a value starting with `-` as an option, and `--upload-pack=<cmd>` makes it run <cmd> for a local-path fetch. .beadroll.json is a committed, repository-controlled file, and prime/onboard (bead:2601-2609) tell agents to run claim/close/sync, which reach fetch/push.

**Reproduction.** In a sandbox, commit .beadroll.json = {"workspace":"ws-g01-0001","remote":"--upload-pack=touch <marker> ;"}; bead init --prefix ex; bead create work; bead claim <id> --json (then bead sync --json).

**Observed.** claim/sync exit 1 with REMOTE_UNREACHABLE but the marker file is created: {"error": {"code": "REMOTE_UNREACHABLE", "message": "cannot reach the coordination remote --upload-pack=touch <box>/PWNED ;: touch <box>/PWNED ; '+refs/beads/journal:refs/beads/remote': ... No such file or directory"}}

**Expected.** A repository-controlled remote string must never be interpreted as a git option; fetch/push pass it after `--`, and an option-looking remote fails cleanly without running anything.

**Proposed fix.** Add a `--` separator before self.url in Link.fetch (bead:1624) and Link.publish (bead:1772) so git always treats the remote as a pathname/URL. After the fix an option-shaped remote fails with `fatal: strange pathname ... blocked` (REMOTE_UNREACHABLE) and runs no command; honest ssh, scp-like, https and local-path (including a relative path beginning with `-`) remotes are unaffected.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G01_TrackedRemoteIsNeverAGitOption`. On e9eed92: AssertionError: True is not false : bead claim passed the tracked remote to git as an option and ran the command it named (rc=1) With the diff: OK (claim and sync refuse the option-shaped remote and create no marker; the dash-named relative local remote still publishes refs/beads/journal)

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Checker, combined copy (G01 + corrected G02 + G13 + G51, all applied cleanly with patch -p1, no conflicts), run from a copy of tests/ with scratch BEAD_HOME/TMPDIR: run sequentially, tests.test_protocol: Ran 54 tests OK; tests.test_commands: Ran 97 tests OK. Also tests.test_storage: Ran 9 tests OK; tests.test_replay: Ran 60 tests OK. In an earlier run of six suites in parallel, test_protocol test_timed_out_accepted_release_returns_success failed on BOTH the pristine and the patched copy (timing under load) and test_commands HelpAndPrime hit a `git clone` exit 128 in setUp; both pass when rerun alone on both copies. tests.test_compat: 2 OK, 1 not runnable here (test_all_available_real_exports_and_br_bv_consumers looks for infra/compat_real.py next to the bead copy), unrelated to these diffs. No shipped test needs adapting.

+++ b/bead
@@ -1621,7 +1621,7 @@

             if created.returncode and not (self.git_dir / "HEAD").exists():

#### G02 · High · security — A committed .beadroll.json can bind one checkout to another repository's store (its URL-derived workspace id) and silently redirect an existing store's coordination remote

**Mechanism.** A workspace id is a pure public function of the origin URL: find_workspace derives wid = sha256(normalize_remote(origin))[:16] (bead:211-212), and Workspace.__init__ (bead:137) accepts any opaque token, so a 16-hex id names a specific repository's store predictably. find_workspace takes config.get('workspace') verbatim (bead:210), so a tracked config may name another repository's derived id; operating in that checkout then uses the victim's existing store at $BEAD_HOME/<id>. Workspace.remote (bead:162-166) ranks self.config.get('remote') above self.meta().get('remote') and self.origin, and synchronise (bead:1868) and coordinated (bead:1896,1933) persist the used remote into meta.json, so a config remote reached from a foreign checkout becomes the store's permanent last-used remote. A07 only closed path-escaping ids, not valid foreign ids. cmd_init (bead:1976) saves meta.remote=None for `init --tracked`, so the store's recorded remote is whatever a later sync used, which is how a tracked-config edit or a foreign config's remote becomes permanent.

**Reproduction.** Victim repo P (origin origin.git), workspace id = sha256(normalize_remote(origin.git))[:16]. Attacker repo M commits .beadroll.json = {"workspace": <P's id>, "remote": <attacker collect.git>}. Victim clones M and runs `bead ready`/`bead sync` inside it. Separately: a benign tracked config {workspace: uuid7, remote: good.git}; edit its `remote` to evil.git and run `bead sync`.

**Observed.** Checker rerun on pristine e9eed92: inside the hostile checkout `bead ready --json` exits 0 and lists the victim's issue {"id": "p-w3ej", "title": "SECRET acquisition plan", ...}; after editing the tracked remote to evil.git, `bead sync --json` exits 0 with {"pulled": 0, "pushed": 2, ..., "remote": ".../evil.git"}. Reporter/verifier also saw: the attacker's collect.git then holds a blob with the victim's issue and P's own `bead info` reports remote=<collect.git>.

**Expected.** A tracked workspace id that equals another repository's URL-derived id (16-hex or local-) is refused as CONFIG unless minted by this checkout's own origin, BEAD_WORKSPACE, or an init-minted uuid7. A tracked `remote` that disagrees with an existing store's last-used remote is refused as CONFIG rather than silently switching it; switching stays possible with explicit `--remote`/BEAD_REMOTE or `init --tracked --remote`.

**Proposed fix.** (1) find_workspace: compute the origin-derived id, and if a tracked workspace is a 16-hex or local- id that differs from this checkout's derived id (and BEAD_WORKSPACE is unset), raise CONFIG. (2) Factor the remote resolution into Workspace.resolve_remote; in Workspace.remote, when there is no explicit override and the tracked remote resolves to something other than the store's recorded last-used remote, raise CONFIG. (3) publish_soon resolves the remote inside its try so the new CONFIG becomes a 'recorded locally' note rather than crashing a local close/release. (4) cmd_init records the resolved remote in meta.json when `init --tracked --remote X` is run, so the documented switch (and the route the new CONFIG message names) actually takes effect. Checker note: the group's original diff lacked (4); with it, `bead init --tracked --remote <new>` left meta.remote at the old endpoint and every later `sync`/`info` failed with CONFIG ('.beadroll.json names coordination remote .../third.git, but this tracker already coordinates through ...'). uuid7 ids from init --tracked do not match the derived-id pattern, so the supported cross-origin sharing path is unaffected (the repo's own .beadroll.json is a 36-char uuid). Behaviour change to note: after a deliberate team edit of the tracked remote, each machine with an existing store must consent once (`bead sync --remote X` or `init --tracked --remote X`), and `bead info` reports the CONFIG error until then.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G02_TrackedConfigCannotAdoptAnotherRepositorysStore` (2 tests). On e9eed92: AssertionError: Tuples differ: (0, None) != (1, 'CONFIG') : a foreign checkout read the victim's store: rc=0 out=[{"id": "p-w3ej", "title": "SECRET acquisition plan", ... (and in the second method: Tuples differ: (0, None) != (1, 'CONFIG') : the store's remote was switched by an edited config without consent) With the diff: OK, 2 tests (ready/sync inside the hostile checkout refuse with CONFIG, the victim's journal does not reach the config's remote, P's own remote stays origin, and the uuid7 shared config still joins across origins; an edited remote is refused, an explicit --remote switch works, and `init --tracked -…

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Checker, combined copy (G01 + corrected G02 + G13 + G51, all applied cleanly with patch -p1, no conflicts), run from a copy of tests/ with scratch BEAD_HOME/TMPDIR: run sequentially, tests.test_protocol: Ran 54 tests OK; tests.test_commands: Ran 97 tests OK. Also tests.test_storage: Ran 9 tests OK; tests.test_replay: Ran 60 tests OK. In an earlier run of six suites in parallel, test_protocol test_timed_out_accepted_release_returns_success failed on BOTH the pristine and the patched copy (timing under load) and test_commands HelpAndPrime hit a `git clone` exit 128 in setUp; both pass when rerun alone on both copies. tests.test_compat: 2 OK, 1 not runnable here (test_all_available_real_exports_and_br_bv_consumers looks for infra/compat_real.py next to the bead copy), unrelated to these diffs. No shipped test needs adapting.

#### G06 · High · privacy — An https token in the user position (https://TOKEN:x-oauth-basic@host, https://TOKEN:@host) is written to .beadroll.json and meta.json and shown by info/sync, while bead says the password was dropped

**Mechanism.** without_password (bead:107-120) keeps the user part whenever the authority has a ':' (bead:115-117: `user = authority.split(":", 1)[0]; return f"{scheme}{user}@{tail}"`). The F03 rule that an http(s) user part may be the token (bead:118-119) is only reached when there is no ':'. For GitHub's documented `https://<token>:x-oauth-basic@github.com/...` and for `https://<token>:@...` the user part IS the token (a local recording server shows git sending `Basic TOKEN:x-oauth-basic`). cmd_init stores the once-cleaned value (bead:1960, .beadroll.json written at bead:1968 with private=False, meta at bead:1976), and the function is not idempotent: Workspace.remote (bead:172) applies it again and drops the token, so info hides what the files hold. For an origin with that shape the value is cleaned only once, so info and the sync result or error show the token, and a successful sync saves it to meta.json (bead:1868).

**Reproduction.** `bead init --prefix ex --tracked --remote https://ghp_TOKEN0123:x-oauth-basic@example.invalid/org/repo.git`, then read .beadroll.json and `bead info --json`; `bead init --remote https://ghp_TOKEN0123:@example.invalid/org/repo.git` then meta.json; `git remote set-url origin http://ghp_TOKEN0123:x-oauth-basic@127.0.0.1:<closed port>/org/repo.git`, `bead init`, `bead info --json`, `bead sync --json`. Test: final/G06.py.

**Observed.** e9eed92: init prints `the password was dropped from the remote URL: bead never stores one; use a git credential helper`, yet `.beadroll.json: { "workspace": "01a1180c-...", "remote": "https://ghp_TOKEN0123@example.invalid/org/repo.git" }` while `info remote: https://example.invalid/org/repo.git`; `meta (TOKEN:@): { "name": "origin", "remote": "https://ghp_TOKEN0123@example.invalid/org/repo.git" }`; origin form: `info remote: http://ghp_TOKEN0123@127.0.0.1:50107/org/repo.git` and `{"error": {"code": "REMOTE_UNREACHABLE", "message": "cannot reach the coordination remote http://ghp_TOKEN0123@127.0.0.1:50107/org/repo.git: fatal: unable to access 'http://127.0.0.1:50107/org/repo.git/': ...` (git's own part is redacted, bead's is not).

**Expected.** Under bead's own rule that an http(s) user part may be the secret, nothing of an http(s) user-info is stored, shown or used; the stored value equals the value bead uses (without_password idempotent), so no token reaches the committed .beadroll.json, meta.json or output.

**Proposed fix.** In without_password, test the http(s) case before the user:password case, so every http(s) user-info is dropped (`https://TOKEN:x-oauth-basic@h` -> `https://h`); ssh:// and other schemes keep `user@` as before. This changes what bead uses for an http(s) remote that had user:password: `https://alice@host` before, `https://host` now. That is what bead already did for an init-stored value (second application in Workspace.remote) and, since F03, for a user-only origin, but it changes two assertions of tests/test_commands.py Review6.test_remote_url_shapes_that_are_already_safe (lines 1746 and 1773), which expect `https://alice@example.invalid/private.git`; G06.diff carries that hunk (it encoded a stored value bead itself does not use). The init note still says `the password was dropped` for these shapes (accurate: there was a password field), left as is. Conflicts with G39 and G40 (all three rewrite without_password); final/urls-combined.diff holds the merged function.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G06_HttpsTokenInTheUserPosition` (2 tests). On e9eed92: FAIL (failures=3): `AssertionError: 'ghp_G06TESTTOKEN0123' unexpectedly found in '{\n "workspace": "01a11810-64c3-...",\n "remote": "https://ghp_G06TESTTOKEN0123@example.invalid/org/repo.git"\n}\n' : the token is in .beadroll.json, which bead says to commit` (x-oauth-basic and empty-password), and `AssertionError: 'ghp_G06TESTTOKEN0123' unexpectedly found in '{"workspace": "f1783f4945b08bad", "workspace_source": "origin url", ... "remote": "http://ghp_G06TESTTOKEN0123@127.0.0.1:...` (origin). With the diff: work-urls/G06/bead: Ran 2 tests in 2.975s OK; combined copy: OK.

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Per-diff selection run from the repo (BpTest.test_worktrees_and_clones_share_one_tracker_and_checkouts_stay_clean, BpTest.test_claim_needs_the_remote_unless_offline_is_asked_for, BpTest.test_tracked_workspace_id_and_a_separate_coordination_remote, CompatAuditCLI.test_workspace_config_cannot_escape_home, CompatAuditCLI.test_changed_workspace_config_does_not_delete_old_store, Review5CLI.test_e02_remote_credentials_are_not_disclosed_or_world_readable, Review6.test_f03_credential_urls_are_not_stored, Review6.test_remote_url_shapes_that_are_already_safe, plus all of test_replay) from the repo (unadapted tests) against work-urls/G06/bead: 'Ran 68 tests in 121.630s FAILED (failures=1)', only `FAIL: test_remote_url_shapes_that_are_already_safe ... AssertionError: 'https://alice@example.invalid/private.git' not found in '{\n "name": "origin",\n "remote": "https://example.invalid/private.git"\n}'` (the intended change); the same selection with G06's test hunk applied (shim-urls-shipped): 'Ran 68 tests in 67.363s OK'. With all five urls diffs applied together (final/urls-combined.diff = G05 + G21 + a hand merge of G06/G39/G40 + G06's test hunk; BEAD_UNDER_TEST=work-urls/all/bead, test_commands taken from that copy): test_commands 'Ran 97 tests in 305.739s OK'; test_protocol 'Ran 54 tests in 227.513s OK'; test_replay 'Ran 60 tests in 109.659s OK'; test_storage 'Ran 9 tests in 30.635s OK' …

#### G03 · High · divergence — A meta.json that cannot be read or parsed silently moves coordination from the remembered remote to origin: claims settle against origin (two confirmed owners), the tracker is pushed into the code origin, and save_meta makes the switch permanent

**Mechanism.** Workspace.meta (bead:143-148) turns OSError, ValueError and a non-object into {}, so an unreadable file is indistinguishable from 'never set'. Workspace.remote (bead:164-165) then falls through override, BEAD_REMOTE, config.remote and meta['remote'] to self.origin. Without --tracked the coordination remote is recorded only in meta.json (cmd_init bead:1976, synchronise bead:1868, coordinated bead:1896/1900/1933). coordinated (bead:1883) links to origin; Link.fetch treats 'find remote ref' as nothing published (bead:1627); the claim is decided against an empty head and published there, creating refs/beads/journal on the code origin. The workspace id is derived from origin, so no WORKSPACE_MISMATCH stops it. save_meta (bead:150-152) merges {} with remote=origin and os.replace overwrites even a file the user cannot read, so the switch is permanent. An owner's close takes the same path through publish_soon (bead:1945), and re-running `bead init` (bead:1976) also overwrites the damaged file, dropping the remote.

**Reproduction.** In a Sandbox with origin.git and coord.git: a: `bead init --prefix ex --remote <coord.git>`; `bead create contested`; `bead sync`. b: `bead sync --remote <coord.git>`. Append a trailing comma to b's <BEAD_HOME>/<ws>/meta.json (or chmod 0 it). a: `bead claim X`; b: `bead claim X --json`; `git --git-dir origin.git for-each-ref`; cat b's meta.json.

**Observed.** b meta before: coord.git | a claim exit 0 codex:a-thread-0001@510de157 | b claim exit 0 codex:b-thread-0001@827285aa | origin refs: ['refs/beads/journal', 'refs/heads/main'] | b meta after: { "remote": "<sandbox>/origin.git", "last_sync": "2026-10-07T20:25:56Z" }

**Expected.** Only a missing meta.json means 'no remote remembered'. When meta.json decides the remote and cannot be read or parsed, coordinated commands fail with a structured error naming the file and never fall back to origin; a remote named with --remote (or from BEAD_REMOTE or a tracked config) still works and rewrites the file; an owner's close is recorded and reported as not yet published; a file bead could not read is not overwritten by a command that has no remote to put in it.

**Proposed fix.** meta(strict=False) stays the default for display callers; remote() reads meta(strict=True), which is evaluated only when no flag, BEAD_REMOTE or config remote was given, and raises IO naming the file when it cannot be read or parsed, is not an object, or holds a non-string remote (a missing file is still {}). save_meta does not rewrite a damaged file unless it is writing a remote (which can only have come from flag/env/config, so the rewrite repairs it). publish_soon resolves the remote inside its try, so an owner's close reports 'recorded locally, not yet published (IO)' with exit 0. cmd_info shows 'unknown (IO: ...)' as the remote instead of failing. This is the verifier's corrected diff (meta strict only in remote(), publish_soon, info) plus the save_meta guard for `bead init`. Residual risk, not fixed: a DELETED meta.json still falls back to origin; closing that needs the coordination URL recorded somewhere sturdier.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G03_DamagedMetaMovesCoordinationToOrigin` (3 tests). On e9eed92: AssertionError: 'refs/beads/journal' unexpectedly found in 'refs/beads/journal\nrefs/heads/main\n' : the tracker was published to the code origin (malformed and EACCES variants); AssertionError: 'refs/beads/journal' unexpectedly found in 'refs/beads/journal\nrefs/heads/main\n' : the owner's close published the tracker to the code origin. Ran 3 tests, FAILED (failures=3). With the diff: Ran 3 tests, OK (also OK against the combined group copy).

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Against work-remote-selection/G03/bead (G03.diff alone), run from the repo root with BEAD_UNDER_TEST set: tests.test_commands 'Ran 97 tests in 305.265s OK'; tests.test_protocol 'Ran 54 tests in 283.042s OK'. No shipped test needs adapting. Combined group copy: with all six diffs merged (work-remote-selection/ALL/bead): tests.test_protocol 'Ran 54 tests in 232.119s OK'; tests.test_storage 'Ran 9 tests in 28.231s OK'; tests.test_replay 'Ran 60 tests in 159.541s OK'; tests.test_commands 'Ran 97 tests in 325.490s OK'; all 15 group tests (G03 3, G49 2, G22 3, G23 2, G24 2, G25 3) OK. test_compat not run (it looks for infra/compat_real.py next to BEAD_UNDER_TEST and reads the real tracker exports). Load average was 80-180 during these runs; no flakes seen. Applied in the order G03, G49, G22, G23, G24, G25 to one pristine copy with `patch -p1 --forward`: G49's publish_soon hunk is rejected because it is identical to G03's (already applied; drop it); G23's find_workspace hunk is rejected because G22 rewrote its trailing context (insert the two-line `else: config = committed_config()` before G22's origin lookup); both G25 hunks are rejected against G03 (save_meta and the remote() `or` chain): merged form is G03's strict save_meta followed by G25's is_origin deletion, and remembered_remote() reading self.meta(strict=True). With G03 applied, G49's isinstance check in remote() is unreacha…

#### G05 · High · divergence — An origin git reads as ssh or a remote helper (host:path without user@, ssh-config aliases, gh: insteadOf shortcuts, [IPv6]:path, an upper-case scheme, transport::address) is joined onto the checkout as a relative path, so every clone and worktree gets its own tracker and sync never reaches the remote

**Mechanism.** find_workspace reads the raw origin (bead:205 `git config --get remote.origin.url`) and bead:207-208 joins it onto the worktree top whenever it does not match `^([a-z][a-z0-9+.-]*://|[^/]+@|/)` and does not exist relative to the process cwd. git's own rule (connect.c url_is_local_not_ssh, transport_get's `<transport>::` check, is_url accepting upper-case scheme letters) is different: a scheme:// or transport:: URL is never a path, and otherwise a ':' before the first '/' means scp-like ssh, with or without `user@`. So `myhost:org/repo.git`, `gh:org/repo`, `[::1]:org/repo.git`, `HTTPS://host/x` and `codecommit::us-east-1://my-repo` become `<checkout>/myhost:org/repo.git` etc. The workspace id (bead:211-212, sha256 of normalize_remote(origin)) then depends on the checkout path, so each clone and each linked worktree has its own store; the name (bead:209) becomes `myhost:org/repo`-like; Workspace.remote (bead:164-173) returns the joined path and Link.fetch (bead:1624-1630) fails with REMOTE_UNREACHABLE. Separately normalize_remote strips only lower-case schemes (bead:128), so `HTTPS://host/x` and `https://host/x` hash differently even once not joined.

**Reproduction.** In a sandbox clone: `git remote set-url origin myhost:org/repo.git`; `git worktree add ../one-wt`; a second clone with the same origin; `bead info --json` in the clone, the worktree, a subdirectory and the second clone. End to end: a fake ssh (GIT_SSH_COMMAND that runs the remote command in the sandbox root) so `git ls-remote origin` works for `myhost:tracker.git`; `bead init --prefix ex`, `bead create ...`, `bead sync --json`; same with a scratch GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL `[url "<root>/"] insteadOf = gh:` and origin `gh:tracker.git`. Test: final/G05.py.

**Observed.** e9eed92: `G05 clone: workspace=bd9812d54f58fa56 remote=<box>/one-main/myhost:org/repo.git` / `G05 worktree: workspace=1cbce26f56fc10e8 remote=<box>/one-wt/myhost:org/repo.git` / `G05 other clone: workspace=da071c4b9bdd081d remote=<box>/two-main/myhost:org/repo.git`. Sync: `{"error": {"code": "REMOTE_UNREACHABLE", "message": "cannot reach the coordination remote <box>/ssh-alias-a-main/myhost:tracker-ssh-alias.git: fatal: '<box>/ssh-alias-a-main/myhost:tracker-ssh-alias.git' does not appear to be a git repository...` while `git ls-remote origin` in the same checkout succeeds; the same for `gh:tracker-insteadof.git`. Upper case: `'987c37c1f56322a0' != '65748d5e515237d1'` for https:// vs HTTPS:// spellings of one URL.

**Expected.** As git reads them, these origins are remotes: one workspace id for every clone, worktree and subdirectory (the same id as the `user@host:path` spelling), Workspace.remote returns the URL itself so git's ssh config, insteadOf and remote helpers resolve it, and sync works. With the patch all four checkouts report 08b46a1826704893 and `remote=myhost:org/repo.git` (the same id as git@myhost:org/repo.git).

**Proposed fix.** Add is_local_path(), git's url_is_local_not_ssh plus the scheme://|transport:: check, placed before find_workspace; join the origin onto the top only when it is a relative local path (os.path.isabs instead of the cwd-relative os.path.exists, so a relative origin no longer depends on the cwd either); make normalize_remote strip the scheme case-insensitively. Not changed, on purpose: the origin is still read raw (not `git remote get-url`), so a machine that spells the remote `gh:org/repo` and one that spells the expanded URL still get different ids; expanding insteadOf would make the id depend on each machine's url rewrites (mirrors). Migration: stores created on e9eed92 under the per-checkout ids (or published with a `--remote` workaround) are left under the old ids; nothing is deleted and BEAD_WORKSPACE or a .beadroll.json with the old id recovers them; worth a release note. Cosmetic: normalize_remote spells `[::1]:org/repo.git` as `[/:1]:org/repo` (deterministic). normalize_remote does not strip a `transport::` prefix, so `https::https://h/r` and `https://h/r` remain different workspaces.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G05_OriginsGitReadsAsRemotes` (3 tests). On e9eed92: FAIL (failures=9): e.g. `AssertionError: 3 != 1 : git reads 'myhost:org/repo.git' as a remote, yet each checkout got its own tracker: {'clone': '92b16a9f42464141', 'worktree': 'd176189b8d080339', 'subdirectory': '92b16a9f42464141', 'other clone': 'f3df52c28ce7564e'}` (one per shape incl. [::1], HTTPS://, codecommit::), `AssertionError: 1 != 0 : bead could not sync through myhost:tracker-ssh-alias.git: {"error": {"code": "REMOTE_UNREACHABLE", ...` (and gh:tracker-insteadof.git), `AssertionError: '987c37c1f56322a0' != '65748d5e515237d1' : an upper-case scheme gave the same remote a different wo… With the diff: work-urls/G05/bead: Ran 3 tests in 4.604s OK; combined copy: OK.

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Per-diff selection run from the repo (BpTest.test_worktrees_and_clones_share_one_tracker_and_checkouts_stay_clean, BpTest.test_claim_needs_the_remote_unless_offline_is_asked_for, BpTest.test_tracked_workspace_id_and_a_separate_coordination_remote, CompatAuditCLI.test_workspace_config_cannot_escape_home, CompatAuditCLI.test_changed_workspace_config_does_not_delete_old_store, Review5CLI.test_e02_remote_credentials_are_not_disclosed_or_world_readable, Review6.test_f03_credential_urls_are_not_stored, Review6.test_remote_url_shapes_that_are_already_safe, plus all of test_replay) against work-urls/G05/bead: 'Ran 68 tests in 190.028s OK'. With all five urls diffs applied together (final/urls-combined.diff = G05 + G21 + a hand merge of G06/G39/G40 + G06's test hunk; BEAD_UNDER_TEST=work-urls/all/bead, test_commands taken from that copy): test_commands 'Ran 97 tests in 305.739s OK'; test_protocol 'Ran 54 tests in 227.513s OK'; test_replay 'Ran 60 tests in 109.659s OK'; test_storage 'Ran 9 tests in 30.635s OK' (load average ~190 during the runs). No shipped test needs adapting.

#### G08 · High · data-loss — A later coordinated command drops the actor's settled receipts right after its build, before its push; when it then fails (crash before push, UNRESOLVED, GIT_ERROR), the retry of the earlier accepted command is not recognised and is rebuilt, re-applying its edits over newer work

**Mechanism.** settle_commands (1805-1827) returns this actor's settled receipts as `stale`; coordinated unlinks them at 1910-1911 immediately after `result = build(tx, "online")` (1909): before append_events, before the new receipt and attempt are written (1918-1919), before the crash hook (1920), the publish (1923) and the next round's settle_pending (1902-1905). Every failure after build therefore loses them: BEAD_TEST_CRASH_BEFORE_PUSH (1920), publish raising GIT_ERROR (1925-1926), UNRESOLVED on the next round (1903-1905), SYNC_CONFLICT (1940). On the retry settle_commands finds no receipt and build runs again against the accepted state: a claiming close fails VALIDATION 'is closed', a release fails NOT_OWNER, and an accepted `update --claim --title` is applied a second time over another machine's later title, with no conflict recorded.

**Reproduction.** tests.support machines. (1) a: create X, Y; sync; `update X --claim --status closed --json` with BEAD_TEST_CRASH_AFTER_PUSH=1 (rc 99); `claim Y --json` with BEAD_TEST_CRASH_BEFORE_PUSH=1 (rc 98); retry the update. (2) a: claim X; sync; `release X --json` lost ack (rc 99); origin pre-receive hook 'while [ -d gate ]; do sleep 0.05; done; exit 1'; `claim Y --json` with BEAD_NET_TIMEOUT=1 (UNRESOLVED); remove gate and hook; retry `release X --json`. (3) a: `update X --claim --title from-A` lost ack; b: sync, `update X --title from-B`, sync; a: `claim Y` crash before push (rc 98); a: retry the update; sync both; b: show X. Tests: final/G08.py.

**Observed.** (1) AssertionError: 1 != 0 : retry of the accepted close not recognised: {"error": {"code": "VALIDATION", "message": "ex-4wpm is closed"}} (2) AssertionError: 1 != 0 : retry of the accepted release not recognised: {"error": {"code": "NOT_OWNER", "message": "ex-zr0u is not claimed by codex:g08-unresolved-thread-0001@f0816e89"}} (3) AssertionError: 'from-A' != 'from-B' (the accepted update was applied a second time, over the other machine's later title)

**Expected.** REVIEWING section 2: 'A command receipt survives a failed later command by the same actor and is dropped only when a later command by that actor goes through.' The retries exit 0 and apply nothing again; B's later title stands.

**Proposed fix.** coordinated: `stale` is initialised before the loop; the unlink loop after build is removed; the new forget(stale) runs only where this command has gone through: the no-ownership return, outcome "ok" after confirm_ownership/drop_pending, and the path where an earlier uncertain push of this command is found on the remote. The reporter's diff stopped there and has the regression the verifier found: when the later command's push lands although that process ends UNRESOLVED, nothing retires the older receipt, so re-running the older `release X` is answered 'Released' while the actor holds X. To avoid it, settle_commands also drops any settled receipt of this actor older (attempt ids are UUIDv7, so name order is time order) than its newest receipt whose attempt was accepted: that later command went through. Checked with the verifier's guard test_later_claim_that_landed_after_unresolved_retires_the_older_receipt: passes on e9eed92, FAILS on a reporter-style copy without the settle_commands rule ('release reported success but the actor still holds X'), passes on the G08 copy and on the combined copy. Removing the loop also removes the G09 TypeError (G08.diff and G09.diff touch the same two lines and conflict; the combined diff carries G08's version).

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G08_StaleReceiptsDroppedBeforeTheCommandGoesThrough` (3 tests). On e9eed92: FAILED (failures=3): AssertionError: 1 != 0 : retry of the accepted close not recognised: ... "VALIDATION" ... is closed / AssertionError: 1 != 0 : retry of the accepted release not recognised: ... "NOT_OWNER" ... / AssertionError: 'from-A' != 'from-B' With the diff: Ran 3 tests ... OK (G08 copy); also OK on the combined copy.

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Against the G08 copy: test_protocol 'Ran 54 tests in 314.772s OK'; test_commands 'Ran 97 tests in 426.471s OK'. Regression guard (verifier's test_later_claim_that_landed_after_unresolved_retires_the_older_receipt): ok on e9eed92, FAIL on a reporter-style copy without the settle_commands rule, ok on the G08 and combined copies. No shipped test needs adapting. Suites were run from an unchanged scratch copy of the repo's tests/ (work-receipts/suite/tests), cwd in scratch, with BEAD_UNDER_TEST set to the patched copy and BEAD_HOME/TMPDIR under work-receipts/. With all six receipts diffs applied together (receipts-combined.diff, work-receipts/combined/bead): test_protocol 'Ran 54 tests in 130.036s OK', test_commands 'Ran 97 tests in 196.742s OK', test_storage 'Ran 9 tests in 13.007s OK', test_replay 'Ran 60 tests in 52.644s OK'; all six G test files run together as one module: 12 failures on e9eed92, 'Ran 12 tests ... OK' on the combined copy.

#### G04 · High · wrong-ownership — Re-running `init --tracked [--remote X]` rewrites .beadroll.json from scratch and drops `ref`, `"where": false` and unknown keys: the checkout moves to refs/beads/journal while teammates stay on the committed ref (two machines then hold an online-confirmed claim on one issue), and branch/commit recording silently resumes

**Mechanism.** cmd_init bead:1965 builds a new dict `config = {"workspace": ws.config.get("workspace") or uuid7()}`, 1966-1967 add only `remote`, and 1968 write_atomic replaces the file, so every other key read by find_workspace (199-204) is lost. Workspace.ref (155-160) then falls back to DEFAULT_REF (38) `refs/beads/journal`: this checkout fetches, pushes and settles claims on a ref its teammates (whose committed .beadroll.json still names the custom ref) never read, so `coordinated` (1874) confirms a claim the other machines cannot see. capture_where (1161-1163) skips branch/commit only when `ws.config.get("where") is False`; after the rewrite every event records `where: {branch, commit}` again, published append-only. checked_config's docstring (179-181) promises unknown keys are left for newer versions; README:151-155 documents both `init --tracked --remote` and `"where": false` (the `ref` key is supported by CONFIG_KEYS/Workspace.ref but not documented in README).

**Reproduction.** Sandbox with origin; machine a: `bead init --prefix ex --tracked --remote <origin> --json`; hand-edit .beadroll.json to add "ref": "refs/beads/team", "where": false, "later_key": 7; commit and push it; machine b pulls; a `create 'shared work'`, a `sync`, b `sync`; a `init --tracked --remote <same origin> --json`; a `claim KEY` (thread a-agent) and b `claim KEY` (thread b-agent). Script: work-store-files/probe.py g04; test: G04.py.

**Observed.** hand-edited .beadroll.json: {"workspace": "01a1180a-b371-...", "remote": "<sandbox>/origin.git", "ref": "refs/beads/team", "where": false, "later_key": 7} / `a init --tracked --remote <same origin>: rc 0` / after re-init .beadroll.json: {"workspace": "01a1180a-b371-...", "remote": "<sandbox>/origin.git"} / `a ref: refs/beads/journal | b ref: refs/beads/team` / `last event where: {'branch': 'main', 'commit': 'b02fa664...'}` / `a claim rc 0: codex:a-agent@3edee04a` / `b claim rc 0: codex:b-agent@8ff32e1c` / `remote refs: ['refs/beads/journal', 'refs/beads/team']`.

**Expected.** init --tracked updates only the keys it manages (workspace, and remote when given) and keeps `ref`, `where` and unknown keys. Both machines stay on refs/beads/team, b's claim is refused with ALREADY_CLAIMED, and later events carry no `where`.

**Proposed fix.** Start the rewritten config from the existing one: `config = {**ws.config, "workspace": ...}`; `--remote` still overrides `remote`. Because a kept `remote` is now written back, a hand-set password in it is stripped with without_password() (bead never stores one; before the fix the key was dropped instead). Patched: `a ref: refs/beads/team | b ref: refs/beads/team`, `last event where: None`, `b claim rc 1: {"error": {"code": "ALREADY_CLAIMED", "message": "ex-lgtm is claimed by codex:a-agent@32520463 (generation 1, ...)"}}`, remote refs only refs/beads/team. Side effect: `init --tracked` without `--remote` now keeps an existing `remote` (it used to drop it).

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G04_TrackedInitKeepsHandSetConfig` (3 tests). On e9eed92: FAIL test_g04_reinit_keeps_where_false_ref_and_unknown_keys: `AssertionError: Tuples differ: (None, None, None) != (False, 'refs/beads/team', 1)` ... init --tracked dropped hand-set keys: {'workspace': '01a117ff-...', 'remote': '<sandbox>/other.git'}; FAIL test_g04_reinit_does_not_move_the_checkout_to_another_ref: `AssertionError: Lists differ: [0, 0] != [0, 1]` ... both machines hold a confirmed claim on the same issue: confirmed owner codex:g04-a-agent@ae9718fe | confirmed owner codex:g04-b-agent@935166e2; FAIL test_g04_reinit_does_not_write_back_a_hand_set_password: `AssertionError: None i… With the diff: Ran 3 tests in 2.205s OK (G04 alone); OK on the combined copy. Checker: the guard test's docstring was renamed to start with 'G04: ' (format rule); pristine 3 failures and patched OK reproduced as stated.

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Against this diff alone: G04 test_commands rc=0 342s: Ran 97 tests in 342.621s OK . Combined copy (all six store-files diffs, applied in order G04 G35 G47 G48 G50 G52 with `git apply`): ALL test_storage rc=0 24s: Ran 9 tests in 22.895s OK ; ALL test_protocol rc=0 212s: Ran 54 tests in 211.977s OK ; ALL test_commands rc=0 304s: Ran 97 tests in 304.071s OK ; ALL test_replay rc=0 114s: Ran 60 tests in 113.549s OK . #### G07 · High · wrong-ownership — A settled receipt survives the actor's later successful local writes, so a later deliberate repeat of the same claim or claiming close is answered from the old receipt and does nothing: 'Claimed (confirmed)' while nobody holds the issue

**Mechanism.** Receipts (pending/<attempt>.command) are removed only by coordinated's stale loop (bead 1910-1911, after a later coordinated command's build) and by settle_commands' done path (1822-1824). Link.merge confirms a lost-ack attempt by unlinking only its .json (1690-1692) and keeps the .command. writing() (1236-1244), through which close/reopen/delete (transition, 2266-2287), update without --claim (2255), comments, create and dep go, never touches receipts. So after a lost acknowledgement the actor's later local writes go through and the receipt stays; a later `claim X` or `update X --claim --status closed` with the same signature finds the receipt with its attempt in claims/, settle_commands returns done (1822-1825), coordinated returns "online" at 1899-1901 without building or pushing, and the command prints its success line. The same happens when the local write was made while the attempt was still pending (the next sync settles the receipt), which is the shipped 'work continues before anyone syncs' pattern; the reporter's diff (drop only settled receipts) misses that case.

**Reproduction.** tests.support Machine a: init --prefix ex; create X; sync. (1) `claim X` with BEAD_TEST_CRASH_AFTER_PUSH=1 (rc 99); `sync`; `close X`; `reopen X`; `update X --title 'X again'`; `claim X`; `show X`. (2) `update X --claim --status closed` with BEAD_TEST_CRASH_AFTER_PUSH=1; `sync`; `reopen X`; the same update again; `show X`. (3) lost-ack `claim X`; `comments add X 'work continues'` (attempt still pending); `sync`; machine b: `takeover X --reason ...`, `release X`; a: `sync`; `claim X`; `show X`. Tests: final/G07.py.

**Observed.** (1) AssertionError: unexpectedly None : 'Claimed ex-srjg: X again (confirmed)' but nobody holds ex-srjg. (2) AssertionError: 'open' != 'closed' ... : 'Updated ex-64dj: X' but ex-64dj is still open. (3) AssertionError: unexpectedly None : 'Claimed ex-8tr3: X (confirmed)' but nobody holds ex-8tr3.

**Expected.** REVIEWING section 2: a receipt 'is dropped only when a later command by that actor goes through'. The close, reopen, edit and comment went through, so the repeat is a new command: `claim X` claims X and the claiming close claims and closes X.

**Proposed fix.** writing(): after a local write that appended events or confirmed ownership, call the new forget_receipts(ws, actor), which unlinks every .command receipt of this actor, pending or settled (non-object or unreadable receipts are skipped). Pending attempts (.json) stay for sync to settle; only the receipts that would answer a repeat go. A failed local write keeps the receipts (code after the contextmanager's yield does not run when the body raises), so F01 holds; sync and publish_soon do not go through writing(), so test_receipt_identity_holds_for_actor_sync_stdin_and_json holds. Behaviour change (measured, combined copy): lost-ack `update X --claim --status closed`, then `comments add X`, then the same update again: e9eed92 answers rc 0 'Updated ex-pe71: X'; patched answers rc 1 'Error: ex-atpq is closed'. In both the log is ['create','claim','close','comment'] and X is closed, so no data changes; the patched answer is what section 2 prescribes for a new command.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G07_ReceiptSurvivesLaterLocalWrites` (3 tests). On e9eed92: FAILED (failures=3): AssertionError: unexpectedly None : 'Claimed ex-srjg: X again (confirmed)' but nobody holds ex-srjg / AssertionError: 'open' != 'closed' ... 'Updated ex-64dj: X' but ex-64dj is still open / AssertionError: unexpectedly None : 'Claimed ex-8tr3: X (confirmed)' but nobody holds ex-8tr3 With the diff: Ran 3 tests ... OK (G07 copy); also OK on the combined copy.

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Against the G07 copy: test_protocol 'Ran 54 tests in 315.332s OK'; test_commands 'Ran 97 tests in 426.533s OK' (load average about 60-100). No shipped test needs adapting.          if tx.ownership:
             confirm_ownership(ws, tx.ownership)

             (ws.dir / "pending" / f"{events[0]['id']}.command").unlink(missing_ok=True)
 
 
+def forget_receipts(ws: Workspace, actor: dict):
+    """A later command of this actor went through. Attempts still pending stay for sync to settle;
+    only the receipts that would answer a repeat of an earlier command go."""
+    folder = ws.dir / "pending"
+    for receipt in sorted(folder.glob("*.command")) if folder.is_dir() else []:
+        try:
+            recorded = loads_strict(receipt.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
+        except (OSError, ValueError):
+            continue
+        if isinstance(recorded, dict) and recorded.get("actor") == actor:
+            receipt.unlink(missing_ok=True)
+
+
 def command_signature(args, actor: dict, request=None) -> str:

     the same actor after a crash is the same request, not a second one. `request` carries what

### Medium

#### G11 · Medium · data-loss — Server hook text on git's stderr decides the push outcome: an applied push whose connection drops after a hook printed '[rejected]' is taken as refused, so an accepted release answers NOT_OWNER and an accepted claiming edit is rebuilt over a later edit

**Mechanism.** Link.publish pushes with `git push --quiet` (bead:1772) and, when git exits non-zero, classifies the outcome with a regex over all of push.stderr (bead:1780-1782: \[rejected\]|\[remote rejected\]|non-fast-forward|fetch first|stale info|cannot lock ref|failed to update ref). stderr includes the `remote:` lines git relays from server hooks. post-receive runs after the ref update and after receive-pack has sent its status report; if receive-pack then dies or the connection drops, git exits 1 although the ref moved (git's own per-ref status line, printed with --porcelain, is a successful fast-forward). A hook line containing one of the tokens makes publish return 'rejected'. coordinated (bead:1935-1936) then drop_pending()s the attempt and its .command receipt, sleeps, fetches again: merge takes the attempt from the remote as an ordinary confirmed file, settle_commands (bead:1805-1827) finds no receipt, and build runs again against the new state. release answers NOT_OWNER (cmd_release: the claim is already gone); `update --claim --title` emits its edit a second time after the retry sleep, on top of any edit made meanwhile by someone who had seen the first (the N01 lost update by another route). Control: the same hook printing text without a token leaves rc 0 (the push is 'uncertain' and the receipt recovers it).

**Reproduction.** tests.support Sandbox, machine a: init --prefix ex; create one; sync; claim KEY. origin.git/hooks/post-receive (chmod 755): `#!/bin/sh` / `cat >/dev/null` / `if mkdir GATE 2>/dev/null; then echo 'mirror: ! [rejected] refs/beads/journal -> refs/beads/journal (fetch first)' >&2; sleep 0.2; kill -9 $PPID; fi; exit 0`. Then `bead release KEY --json`. Lost update: same hook, machine b synced, in-process cmd_update(['update', KEY, '--claim', '--title', 'accepted first']) with time.sleep patched so that b syncs, sees 'accepted first', sets 'later observed edit' and syncs in the retry window. G11.py has both.

**Observed.** release: rc=1 {"error": {"code": "NOT_OWNER", "message": "ex-xlkb is not claimed by codex:g11-thread-0001@c6b40ee8"}} while the hook had fired, the remote head had moved and `show` had no claim. Lost update: 'AssertionError: 2 != 1 : the accepted edit was dropped as refused and emitted a second time'. Plain `git push --porcelain` in the same state: rc=1, stdout ' \t5a8b2404...:refs/beads/journal\t15a6466..5a8b240', stderr 'remote: ! [rejected] refs/beads/journal -> refs/beads/journal (fetch first) / send-pack: unexpected disconnect while reading sideband packet / error: error in sideband demultiplexer / error: git-receive-pack '...origin.git' died of signal 9 / error: failed to push some refs to ...'; remote head == pushed commit.

**Expected.** The outcome comes from git's own per-ref status, never from text a server prints: an applied push is 'ok' (release rc 0, the edit emitted once), whatever happens to the connection afterwards.

**Proposed fix.** publish pushes with --porcelain instead of --quiet and reads git's status line for `<commit>:<ref>` from stdout ('<flag>\t<from>:<to>\t<summary>', flags are never translated): ' ', '+', '*', '=' -> ok (also when git then exits non-zero), '!' with '[rejected]' or '[remote rejected]' -> rejected (as before), anything else including no status line -> uncertain. stderr is no longer consulted. Shipped tests: three lose_ack mocks in tests/test_protocol.py simulate a lost acknowledgement by setting returncode=1 on a real push result, which leaves git's real porcelain success line on stdout; publish now (correctly) answers 'ok' from it and the retry path those tests exist for is never reached. The diff adds `result.stdout = b''` to each: ProtocolReaudit.test_nonzero_applied_push_with_interleaved_writer_must_not_fork (line 655) and Protocol3.test_recovered_atomic_update_is_not_applied_again_after_a_later_observed_edit (line 892) fail without it ('Lists differ: [] != [True]'); Protocol3.test_release_recovers_a_successful_push_with_lost_acknowledgement (line 914) still passes without it but would no longer exercise recovery, so it gets the same line. The adapted tests also pass on e9eed92 (--quiet writes nothing to stdout). A smaller alternative needing no test change (the verifier's): drop `remote:` lines before the regex; such a push is then 'uncertain' and recovered through the receipt one round later. The bead hunk applies to a directory holding only bead; the tests hunk needs tests/test_protocol.py (checked: bead + tests/test_protocol.py from e9eed92, patch -p1 applies all, result identical).

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G11_ServerHookTextDoesNotDecideThePushOutcome` (2 tests). On e9eed92: FAIL test_applied_release_is_not_reported_not_owner_because_a_hook_printed_rejected: AssertionError: 1 != 0 : the remote applied the release, but bead reported: {"error": {"code": "NOT_OWNER", "message": "ex-ekiy is not claimed by codex:g11-thread-0001@7d1bd28f"}}; FAIL test_applied_claiming_edit_is_not_rebuilt_over_a_later_observed_edit: AssertionError: 2 != 1 : the accepted edit was dropped as refused and emitted a second time (Ran 2 tests, FAILED (failures=2)) With the diff: Ran 2 tests in 2.806s OK (also OK on the combined copy)

**Shipped suites with the diff.** G11 copy: tests.test_protocol unmodified: Ran 54 tests, FAILED (failures=2): ProtocolReaudit.test_nonzero_applied_push_with_interleaved_writer_must_not_fork and Protocol3.test_recovered_atomic_update_is_not_applied_again_after_a_later_observed_edit, both 'AssertionError: Lists differ: [] != [True]' (their lose_ack mocks keep git's real porcelain success line on stdout, so publish answers ok and the retry hook is never reached). With the diff's tests/test_protocol.py hunks (result.stdout = b'' in all three lose_ack mocks): Ran 54 tests in 209.769s OK. tests.test_storage: Ran 9 tests OK. All five diffs applied together (push-outcomes-combined.diff, bead + tests/test_protocol.py): three textual conflicts when applied one after another with patch(1), resolved by hand in the combined diff: G16 x G41 (both edit Link.fetch's failure branch; G41's hunk fails after G16), G11 x G18 (both rewrite Link.publish's classification; combined: --porcelain status line decides, '[remote rejected]' with a non-race reason is 'refused', last_error = status summary + git's stderr), G41 x G18 (both add a line to PRIME's error codes; combined lists REMOTE_REFUSED and REMOTE_DAMAGED). Combined copy (bead from push-outcomes-combined.diff; tests from the same diff, i.e. tests/test_protocol.py with the G11 and G18 hunks): tests.test_protocol Ran 54 tests in 203.591s OK; tests.test_commands Ran 97 tests in …

#### G52 · Medium · data-loss — A store directory bead cannot read, or a store path that is not a directory, is taken for an empty journal: readers say NOT_INITIALIZED ('run bead init'), info exits 0 with no issues, an unreadable claims/ makes a claimed issue ready, and a create into an unlistable own journal is acknowledged, then rejected as a broken chain that wedges the machine's sync

**Mechanism.** segment_files/claim_files bead:410-419: `if not ws.events_dir.is_dir(): return []` (Path.is_dir returns False on EACCES and ENAMETOOLONG from Python 3.14, and on ENOTDIR always) and `d.glob("*.jsonl")` / `d.glob("claims/*.json")`, which return nothing for a directory that cannot be listed (every Python version). load_journal then replays an empty or partial journal: need_tracker (1247-1249) raises NOT_INITIALIZED, cmd_info (2514) answers rc 0 with 0 issues, an unreadable claims/ hides confirmed claims (wrong readiness), and Tx appends to an own journal it could not list with deps that miss its earlier events, so replay quarantines the event as 'journal chain broken ... (copied machine identity?)' and Link.merge raises JOURNAL_FORK on every later sync. Writers recover by accident because locked() runs private_dir (1221-1222) on home and <ws>. REVIEWING §2: 'OS errors are structured (IO)'. All of this needs a store directory whose mode or type was broken from outside, hence Medium.

**Reproduction.** Sandbox; a: `init --prefix ex`, `create x`; chmod 0 events/<own> -> `show KEY --json`; chmod 0 <ws> -> `show`, `info`; `update KEY --claim`, chmod 0 events/<own>/claims -> `ready --json`; BEAD_HOME=<a regular file> -> `show`; second machine b: `sync`, `create first`, chmod 0o300 events/<own> -> `create second`, chmod 0700 -> `show <id>`, `info`, `sync`. Scripts: work-store-files/probe.py g52, wedge.py; test: G52.py.

**Observed.** `own journal chmod 0: show: rc 1 {"error": {"code": "NOT_INITIALIZED", "message": "no tracker for this repository yet: run `bead init` or `bead sync`"}}` / `store chmod 0: show: rc 1 ... NOT_INITIALIZED` / `store chmod 0: info rc 0 {'prefix': None, 'issues': 0, 'events': 0}` / `claims chmod 0: ready: rc 0 [{"id": "ex-5ljf", "title": "x", "status": "open", ...}]` / `BEAD_HOME a regular file: show: rc 1 ... NOT_INITIALIZED` while `create` gives `IO "File exists: .../home-file"` / `own journal 0o300: create: rc 0 {"id": "ex-ssu9", "title": "second", ...}` / `after restoring 0700: show: rc 1 {"error": {"code": "ISSUE_NOT_FOUND", "message": "Issue not found: ex-ssu9"}}` / `info integrity: [{... 'problem': 'journal chain broken at position 1 of machine 8e610eb9 (copied machine identity?)'}]` / wedge.py: `sync rc 1 {"error": {"code": "JOURNAL_FORK", "message": "this machine's journal no longer forms one chain: ..."}}` on every later sync, and the other machine never receives the earlier `first`.

**Expected.** An IO error naming the directory that cannot be read (or is not a directory), as for every other OSError; never an empty or partial journal, never an acknowledged write that does not exist.

**Proposed fix.** New dir_entries(folder): `sorted(folder.iterdir())`, with only FileNotFoundError meaning empty; segment_files and claim_files list each level with it and filter by suffix (`.jsonl`, `.json`), giving the same files in the same order. Patched: every case above is `IO` naming the directory (`Permission denied: .../events/9c6bb36d`, `Not a directory: .../home-file/<wid>/events`, `Permission denied: .../claims`); the 0o300 create is refused with IO and nothing is written, and the machine syncs normally afterwards (wedge.py: `sync rc 0`, the other machine sees `first` and `third`). Not covered (same class, follow-up): pending_files (422-425), active_segment/append_events (460-476) and the receipt scan (1811) still pair glob() with is_dir().

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G52_UnreadableStoreIsIO` (5 tests). On e9eed92: FAIL test_g52_unlistable_own_journal_is_io_not_no_tracker: `AssertionError: 'NOT_INITIALIZED' != 'IO'`; FAIL test_g52_unlistable_claims_do_not_turn_a_claimed_issue_ready: `AssertionError: None != 'IO' : ready answered from a journal it could not read: [{"id": "ex-klni", "title": "x", "status": "open", ...}]`; FAIL test_g52_create_into_an_unlistable_journal_is_refused_not_lost: `AssertionError: 1 != 0 : create returned ex-2c24 but it is gone: {"error": {"code": "ISSUE_NOT_FOUND", "message": "Issue not found: ex-2c24"}}`; FAIL test_g52_store_that_is_not_a_directory_is_io [show] `'NOT_INITIALIZE… With the diff: Ran 5 tests in 3.040s OK (G52 alone); OK on the combined copy. Checker: pristine 7 failures (5 tests, none skipped on 3.14) and patched OK reproduced; probe confirmed the pristine 0o300 create is acknowledged, then 'journal chain broken ... (copied machine identity?)', JOURNAL_FORK on every later s…

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Against this diff alone: G52 test_storage rc=0 25s: Ran 9 tests in 24.115s OK ; G52 test_replay rc=0 179s: Ran 60 tests in 179.450s OK . Combined copy (all six store-files diffs, applied in order G04 G35 G47 G48 G50 G52 with `git apply`): ALL test_storage rc=0 24s: Ran 9 tests in 22.895s OK ; ALL test_protocol rc=0 212s: Ran 54 tests in 211.977s OK ; ALL test_commands rc=0 304s: Ran 97 tests in 304.071s OK ; ALL test_replay rc=0 114s: Ran 60 tests in 113.549s OK . No shipped test needed adapting. Not run: test_compat (its real-exports test looks for infra/compat_real.py beside BEAD_UNDER_TEST and reads the owner's real tracker exports) and test_stress (slow models). Independent check (check-store-files, Python 3.14.7, git 2.55.0): all six diffs apply with `patch -p1` to fresh pristine copies, singly and combined in either order (G04..G52 and G52..G04 give identical files). Combined copy (with the revised G47.diff): test_storage Ran 9 OK; test_commands Ran 97 in 254s OK; test_replay Ran 60 in 161s OK; test_protocol Ran 54 OK when run alone (120s). In a parallel run at load average ~185, test_protocol had 1 failure, test_timed_out_accepted_release_returns_success (BEAD_NET_TIMEOUT=0.1 timing test, also seen flaking by the triage-33 verifier); that test passed 3/3 alone on the combined copy and 2/2 on pristine, so it is load, not these diffs.

#### G13 · Medium · divergence — .beadroll.json is decoded with the locale encoding, so one committed config routes to a different coordination ref under a non-UTF-8 locale (double claim; divergence)

**Mechanism.** find_workspace reads the tracked config with loads_strict((root/'.beadroll.json').read_text()) (bead:201) and passes no encoding, so locale.getpreferredencoding() applies. Every other state read passes encoding='utf-8' (bead:145, 261, 272, 276, 1813, 1822, 2538). Under a non-UTF-8 locale (ISO-8859-1, US-ASCII) a UTF-8-committed non-ASCII `ref`/`remote`/`workspace` decodes to a different string, so ws.ref (bead:156) becomes a second ref. Python 3.14 is not in UTF-8 mode by default; C/POSIX coerce to UTF-8 but real locales like en_US.ISO8859-1 do not.

**Reproduction.** Two clones with the same committed bytes {"workspace":"ws-g13-0001","ref":"refs/beads/équipe"} (UTF-8). init/create/sync under UTF-8. Then on the other clone: `bead info --json` and `bead claim <id> --json` under LC_ALL=en_US.ISO8859-1; and `bead info --json` under LC_ALL=en_US.US-ASCII.

**Observed.** Latin-1 session reports ref = 'refs/beads/Ã©quipe' and the origin ends up with both refs/beads/Ã©quipe and refs/beads/équipe; a claim under Latin-1 and a claim under UTF-8 both succeed on the same issue. US-ASCII: {"error": {"code": "CONFIG", "message": ".../.beadroll.json is not valid JSON"}} for a valid UTF-8 config.

**Expected.** .beadroll.json is UTF-8 regardless of locale (matching the §2 rule 'text from files and stdin is UTF-8 regardless of locale'); both sessions resolve the same ref and a valid UTF-8 config is accepted everywhere.

**Proposed fix.** Pass encoding='utf-8' to the read_text() at bead:201, consistent with every other state-file read. Both locales then resolve the same committed ref, the online claim is exclusive, and a US-ASCII session reads a valid UTF-8 config. (A non-UTF-8 config still reports 'is not valid JSON'; a clearer 'not valid UTF-8 JSON' would be an optional follow-up.)

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G13_TrackedConfigIsUtf8WhateverTheLocale` (3 tests). On e9eed92: AssertionError: 'refs/beads/Ã©quipe' != 'refs/beads/équipe' (and: two agents both hold a confirmed claim on ex-...; the remote has refs ['refs/beads/Ã©quipe', 'refs/beads/équipe']; and: a valid UTF-8 config was refused under US-ASCII) With the diff: OK. The tests self-skip if python3 on bead's PATH does not actually decode text as ISO-8859-1 / US-ASCII under those locales (it does here: ISO8859-1 and US-ASCII with utf8_mode 0).

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Checker, combined copy (G01 + corrected G02 + G13 + G51, all applied cleanly with patch -p1, no conflicts), run from a copy of tests/ with scratch BEAD_HOME/TMPDIR: run sequentially, tests.test_protocol: Ran 54 tests OK; tests.test_commands: Ran 97 tests OK. Also tests.test_storage: Ran 9 tests OK; tests.test_replay: Ran 60 tests OK. In an earlier run of six suites in parallel, test_protocol test_timed_out_accepted_release_returns_success failed on BOTH the pristine and the patched copy (timing under load) and test_commands HelpAndPrime hit a `git clone` exit 128 in setUp; both pass when rerun alone on both copies. tests.test_compat: 2 OK, 1 not runnable here (test_all_available_real_exports_and_br_bv_consumers looks for infra/compat_real.py next to the bead copy), unrelated to these diffs. No shipped test needs adapting.

```diff
--- a/bead
+++ b/bead
@@ -198,7 +198,7 @@
     config = {}
     if root and (root / ".beadroll.json").exists():
         try:
-            config = loads_strict((root / ".beadroll.json").read_text())
+            config = loads_strict((root / ".beadroll.json").read_text(encoding="utf-8"))  # UTF-8 whatever the locale
         except (OSError, ValueError):
             raise BpError("CONFIG", f"{root / '.beadroll.json'} is not valid JSON") from None
         config = checked_config(config, root / ".beadroll.json")
```
</details>

#### G22 · Medium · divergence — The coordination remote and the URL-derived workspace id come from the LAST remote.origin.url and ignore remote.origin.pushurl, so bead coordinates where `git push origin` does not go

**Mechanism.** find_workspace (bead:205-206) runs `git config --get remote.origin.url`, which returns the last value of a multi-valued key; git fetches from the first url and pushes to the pushurl (or every url). The id is hashed from that value (bead:211-212) and Workspace.remote falls back to it (bead:165); Link.fetch/publish (bead:1624, bead:1772) use it. So with `git remote set-url --add origin <backup>` the machine moves to another workspace on a remote git never fetches from (NOT_INITIALIZED, advice 'run bead init' starts a second tracker); with url=mirror, pushurl=primary the tracker is pushed to the mirror (lost at the mirror's next prune, or refused by a read-only mirror), and such a machine derives another id than direct clones of the primary (WORKSPACE_MISMATCH).

**Reproduction.** (a) a: `bead init; bead create one; bead sync`; `git clone --bare origin.git backup.git`; fresh machine c: `git remote set-url --add origin backup.git`; `bead info --json`; `bead sync --json`. (b) `git clone --mirror origin.git mirror.git`; machine m: `git remote set-url origin mirror.git; git remote set-url --push origin origin.git`; `bead sync --json`.

**Observed.** c: git remote get-url origin -> origin.git | c: bead info remote -> backup.git | c: bead sync exit 1 {"error": {"code": "NOT_INITIALIZED", "message": "no tracker here and none published on the remote: run `bead init`"}} | m: git remote get-url --push origin -> origin.git | m: bead sync exit 1 {"error": {"code": "WORKSPACE_MISMATCH", "message": "refs/beads/journal on mirror.git belongs to workspace 49f6103b0b20ee79, not 7b54ea0780afc42e"}}

**Expected.** origin means where `git push origin` goes: the first pushurl if set, else the first url (also the one git fetches from). The same URL gives the id and the endpoint (a conditional update needs one endpoint), so mirror-fetching machines and direct clones share one tracker.

**Proposed fix.** find_workspace reads `git config --get-regexp ^remote\.origin\.(push)?url$` (one call, replacing the old one) and takes the first pushurl, else the first url, as configured. The existing relative-path resolution still applies. Deliberately NOT `git remote get-url --push` as the verifier proposed: that applies insteadOf/pushInsteadOf, which are per-machine settings, so the workspace id would differ between machines with different rewrites and would change on upgrade for every user whose insteadOf rewrites origin to a differently-normalising URL; the raw value keeps today's id for every single-url, no-pushurl checkout, and git still applies global insteadOf/pushInsteadOf when sync.git fetches and pushes. Upgrade cost: a store created under hash(last url) by a multi-url or pushurl checkout is no longer found (NOT_INITIALIZED); G24's adoption recovers it at the next sync when the new origin holds the tracker. A pushInsteadOf in the checkout's own .git/config is still not seen by sync.git (lead's observation 4).

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G22_OriginIsNotWhereGitPushes` (3 tests). On e9eed92: AssertionError: False is not true : the tracker was not published where `git push origin` goes (the pushurl); AssertionError: 1 != 0 : {"error": {"code": "WORKSPACE_MISMATCH", "message": "refs/beads/journal on .../mirror.git belongs to workspace 94f95b0928b3505a, not 4fe40022fb0f2cd7"}}; AssertionError: '.../backup.git' != '.../origin.git'. FAILED (failures=3). With the diff: Ran 3 tests, OK (also OK against the combined group copy). Manual guard: a relative origin (`../origin.git`, also as pushurl) still syncs from a subdirectory.
