#### G23 · Medium · divergence — A tracked .beadroll.json is read from the working tree only, so a worktree on an older branch, a bisect or a bare clone is a different workspace, and following bead's advice there creates a second store (or, with a separate coordination remote, a second tracker on the code origin)

**Mechanism.** find_workspace reads root/.beadroll.json only (bead:199-204). A checkout whose tree predates the commit that added it (a release-branch worktree, a bisect, a bare clone where --show-toplevel fails) falls back to sha256(normalize_remote(origin)) (bead:210-212) and to origin as the remote. Read commands give NOT_INITIALIZED 'run `bead init` or `bead sync`' (need_tracker, bead:1247-1249); claim/sync give WORKSPACE_MISMATCH (bead:1640-1641); `bead init` succeeds and creates a second store. With `init --tracked --remote <coordination>` the old-branch checkout also loses the remote: sync says NOT_INITIALIZED, and init + sync then publish a second tracker into the code origin.

**Reproduction.** a: `git branch release-1` (and push it); `bead init --tracked`; commit and push .beadroll.json; `bead create 'fix the release build'`; `bead sync`; `git worktree add ../release-1 release-1`; in that worktree: `bead info|show X|claim X|init|create stranded|sync --json`.

**Observed.** main checkout workspace: 01a1180c-9b0a-7883-bb73-aac33e3f5cd5 | release-1 worktree workspace: 1f65b4c695c55665 source: origin url | show exit 1 NOT_INITIALIZED "no tracker for this repository yet: run `bead init` or `bead sync`" | claim exit 1 WORKSPACE_MISMATCH "refs/beads/journal on origin.git belongs to workspace 01a1180c-... not 1f65b4c695c..." | init exit 0 {"workspace": "1f65b4c695c55665" ...} | create exit 0 | sync exit 1 WORKSPACE_MISMATCH | stores: ['01a1180c-9b0a-7883-bb73-aac33e3f5cd5', '1f65b4c695c55665']

**Expected.** Tracked means committed: when the working tree has no .beadroll.json, use the committed one (HEAD's, then the default branch's, refs/remotes/origin/HEAD), so every checkout of the repository resolves to its tracker and coordination remote, as `init --tracked` promises ('commit it so every checkout and machine uses this workspace').

**Proposed fix.** New committed_config(): one `git cat-file --batch` asking for HEAD:.beadroll.json and refs/remotes/origin/HEAD:.beadroll.json; the first blob found is parsed with loads_strict and checked_config (CONFIG error naming '<ref>:.beadroll.json' if invalid). find_workspace uses it only when the working tree has no .beadroll.json (the working-tree file still wins, so uncommitted configs and the shipped tests behave as before). HEAD first so a sparse checkout or bare clone uses its own commit; origin/HEAD for older branches. Cost: one extra git process per command in repositories without the file (the default setup): `bead list --json` median 103.7 -> 114.5 ms over 25 runs at load ~77 (the cat-file call alone 7.6 ms); the verifier measured +12 ms for the reporter's two-call variant. If that matters, run the lookup only when the URL-derived store has no journal (cheaper, but then a store already created by following the old advice keeps winning). Limits: a clone without refs/remotes/origin/HEAD (git init + remote add + fetch) or one that has not fetched the commit still falls back.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G23_TrackedConfigReadFromWorkingTreeOnly` (2 tests). On e9eed92: AssertionError: '048b7071bb1a771a' != '01a11806-31ec-725d-b0af-3af3bb5f92cd' : a worktree of the same repository resolves to another workspace; AssertionError: 1 != 0 : {"error": {"code": "NOT_INITIALIZED", "message": "no tracker here and none published on the remote: run `bead init`"}}. FAILED (failures=2). With the diff: Ran 2 tests, OK (also OK against the combined group copy).

```diff
+    return {}
```

#### G25 · Medium · divergence — The remote the first sync used, origin included, is saved as 'last used' and outranks origin, so after `git remote set-url origin <new>` bead keeps coordinating through the old URL (failing, or splitting the crew when the old one still works)

**Mechanism.** synchronise (bead:1868) and coordinated (bead:1896, 1900, 1933) call save_meta(remote=remote) for every remote, including one that merely came from origin. Workspace.remote (bead:164-165) ranks meta['remote'] above self.origin, and save_meta (bead:150-152) drops falsy values, so nothing ever clears it. After the first contact origin is never consulted again for that store: a moved repository gives REMOTE_UNREACHABLE naming the old URL; an old remote left in place keeps this machine on it while fresh clones use the new one, and both exclusive claims of one issue are confirmed.

**Reproduction.** a: `bead init --tracked; bead create one; bead sync`; `mv origin.git moved.git`; `git remote set-url origin moved.git`; `git fetch origin` (works); `bead info`; `bead sync --json`; `bead claim X --json`. Split variant: commit .beadroll.json, `git clone --mirror origin.git new-home.git`, a re-points origin to new-home.git, fresh clone b of new-home claims X, then a claims X.

**Observed.** meta after first sync: { "name": "origin", "remote": "<sandbox>/origin.git", ... } | bead info remote: origin.git | bead sync exit 1 {"error": {"code": "REMOTE_UNREACHABLE", "message": "cannot reach the coordination remote <sandbox>/origin.git: fatal: '<sandbox>/origin.git' does not appear to be a git repository ..."}} | bead claim exit 1 (same). Split variant: a's claim exit 0, "assignee": "codex:a-thread-0001@247c16fd" after b's claim was confirmed.

**Expected.** origin is looked up each time, so a set-url is followed; only a remote that is not origin (named with --remote, init --remote) is remembered. A remembered value that is origin in another spelling (saved by older versions) is ignored.

**Proposed fix.** save_meta drops 'remote' when it normalises (normalize_remote) to origin, so origin is never remembered and `bead sync --remote origin` clears a stale entry; remote() reads remembered_remote(), which ignores a remembered value equal to origin (legacy entries such as https saved before a switch to ssh). Smaller than the verifier's 'named-only' diff (no `named` threaded through synchronise/coordinated/publish_soon/cmd_sync) and without the reporter's migration hazard (unmarked legacy `init --remote` entries are still honoured). Not covered: a legacy entry for a repository that moved to a URL that normalises differently stays until `bead sync --remote origin` once (which now clears it); BEAD_REMOTE and a tracked config remote that differ from origin are still remembered as before (a separate, related issue: the README says --remote and $BEAD_REMOTE apply per call).

On e9eed92: AssertionError: 1 != 0 : {"error": {"code": "REMOTE_UNREACHABLE", "message": "cannot reach the coordination remote .../origin.git: fatal: '.../origin.git' does not appear to be a git repository ..."}}; AssertionError: 0 == 0 : two exclusive claims of one issue were both confirmed: [{"id": "fz-u9pe", ...; AssertionError: 'https://example.invalid/org/repo.git' != 'git@example.invalid:org/repo.git' : the remembered spelling of origin outranks origin itself. FAILED (failures=3). With the diff: Ran 3 tests, OK (also OK against the combined group copy).

#### G10 · Medium · wrong-ownership — A recognised retry reports the original success without looking at the claim now: 'Claimed X (confirmed)' or 'Updated X' with exit 0 while another agent holds X, and a takeover retry crashes with KeyError: 'claim' when the claim has since ended

**Mechanism.** When settle_commands recognises a retry (done), coordinated returns "online" at 1899-1901 without looking at current ownership. cmd_claim (2127-2131) builds 'Claimed {id}: ... (confirmed)' from that mode; cmd_update with --claim (2252-2264) prints 'Updated {id}'; cmd_takeover (2134-2138) indexes r['claim']['generation'], and detail() adds 'claim' only when the issue has one (1520-1521), so a takeover retry after the claim ended raises KeyError, which run() (2867-2870) does not catch: a traceback with exit 1.

**Reproduction.** tests.support machines a, b (both synced). (1) a: `claim X` with BEAD_TEST_CRASH_AFTER_PUSH=1 (rc 99); b: `takeover X --reason 'a looked dead'`; a: `claim X`. (2) a: `update X --claim --add-label mine` lost ack; b: takeover X; a: the same update again. (3) b: claim X; a: sync; a: `takeover X --reason idle` lost ack; b: sync, `close X --force`; a: `takeover X --reason idle`. Tests: final/G10.py.

**Observed.** (1) rc 0 stdout 'Claimed ex-uwov: X (confirmed)' while show gives owner b; test: AssertionError: (0, 'Claimed') == (0, 'Claimed') : told the agent it holds ex-nm1m, which codex:g10-moved-b-thread-0001@b925bc81 holds: Claimed ex-nm1m: X (confirmed). (2) rc 0 'Updated ex-3pay: X' (AssertionError: 0 == 0 : claiming update exited 0 while codex:g10-update-b-thread-0001@d5e2edd7 holds ex-udy7). (3) rc 1, traceback ending 'line 2138, in cmd_takeover ... KeyError: 'claim''.

**Expected.** Recognising the retry is section 2; the answer must still describe the claim as it is now. When the actor no longer holds it: ALREADY_CLAIMED (held by another) or NOT_OWNER (ended), never 'Claimed (confirmed)' with exit 0 and never a traceback.

**Proposed fix.** New still_held(st, keys, actor), called after the coordinated call in cmd_claim (unless offline), cmd_takeover, and cmd_update with a claim (unless offline, or the update closes the issue: a claiming close ends the claim on purpose, which keeps the F01/Q02 claiming-close retries at rc 0). It raises ALREADY_CLAIMED '<id>: your claim was accepted but is now held by <owner>' or NOT_OWNER '<id>: your claim was accepted but has since ended (<status>)'. For a fresh online claim it is a no-op (the claim was just confirmed under the lock). Patched answers (combined copy): (1) rc 1 'Error: ex-34x3: your claim was accepted but is now held by codex:moved-b-thread-0001@5d4b4404'; (2) rc 1 'Error: ex-n105: your claim was accepted but is now held by codex:upd-b-thread-0001@1ce71f1e'; (3) rc 1 'Error: ex-ldys: your claim was accepted but has since ended (closed)'. The JSON payload of a recognised --json claim already carried the true owner; the exit status and text now agree with it.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G10_RecognisedRetryReportsAClaimThatMovedOn` (3 tests). On e9eed92: FAILED (failures=3): AssertionError: (0, 'Claimed') == (0, 'Claimed') : told the agent it holds ex-nm1m, which codex:g10-moved-b-thread-0001@b925bc81 holds: Claimed ex-nm1m: X (confirmed) / AssertionError: 0 == 0 : claiming update exited 0 while codex:g10-update-b-thread-0001@d5e2edd7 holds ex-udy7: Updated ex-udy7: X / AssertionError: 'Traceback' unexpectedly found in ... KeyError: 'claim' With the diff: Ran 3 tests ... OK (G10 copy); also OK on the combined copy.

<details><summary>Proposed diff (G10, applies to e9eed92 with <code>patch -p1</code>)</summary>

```diff
--- a/bead
+++ b/bead
@@ -2122,11 +2122,25 @@
 
 CLAIM_NOTE = {"online": "confirmed", "local": "local tracker, no remote",
               "offline": "PROVISIONAL: not exclusive until claimed online"}
+
+
+def still_held(st: State, keys, actor: dict):
+    """A retry recognised from its receipt answers for a claim accepted earlier, which may have moved
+    on since: say so rather than report a claim this actor no longer holds."""
+    me = actor_label(actor, replica_id())
+    for key in keys:
+        issue = st.resolve(key)
+        held = issue["claim"]
+        if not held or held["owner"] != me:
+            raise BpError("ALREADY_CLAIMED" if held else "NOT_OWNER", f"{issue['id']}: your claim was accepted but "
+                          + (f"is now held by {held['owner']}" if held else f"has since ended ({issue['status']})"))
 
 
 def cmd_claim(args, ws, actor):
     mode = claim_issues(args, ws, actor, args.ids)
     st = view(ws)
+    if mode != "offline":
+        still_held(st, args.ids, actor)
     result = [detail(st, st.resolve(key)) for key in args.ids]
     return result, [f"Claimed {r['id']}: {r['title']} ({CLAIM_NOTE[mode]})" for r in result]
 
@@ -2134,6 +2148,7 @@
 def cmd_takeover(args, ws, actor):
     claim_issues(args, ws, actor, args.ids, takeover=True)
     st = view(ws)
+    still_held(st, args.ids, actor)
```

#### G12 · Medium · wrong-ownership — The owner's close/delete (or update --status closed) that died before its publish is retried, answers success, and never publishes the end of the claim

**Mechanism.** transition (bead:2266-2287) publishes at once only when `ended`, which is `bool(held)` (2281): the issue had a claim when this run started. The first run appends the close (ending the owner's claim, end_claim 881-885) under the lock and then calls publish_soon (2285); if it dies before the push, the close is only local. The retry sees the issue closed with no claim, so held is None, ended stays False, publish_soon is skipped, and it answers success. cmd_update has the same test (2213: `status == "closed" and held`). Other machines keep seeing the issue in_progress and claimed by the owner until some later, unrelated sync from that machine. Found by the lead's sweep: exactly the 10 G12_SCHEDULES of 660 (close/delete x crash-before-push x RD/RO/RS/OR/SR) on every host.

**Reproduction.** Machines a, b: a init, create X, sync; b sync; a claim X; b sync. Run a's `close X --reason done --json` under a driver that exits 98 right before the first git push (the sweep WRAPPER's BEAD_SWEEP_FAULT=push-before; G12.py carries an equivalent self-contained one, _g12_push_before_script, so it does not depend on test_review7.WRAPPER). Retry `close X --reason done` on a; `sync` on b; `show X` on b. Same for `delete X --reason gone` and `update X --status closed`.

**Observed.** Pristine: 'close (killed before its push): 98', 'retry close: 0 '✓ Closed gc-ap67: work' ''' (no warning), 'b after sync: in_progress codex:a-thread-0001@642f2e88'. Test: 'Tuples differ: ('in_progress', 'codex:a-thread-0001@9c48a0a5') != ('closed', None)' (delete: != ('tombstone', None); update --status closed: != ('closed', None)).

**Expected.** A retry of the owner's close/delete/update --status closed publishes as promptly as the first run would have (or says 'recorded locally, not yet published'), so b sees the issue closed and unclaimed.

**Proposed fix.** New ended_by(issue, me): the issue's last claim_history entry was ended by `me` with how close or delete. transition sets ended also when op is close/delete and ended_by(issue, me); cmd_update appends to `ended` when status is closed and (held or ended_by(issue, me)). Cost: closing again an issue whose claim you ended by closing it runs one more publish (a sync) even if the first one landed; harmless and idempotent. Replaces the lead's hunk 'a retry publishes too', which covered close/delete only (update --status closed has the same bug).

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G12_RetriedEndOfClaimIsPublished` (3 tests). On e9eed92: Ran 3 tests, FAILED (failures=3): 'AssertionError: Tuples differ: ('in_progress', 'codex:a-thread-0001@9c48a0a5') != ('closed', None) : the retried close answered success, yet the other machine still sees the issue in_progress and claimed by codex:a-thread-0001@9c48a0a5: the end of the claim was never published' (and the same for delete -> ('tombstone', None), update --status closed -> ('closed', None)). With the diff: G12.diff alone: Ran 3 tests in 16.777s OK; all five pending-io diffs: Ran 3 tests in 19.380s OK.

**Shipped suites with the diff.** All five pending-io diffs (G12, G28, G29, G37, G53) applied together to one copy (work-pending-io/all/bead; they apply cleanly in either order with an identical result, no conflicts among them), run from the repo root with BEAD_UNDER_TEST=<that copy> and scratch BEAD_HOME/TMPDIR: tests.test_protocol 'Ran 54 tests in 211.758s OK'; tests.test_commands 'Ran 97 tests in 280.893s OK' (includes test_f12_unreadable_pending_file_is_not_fenced_forever, test_f13_local_state_files_are_strict_json, test_f16_read_only_store_write_is_a_structured_error); tests.test_storage 'Ran 9 tests in 26.985s OK' (its pause-view driver replaces load_pending with a one-argument function, which is why G28 keeps that signature); tests.test_replay 'Ran 60 tests in 214.925s OK'. tests.test_stress (randomised 6-machine models, 2400 sequential and 3000 concurrent commands with readers racing writers) 'Ran 2 tests in 1046.906s OK'. The lead's LeadClose tests (test_lead.py, 3 tests) pass on the combined copy and all fail on pristine. Sweep (tests/test_review7.py run_schedule): the 24 close/delete x crash-before-push schedules, which include the 10 G12_SCHEDULES, 0 failures on the combined copy; the 10 G12_SCHEDULES on pristine: 10 failures ('UNPUBLISHED R close ... -> rc 0 (json carries no warning): remote status in_progress, local status closed'). After G12 lands, Sweep.test_sweep's comment and G12_SCHEDULES ('…

```diff
+def ended_by(issue: dict, me: str) -> bool:
+    """`me` ended the issue's last claim by closing or deleting it. The run that did so may have died
+    before it published that, so a retry of the command publishes as promptly as the first run."""
```

#### G21 · Medium · wrong-ownership — The coordination remote is resolved against the process cwd: a stored relative remote path works only from the checkout top, and a directory in the cwd named like a configured remote replaces that remote, so two machines can both be granted one claim

**Mechanism.** Workspace.remote (bead:162-173): bead:168 looks up `git remote get-url NAME` only when no file or directory of that name exists in the cwd, and bead:173 resolves an existing relative path with os.path.realpath against the cwd; a relative value that does not exist from the cwd is passed through, and Link runs git with GIT_DIR=sync.git in the caller's cwd (bead:1603-1606), so git also resolves it from the cwd. cmd_init stores --remote verbatim (bead:1960, .beadroll.json at 1968, meta at 1976). git itself resolves a relative remote URL (configured or typed) from the top of the worktree and prefers a configured remote over a same-named path (checked: in src/, `git ls-remote tr` with tr=../tracker.git and `git ls-remote ../tracker.git` both list the tracker; at the top `git ls-remote upstream` lists team.git although ./upstream is a repository; with GIT_DIR set elsewhere `git fetch ../tracker.git` from src/ fails). Consequences: `.beadroll.json {"remote": "../tracker.git"}` fails from every subdirectory; a remote given relative to a subdirectory at init fails from the top; a configured remote with a relative URL fails from subdirectories; and with `{"remote": "upstream"}` plus a nested repository ./upstream (vendored code), commands at the top publish the journal and settle claims inside that nested repository while commands in subdirectories use the real remote, so a second machine is granted a claim the first already holds.

**Reproduction.** (1) `bead init --prefix ex --tracked --remote ../tracker.git` at the top; `bead sync` at the top and in src/. (2) `git remote add upstream <box>/team.git`; .beadroll.json {"workspace": "named-ws", "remote": "upstream"}; `git init upstream` inside the checkout; `bead init`; `bead info`/`bead sync` at the top and in src/; `git for-each-ref refs/beads` in both repositories. (3) two machines with that config, A has the nested ./upstream: A creates and syncs from src/, B syncs, A claims at the top, B claims. Test: final/G21.py.

**Observed.** e9eed92: (1) `sync top: rc=0 ... "remote": "<box>/tracker.git"`; `sync src/: rc=1 {"error": {"code": "REMOTE_UNREACHABLE", "message": "cannot reach the coordination remote ../tracker.git: fatal: '../tracker.git' does not appear to be a git repository...`. (2) `info top: <box>/g21n-main/upstream`, `info src/: <box>/team.git`; sync at the top rc=0 `"remote": "<box>/g21n-main/upstream"`; `refs/beads in nested upstream/: 83fa73ad... refs/beads/journal`, `refs/beads in team.git:` (empty). (3) B's claim: rc 0, `"status": "in_progress", ... "assignee": "codex:b-thread-0001@..."` while A also holds it.

**Expected.** As git does: a configured remote name wins over a same-named path, and a stored relative path (tracked config, meta, origin, or a configured remote's relative URL) is read from the top of the checkout, so the endpoint is the same from any cwd and bead writes nothing into a repository inside the checkout.

**Proposed fix.** Workspace.remote always tries `git remote get-url` for a name-shaped value (a configured remote wins); a stored value, or the URL a configured remote resolves to, that is a relative local path (no ':' before the first '/', git's rule) is joined to `git rev-parse --show-toplevel`. A path typed with --remote or BEAD_REMOTE stays relative to the cwd, as before. cmd_init, which stores --remote, converts a typed relative path that exists (and is not a configured remote name) into a path relative to the top for --tracked and an absolute real path for meta.json, so a value typed in a subdirectory keeps meaning the same place; a configured remote name is stored as the name; outside a repository it uses check=False and stores the absolute path. Verified against the verifier's guards: `init --tracked --remote upstream` in src/ with src/upstream present keeps "upstream"; `BEAD_WORKSPACE=ws init --remote x.git` outside a repository still returns 0. Same as git and not changed: a committed relative path means a different place in a linked worktree at another depth. Independent of the other urls diffs (no conflicts); G05 carries the matching change for the origin itself (os.path.isabs instead of the cwd-relative os.path.exists at bead:207).

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G21_RemoteResolvedAgainstTheCwd` (5 tests). On e9eed92: FAIL (failures=6): `AssertionError: 0 == 0 : B was granted a claim A already holds: [{"id": "ex-9qah", "title": "shared work", "status": "in_progress", ... "assignee": "codex:b-thread-0001@e794deed"...`; `AssertionError: '<box>/shadow-main/upstream' != '<box>/team.git'`; `AssertionError: 1 != 0 : {"error": {"code": "REMOTE_UNREACHABLE", "message": "cannot reach the coordination remote ../tracker.git: fatal: '../tracker.git' does not appear to be a git repository...` (named relative URL from src/, and `the same tracked remote fails from src/`); `AssertionError: '../../tracker-True.git' != '<bo… With the diff: work-urls/G21/bead: Ran 5 tests in 7.461s OK; combined copy: OK. Checker (check-urls) added one assertion to the two-machines test, that B's refused claim reports ALREADY_CLAIMED (support.code), so it passes for the right reason: pristine still FAIL (failures=6), work G21 copy 'Ran 5 tests in 7.245…

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Per-diff selection run from the repo (BpTest.test_worktrees_and_clones_share_one_tracker_and_checkouts_stay_clean, BpTest.test_claim_needs_the_remote_unless_offline_is_asked_for, BpTest.test_tracked_workspace_id_and_a_separate_coordination_remote, CompatAuditCLI.test_workspace_config_cannot_escape_home, CompatAuditCLI.test_changed_workspace_config_does_not_delete_old_store, Review5CLI.test_e02_remote_credentials_are_not_disclosed_or_world_readable, Review6.test_f03_credential_urls_are_not_stored, Review6.test_remote_url_shapes_that_are_already_safe, plus all of test_replay) against work-urls/G21/bead: 'Ran 68 tests in 77.116s OK'; the verifier's extra guards (verify-urls-cwdremote/extra/test_u04_extra.py) against work-urls/G21/bead: 'Ran 4 tests in 4.435s OK' (pristine: 2 failures, the two-claims and named-relative-URL cases). With all five urls diffs applied together (final/urls-combined.diff = G05 + G21 + a hand merge of G06/G39/G40 + G06's test hunk; BEAD_UNDER_TEST=work-urls/all/bead, test_commands taken from that copy): test_commands 'Ran 97 tests in 305.739s OK'; test_protocol 'Ran 54 tests in 227.513s OK'; test_replay 'Ran 60 tests in 109.659s OK'; test_storage 'Ran 9 tests in 30.635s OK' (load average ~190 during the runs). No shipped test needs adapting.

#### G14 · Medium · wrong-readiness — A key-based retraction of an imported dangling dependency (`dep remove ISSUE KEY`, or a re-import that drops it) is lost when another machine's concurrent create of KEY has the earlier event id; the clock, not causality, decides whether the edge comes back

**Mechanism.** apply_event dep_remove, key branch (bead 1104-1107 at e9eed92) only filters issue['dangling']: it records nothing in issue['retracted'] (only the uid branch does, 1113) and never touches dep_tags. Every create runs materialise_dangling for every issue that still has dangling entries (1014-1017, 862-878), which moves the entry into dep_tags[(target, type)] tagged with the importing create's uid (877). causal_order (721-750) breaks ties between concurrent events by event id (UUIDv7, wall clock). `dep remove` emits {key} when KEY does not resolve on the remover's machine (2434-2436); import_records emits the same for a known record that no longer lists a dangling dependency (2003-2008). If a concurrent create of KEY sorts before that dep_remove, the entry is already an edge when the removal applies and the removal does nothing; if it sorts after, the removal empties dangling and the create binds nothing. Same causal history, two results. The F09 retracted check in materialise_dangling (873-875) does not help: key removals record no retraction.

**Reproduction.** m1: `init --prefix ex`; `import a.jsonl` (ex-a depends on ex-b, which is not an issue); `sync`. m2: `sync`; `import b.jsonl` (ex-b, depends on ex-a). m1 (has not seen ex-b): `dep remove ex-a ex-b` -> key-based. Then sync m1, m2, m1, m2 and `show ex-a` / `ready` / `dep cycles`. Variant: m1 re-imports ex-a without the dependency instead of `dep remove`. Replay-level: four hand-written events (init, create ex-a with dangling ex-b, dep_remove {key: ex-b}, concurrent create ex-b) projected with the two concurrent ids swapped. All three are in G14.py.

**Observed.** Pristine e9eed92, G14.py: "AssertionError: Lists differ: [('ex-b', 'blocks')] != [] : the dependency m1 took back came back once the concurrent import of ex-b was merged" (m1 showed [] right after its removal); re-import variant: "Lists differ: [('ex-b', 'blocks')] != [] : the re-import's retraction was undone by the concurrent import of its target"; replay: "Tuples differ: ([('01900000-0000-7000-8000-000000000002', 'blocks')], True) != ([], False) : the key-based removal lost because the target's create had the smaller id" (the removal-first order gives ([], False)).

**Expected.** A dependency taken back by a writer who had seen the import that carried it stays taken back whichever concurrent event has the earlier clock: ex-a has no dependency on ex-b, ex-a is ready, no cycle. A concurrent explicit `dep add` by another writer still wins (add-wins), because only the import's own tag is retracted.

**Proposed fix.** In the key branch of dep_remove, after filtering dangling, look up st.by_key[key]; if the key is already bound and the removal saw the issue's create, pop the issue's own uid tag (the tag only materialise_dangling writes) from edges to that target of the matching type. by_key[K] never changes once set, so the removal finds the edge the import bound to in either order. Explicit dep_add tags (event ids) are untouched. Caveat: this changes how existing {key} dep_remove events replay; a crew on mixed bead versions disagrees on such an edge until all machines upgrade (FORMAT is unchanged, nothing flags it).

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G14_KeyRetractionLostToConcurrentTarget` (3 tests). On e9eed92: FAILED (failures=3): AssertionError: Lists differ: [('ex-b', 'blocks')] != [] : the dependency m1 took back came back once the concurrent import of ex-b was merged With the diff: Ran 3 tests in 4.718s OK (work-dangling/G14/bead); also OK on work-dangling/combined/bead

**Shipped suites with the diff.** This diff alone (work-dangling/G14/bead): test_replay Ran 60 tests in 121.079s OK; test_commands Ran 97 tests in 407.416s OK. Reporter's model_dangling.py (in-process, 500 seeds): pristine 111 clock-dependent histories, combined copy 0 (0 nondeterministic on both; --twins 300 seeds: 0 nondeterministic on both). Combined copy (work-dangling/combined/bead = dangling-combined.diff, all seven fixes): test_replay Ran 60 OK; test_commands Ran 97 OK; test_protocol Ran 54 OK; test_storage Ran 9 OK; test_compat CompatAuditCommentIds + CompatAuditRefValidation Ran 2 OK (CompatAuditRealCompatibility not run: it reads the real exports under ~/Documents). test_stress not run (it never imports, so no dangling dependency exists there). No shipped test needs adapting.

```diff
         issue["dep_tags"].setdefault(edge, {})[eid] = {"created_at": when, "created_by": who, **(data.get("meta") or {})}
     elif op == "dep_remove":  # removes only the additions its writer had seen
-        if data.get("key"):  # a dependency on a key that is not an issue yet
+        if data.get("key"):  # an imported dependency on a key that was not an issue for its writer
```

#### G15 · Medium · wrong-readiness — create --parent (mint_key) and the merge rename (State.free_key) can take a key an imported dependency is still waiting for, so an unrelated new issue becomes that dependency's blocker and the genuine record imported later is skipped as already known

**Mechanism.** mint_key (bead 1252-1261 at e9eed92) numbers a child as max(N for parent.N in st.by_key)+1 and checks hash keys only against st.by_key; State.free_key (798-809), which renames the second create of a key two machines minted concurrently (apply_event 991-993), also consults only self.by_key. Neither looks at issue['dangling'], where an imported dependency on a key that is not an issue yet waits. The create (1011) puts the new key in by_key and runs materialise_dangling for every issue with dangling entries (1014-1017, 862-878), so the waiting dependency binds at once to the unrelated issue. import_records then treats the genuine record with that id as known (2001-2002) and imports nothing (imported: 0).

**Reproduction.** (1) mint_key: `init --prefix ex`; import {ex-p epic, ex-p.1 child, ex-r blocks-on ex-p.2 (not in the file)}; `create 'unrelated local child' --parent ex-p`; import {ex-p.2}. (2) free_key: m1 `init`, imports ex-p, all of m1/m2/m3 sync; m3 imports ex-r (blocks on ex-p.2); then m1 and m2 each `create --parent ex-p` (both mint ex-p.1, neither has seen m3's import); sync m3, m1, m2 twice. Also reproduced the critic's shape (import synced to both machines first, then both mint ex-p.1). Scripts: work-dangling/repro_g15.py, repro_g15_late.py; tests in G15.py.

**Observed.** Pristine e9eed92 (repro_g15.py): "create --parent ex-p -> {'id': 'ex-p.2', 'title': 'unrelated local child', ... 'dependents': [{'id': 'ex-r', 'title': 'waits for ex-p.2', 'status': 'open', 'dependency_type': 'blocks'}]"; "ex-r after: [{'id': 'ex-p.2', 'title': 'unrelated local child', ...}] blocked True"; "import second -> {'imported': 0}"; "after closing the local child, ready: ['ex-p.1', 'ex-r']". Merge rename (critic's shape): "x ex-r deps: [('ex-p.2', 'machine y child')] blocked True", same on y. G15.py on pristine: "AssertionError: 'ex-p.2' == 'ex-p.2' : create --parent minted ex-p.2, the key an imported dependency waits for" and "AssertionError: Lists differ: ['machine 2 child'] != []".

**Expected.** A new or renamed issue never takes a key an imported dependency is still waiting for: the dependency stays unresolved (ex-r stays ready), and the genuine record imports normally (imported: 1) and becomes ex-r's blocker.

**Proposed fix.** Add State.claimed_keys() = set(by_key) | every issue's dangling depends_on_id, and make mint_key and free_key avoid it (a child is numbered above any waiting parent.N; a rename skips waiting keys). Both halves are needed: with only the mint_key half the merge-rename test still fails ('machine 2 child' captured); with only the free_key half the create --parent test still fails. Limit: free_key knows only dependencies already applied in replay order; if the import carrying the dangling dependency sorts after the colliding creates (its writer was concurrent and later by clock), the renamed child still takes the key (repro_g15_late.py on the patched copy: "import sorts after the children: [('ex-p.2', 'machine 2 child')]"). That residual case is the general 'a dangling key binds to whatever issue holds it' semantics and would need a design change. Replay-semantics caveat as in G14: a renamed key can differ between bead versions when a dangling dependency names it.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G15_NewKeyCapturesPendingImportedDependency` (2 tests). On e9eed92: FAILED (failures=2): AssertionError: 'ex-p.2' == 'ex-p.2' : create --parent minted ex-p.2, the key an imported dependency waits for; AssertionError: Lists differ: ['machine 2 child'] != [] : m1: the renamed child took ex-p.2 and captured ex-r's imported dependency With the diff: Ran 2 tests in 4.234s OK (work-dangling/G15/bead); also OK on combined. Partial copies: G15mint fails test_merge_rename_..., G15free fails test_local_child_key_...

#### G33 · Medium · wrong-readiness — Two records with the same id in one import are silently minted as two issues (second renamed, first record's blockers moved onto it) instead of failing whole

**Mechanism.** import_records validates each record alone (bead:1993-2000) and never checks for a repeated id. `fresh` keeps both copies (2002). `created[rec['id']]` is overwritten by the second copy's uid (2025), so every copy's dependencies, the first record's included, attach to the second copy (2029-2033). In replay the second create finds its key taken and is renamed by free_key (bead:989-993; e.g. ex-a -> ex-a8). The first copy loses its blockers and shows as ready. A file holding an export twice (a union merge of issues.jsonl) yields two claimable copies of every issue. br 0.5.7 refuses such a file (`Duplicate issue id 'ex-a' ... at line 2`, rc 7, per verifier 21). Not covered by section 2: the renumbered-key decision is about two machines minting offline, while section 2 also says an import with an invalid record fails whole.

**Reproduction.** `bead import dup.jsonl` with two records `{"id":"ex-a",...}`; and `bead import twice.jsonl` holding [ex-x, ex-y blocked by ex-x] twice, then `bead list --all` and `bead ready`.

**Observed.** e9eed92: `import with a repeated id was accepted: {"imported": 2}`; the doubled export gives `['ex-x', 'ex-xd', 'ex-y', 'ex-yp'] not found in ([], ['ex-x', 'ex-y']) : import rc 0 {"imported": 4}`.

**Expected.** An import naming one id twice fails whole with VALIDATION (like br) and writes nothing; importing the same records once stays idempotent.

**Proposed fix.** After the per-record validation loop, a second pass over the records refuses a repeated id with VALIDATION, `<path>:<n>: record id 'ex-a' repeats record <m>`, before anything is emitted.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G33_DuplicateIdsInOneImportFailWhole` (2 tests). On e9eed92: FAILED (failures=2): `AssertionError: 0 == 0 : import with a repeated id was accepted: {"imported": 2}` and `AssertionError: ['ex-x', 'ex-xd', 'ex-y', 'ex-yp'] not found in ([], ['ex-x', 'ex-y']) : import rc 0 {"imported": 4}`. With the diff: Ran 2 tests OK.

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Run from the repo root with BEAD_UNDER_TEST=<scratch>/tree-G33/bead (the patched copy, with infra/compat_real.py copied beside it so test_compat's real-export test runs) and a scratch BEAD_HOME/TMPDIR: tests.test_commands: Ran 97 tests OK; tests.test_compat: Ran 3 tests OK (real exports: none repeats an id). No shipped test needs adapting.

```diff
+++ b/bead
@@ -1998,6 +1998,10 @@
                                              or not d["depends_on_id"] or not isinstance(d.get("type"), (str, type(None)))
                                              for d in deps):
             raise BpError("VALIDATION", f"{path}:{number}: record {rec['id']!r} has a malformed dependency")
+    first = {}
+    for number, rec in enumerate(records, 1):  # one id is one issue; br refuses a file that repeats one too
+        if first.setdefault(rec["id"], number) != number:
+            raise BpError("VALIDATION", f"{path}:{number}: record id {rec['id']!r} repeats record {first[rec['id']]}")
     known = dict(tx.state.by_key)
     fresh = [rec for rec in records if rec["id"] not in known]
     for rec in records:  # a known record that no longer lists a dependency we still hold by key takes it back
```

#### G16 · Medium · robustness — Concurrent bead processes on one machine collide on refs/beads/remote: syncs and claims fail with a false REMOTE_UNREACHABLE 'cannot reach the coordination remote ... cannot lock ref'

**Mechanism.** Link.fetch (bead:1624-1625) fetches `+<ref>:refs/beads/remote` into the store's one private sync.git, then reads the tip back from that ref (bead:1631). git fetch updates its destination against the old value it read first, so when another bead process's fetch (or publish's update-ref, bead:1777) moved it in between, git exits 1 with "error: cannot lock ref 'refs/beads/remote': is at X but expected Y". bead:1626-1630 maps every fetch failure except 'find remote ref' to REMOTE_UNREACHABLE 'cannot reach the coordination remote'. synchronise's first fetch (bead:1850) runs outside the store lock, so it collides with any other process's fetch, including a claim's fetch under the lock (bead:1892); a claim does not retry a fetch error. Agents in several worktrees of one repository on one machine share one store, the target workload.

**Reproduction.** Sandbox, machines a and b; a: init, create 6 issues, sync; b: sync. Three rounds of: b creates and syncs (the remote moves), then on a start 5 `bead sync --json` and 2 `bead claim KEY --json` (distinct issues, distinct BEAD_THREAD) at once. G16.py.

**Observed.** 'AssertionError: Lists differ: ["sync: cannot reach the coordination remote .../origin.git: error: cannot lock ref 'refs/beads/remote': is at d4321e308cb0cbe6df29854bf276c69806d1e3fc but expected 9337aafecc9d29cbe18532dbf72a06ff2beb7aef", ...] != [] : N of 21 concurrent commands on one machine failed' with N = 9, 15, 10 in three runs (larger runs: 27 of 40, 21 of 32).

**Expected.** Contention inside bead's private sync.git is not a remote failure: every command succeeds.

**Proposed fix.** Each fetch goes into a ref of its own process, refs/beads/fetch/<pid>-<random>; the tip is read from it, refs/beads/remote is then pointed at it with a plain update-ref (check=False: it only keeps the fetched objects reachable, nothing reads its value any more) and the per-process ref is deleted. No lock is added, so local writers never wait for network fetches: the reporter's fetch-lock diff made a local `create` wait 14.3 s behind 4 syncs and a claim against a hung remote (BEAD_NET_TIMEOUT=3); with this diff the same probe waits 2.7 s, as on e9eed92 (all five commands fail REMOTE_UNREACHABLE after 3.6 s on both). Afterwards only refs/beads/remote is left in sync.git. A process killed between fetch and delete leaves one stale refs/beads/fetch/* ref, which is harmless. publish's update-ref of refs/beads/remote (bead:1777) is unchanged (no old-value check; git retries a held ref lock for 100 ms).

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G16_ConcurrentProcessesShareNoFetchDestination`. On e9eed92: FAIL test_concurrent_syncs_and_claims_on_one_machine_do_not_fail_on_the_shared_remote_ref: AssertionError: Lists differ: ["sync: cannot reach the coordination remote .../origin.git: error: cannot lock ref 'refs/beads/remote': is at ... but expected ...", ...] != [] : 15 of 21 concurrent commands on one machine failed (earlier runs 9, 15, 10 of 21) With the diff: Ran 1 test in 6.3s OK unloaded; 14.7-17.5 s while other suites ran (also OK on the combined copy)

**Shipped suites with the diff.** G16 copy: tests.test_protocol Ran 54 tests in 130.178s OK; tests.test_storage Ran 9 tests OK. No shipped test needs a change. All five diffs applied together (push-outcomes-combined.diff, bead + tests/test_protocol.py): three textual conflicts when applied one after another with patch(1), resolved by hand in the combined diff: G16 x G41 (both edit Link.fetch's failure branch; G41's hunk fails after G16), G11 x G18 (both rewrite Link.publish's classification; combined: --porcelain status line decides, '[remote rejected]' with a non-race reason is 'refused', last_error = status summary + git's stderr), G41 x G18 (both add a line to PRIME's error codes; combined lists REMOTE_REFUSED and REMOTE_DAMAGED). Combined copy (bead from push-outcomes-combined.diff; tests from the same diff, i.e. tests/test_protocol.py with the G11 and G18 hunks): tests.test_protocol Ran 54 tests in 203.591s OK; tests.test_commands Ran 97 tests in 248.353s OK; tests.test_storage Ran 9 tests OK; tests.test_replay Ran 60 tests OK; tests.test_review7 (the lead's sweep slice, untracked file as of now) Ran 1 test OK. All five G tests and the triage tests of sources 01, 02, 03, 05 and 50 pass on the combined copy. Not run: test_compat, test_stress.

```diff
+++ b/bead
@@ -1621,14 +1621,19 @@
             created = run_git(["init", "--quiet", "--bare", str(self.git_dir)], check=False)
             if created.returncode and not (self.git_dir / "HEAD").exists():
                 raise BpError("GIT_ERROR", created.stderr.decode(errors="replace").strip())
-        proc = run_git(["fetch", "--quiet", "--no-tags", self.url, f"+{self.ref}:{REMOTE_REF}"],
+        # Into a ref of this process's own: git fetch updates its destination only if it still holds the value
+        # fetch read first, and every bead process of this store fetches at its own time.

         if manifest.returncode:
             raise BpError("MANIFEST_MISSING", f"{self.ref} on {self.url} has no manifest; refusing to adopt it")
```

#### G18 · Medium · robustness — A definite push refusal is treated as a race or as no answer: a declining hook costs 12 rounds (~20 s) and ends SYNC_CONFLICT 'the remote journal kept moving'; a read-only key, HTTP 403 or pre-push hook is reported as 'the push got no answer' / 'the remote is not answering'; git's reason is never shown

**Mechanism.** Link.publish (bead:1758-1783) knows three outcomes. A '[remote rejected]' status line (the remote's own verdict on the ref: pre-receive or update hook declined, hidden ref, missing objects, unpacker error) matches the race regex at bead:1780-1782 and is 'rejected', i.e. 'someone else got there first'; synchronise (bead:1849-1866) and coordinated (bead:1890-1940) merge and retry up to 12 times with sleeps random(0.1,0.4)*attempt, then raise SYNC_CONFLICT 'the remote journal kept moving' (bead:1866, 1940) without the remote's reason. Every other non-zero push (no per-ref status: HTTP 401/403, a read-only ssh key, a local pre-push hook, push.gpgSign against a server without signed push) falls through to 'uncertain' at bead:1783 and push.stderr is discarded: synchronise raises the fixed text 'the push got no answer; nothing was lost, run sync again' (bead:1862-1863); coordinated keeps the attempt pending, the fence in settle_pending (bead:1830-1842) is also 'uncertain', and it raises UNRESOLVED 'the remote is not answering' (bead:1903-1905). PRIME says REMOTE_UNREACHABLE means 'retry later', which never helps here. A failed fetch, by contrast, quotes git (bead:1629). Keeping such a push 'uncertain' is right (a non-zero push without a status line may have landed); only the message is wrong.

**Reproduction.** (1) Sandbox, machine a: init --prefix po; create x; origin.git/hooks/pre-receive `echo 'refs/beads/* may not be pushed here' >&2; exit 1`; `bead sync --json`; `bead claim KEY --json`. (2) writer: init, create, sync; reader with GIT_SSH_COMMAND=a fake ssh that execs git-upload-pack but answers git-receive-pack with 'ERROR: The key you are authenticating with has been marked as read only.' and exit 1, BEAD_REMOTE=ssh://fakehost<origin>: sync (reads), comments add KEY, sync, claim KEY. Both in G18.py.

**Observed.** (1) 'AssertionError: 'SYNC_CONFLICT' == 'SYNC_CONFLICT' : sync: a refusal by the remote was retried as a race: {'code': 'SYNC_CONFLICT', 'message': 'the remote journal kept moving; run sync again'} after 20.3s'. (2) ''marked as read only' not found in 'the push got no answer; nothing was lost, run sync again''; the claim then fails UNRESOLVED 'an ownership attempt is unsettled and the remote is not answering; ...' (source 3; same for HTTP 403, a pre-push hook and push.gpgSign).

**Expected.** A refusal by the remote is reported at once as such, naming the ref and quoting git; nothing is retried. A push whose outcome is unknown stays unknown (pending attempt kept) but the message says what git said instead of claiming the remote did not answer.

**Proposed fix.** Link.last_error keeps what git said about the last push that did not go through. publish returns a new outcome 'refused' when git's own line ` ! [remote rejected] ...` (line-anchored on stderr, so a hook's `remote:` text cannot forge it) names a reason other than a lost race (cannot lock ref, failed to update ref, failed to lock, incorrect old value, non-fast-forward stay 'rejected'). synchronise and settle_pending raise REMOTE_REFUSED 'the remote refuses bead's update of refs/beads/journal; nothing was lost, and retrying will not help until the remote allows it: <git>' (settle_pending leaves pending attempts pending: the fence did not land); coordinated drops its own attempt (definitely not applied) and raises the same. The REMOTE_UNREACHABLE text for an uncertain push becomes 'the push did not complete; nothing was lost, run sync again once the cause is fixed: <git>' and UNRESOLVED says 'the push that would settle it did not complete (<git>)'. PRIME lists REMOTE_REFUSED. Merges the lead's G04 hunks (same 'refused'/REMOTE_REFUSED design, reason taken from git's own status line rather than any stderr line) with source 3's reason reporting. Shipped test hunk: tests/test_protocol.py:960 Protocol3.test_rejected_fences_leave_attempt_pending_and_excluded pins SYNC_CONFLICT for a pre-receive hook that declines every push; it becomes REMOTE_REFUSED, and every other assertion of that test (pending attempt unchanged, issue not claimed, title unchanged, settled once the hook is removed) passes unchanged. The bead hunks apply to a directory holding only bead; the tests hunk needs tests/test_protocol.py (checked with it present: applies, result identical). Not in this diff: LC_ALL=C for Link's git (lead's G03) would make the English patterns locale-proof.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G18_DefiniteRefusalIsNeitherARaceNorNoAnswer` (2 tests). On e9eed92: FAIL test_policy_refusal_is_reported_at_once_with_the_remotes_reason: AssertionError: 'SYNC_CONFLICT' == 'SYNC_CONFLICT' : sync: a refusal by the remote was retried as a race: {'code': 'SYNC_CONFLICT', 'message': 'the remote journal kept moving; run sync again'} after 18.0s; FAIL test_transport_refusal_reports_gits_reason_not_no_answer: AssertionError: 'marked as read only' not found in 'the push got no answer; nothing was lost, run sync again' : sync hid git's reason (Ran 2 tests in 19.2s, FAILED (failures=2)) With the diff: Ran 2 tests in 2.430s OK (also OK on the combined copy). Patched messages: sync/claim REMOTE_REFUSED "the remote refuses bead's update of refs/beads/journal; nothing was lost, and retrying will not help until the remote allows it: remote: refs/beads/* may not be pushed here ! [remote rejected] <sha…

**Shipped suites with the diff.** G18 copy: tests.test_protocol unmodified: Ran 54 tests, FAILED (failures=1): Protocol3.test_rejected_fences_leave_attempt_pending_and_excluded, AssertionError: 'REMOTE_REFUSED' != 'SYNC_CONFLICT' at its first assertion (line 960); with the diff's one-line hunk (expect REMOTE_REFUSED) the whole test passes, i.e. its pending-attempt, not-claimed, title and settle-after-unhook assertions hold unchanged: Ran 54 tests in 98.854s OK. tests.test_storage: Ran 9 tests OK. @@ -2615,7 +2639,8 @@
```diff
        seen both sides settles it. --offline claims are provisional, never exclusive.
        Use --json for machine output. Error codes: ALREADY_CLAIMED, CLAIMED_BY_OTHER, NOT_OWNER,
        STATUS_CONFLICT, CYCLE, VALIDATION, ISSUE_NOT_FOUND, REMOTE_UNREACHABLE (nothing is lost;
-       retry later), UNRESOLVED (an attempt of yours awaits the remote: run bead sync), SYNC_CONFLICT,
```

#### G19 · Medium · robustness — bead's private journal pushes run the user's client pre-push hooks (init.templateDir copied into sync.git and kept there, global core.hooksPath, config-based hooks); a hook that fails there leaves every claim UNRESOLVED and every sync REMOTE_UNREACHABLE, with the hook's message never shown

**Mechanism.** bead:1621 runs `git init --quiet --bare <sync.git>` without --template=, so init.templateDir (hooks/ included) is copied into $BEAD_HOME/<ws>/sync.git and stays there after the user drops the setting. Link.env (bead:1604-1608) keeps the user's global config, so a global core.hooksPath and git >= 2.54 config-based hooks (`[hook "x"] event = pre-push`) apply too. The push at bead:1772-1773 runs pre-push with GIT_DIR=<store>/sync.git (bare) and cwd = the user's checkout. A failing hook makes git print only "error: failed to push some refs" (no [rejected]), so bead:1780-1783 return "uncertain": synchronise raises REMOTE_UNREACHABLE "the push got no answer" (bead:1862-1863); coordinated keeps the attempt pending, settle_pending's fence push (bead:1838) is refused by the same hook, and the command ends UNRESOLVED "the remote is not answering" (bead:1903-1905). Every later claim does the same. The hook's stderr is discarded. Decision on the verifiers' disagreement: global core.hooksPath and config-based pre-push hooks ARE a bead bug, not user policy. (1) The hook runs against bead's bare sync.git, a context no client hook is written for: a global hook that only runs `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` passes for the user's own `git push` from the same checkout and fails on bead's push ("fatal: this operation must be run in a work tree"). (2) bead's push is not the user's code push: a private ref holding an append-only journal, so a content-based refusal could never be cleared. (3) Client hooks are not an enforcement point (any `--no-verify` skips them); the remote's own pre-receive still runs on bead's push (its refusals are the lead's G04). push.gpgSign=true is NOT a bead bug: it is the user's explicit request, the user's own push to that remote fails the same way ("the receiving end does not support --signed push"), and the fix leaves it alone; that bead reports such a failure as "the push got no answer" is the general 'uncertain push hides git's reason' message problem, not this finding.

**Reproduction.** G19.py, each with a scratch GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL and GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1. (a) `[core] hooksPath=<dir>` whose pre-push is `git rev-parse --show-toplevel >/dev/null || exit 1`: the user's `git push -q origin HEAD:refs/heads/topic` gives rc 0; then `bead claim <id> --json` rc 1, `bead sync --json` rc 1. (b) `[init] templateDir=<t>` with a refusing pre-push: `bead init; bead create one; bead sync` creates sync.git/hooks/pre-push; empty the config; `bead claim` rc 1. (c) a refusing pre-push placed in an existing sync.git/hooks (what an older bead copied). (d) `[hook "policy"] event=pre-push command=<refusing script>` (skipped if `git hook list pre-push` does not show it).

**Observed.** (a) user push rc 0 / claim 1 {"error": {"code": "UNRESOLVED", "message": "an ownership attempt is unsettled and the remote is not answering; it counts for nothing until `bead sync` can settle it"}} / sync 1 {"error": {"code": "REMOTE_UNREACHABLE", "message": "the push got no answer; nothing was lost, run sync again"}} / pending: ['01a11807-...command', '01a11807-...json'] / hook log: "pre-push cwd=<tmp>/bp-test-j3he0npl/a-main GIT_DIR=" (the user's push, then the toplevel) followed 3 times by "pre-push cwd=<tmp>/bp-test-j3he0npl/a-main GIT_DIR=<tmp>/bp-test-j3he0npl/home-a/1ee6e7a1044276e6/sync.git\nfatal: this operation must be run in a work tree". (b) "AssertionError: True is not false : the user's template hook was copied to .../home-a/621f96e200eb4158/sync.git/hooks/pre-push". (c), (d) claim rc 1 UNRESOLVED as in (a).

**Expected.** bead's private journal pushes do not run the user's client hooks and nothing from the user's template lands in sync.git; the remote's own hooks still apply.

**Proposed fix.** `git push --quiet --no-verify` at bead:1772 (skips pre-push from every source: core.hooksPath, hook.<name> config, and a hook an older bead already copied into an existing sync.git) and `git init --quiet --bare --template=` at bead:1621 (an empty template copies nothing, so new stores get no hooks). Deliberately left out: core.hooksPath=/dev/null for the other hooks git runs in sync.git (reference-transaction on fetch/update-ref, pre-auto-gc) from a global hooksPath (rare; config-based hooks would bypass it anyway); push.gpgSign (see mechanism); and reporting git's stderr for 'uncertain' pushes (separate message issue).

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G19_ClientHooksOnPrivatePushes` (4 tests). On e9eed92: FAILED (failures=4): template: "AssertionError: True is not false : the user's template hook was copied to .../sync.git/hooks/pre-push"; global hooksPath, existing copy and config-based hook: "AssertionError: 1 != 0 : claim: {\"error\": {\"code\": \"UNRESOLVED\", \"message\": \"an ownership attempt is unsettled and the remote is not answering; it counts for nothing until `bead sync` can settle it\"}}" With the diff: Ran 4 tests, OK (G19 copy and the combined git-env copy)

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Run against one copy with all six git-env diffs applied together (G17+G19+G20+G26+G27+G62, hand-merged: work-git-env/all/bead, diff work-git-env/git-env-combined.diff), from the repo root with BEAD_UNDER_TEST set: tests.test_protocol "Ran 54 tests in 364.958s OK"; tests.test_storage "Ran 9 tests in 25.254s OK"; tests.test_commands first run "Ran 97 tests in 467.734s FAILED (errors=1)": the error is Review6.test_remote_url_shapes_that_are_already_safe failing in its first line, support.Sandbox.clone's plain `git clone` of the fresh sandbox origin (exit 128) before any bead call, under load average ~168 from other runs; that test then passed 3/3 alone and a full rerun gave "Ran 97 tests in 259.754s OK". No shipped test needs adapting. Textual conflicts (checked pairwise with patch --dry-run): G17, G19, G20 and G62 stack cleanly (applied in that sequence without fuzz); G27 conflicts with G17, G19, G20 and G26 in either order, G26 conflicts with G19 and G27, and G20 does not apply after G26 (G26 does apply after G20): same env and `git init` lines, bead:1604-1625, so the combined copy was merged by hand. G17.diff is identical to hunk 1 of final/g01-g04.diff (apply only one); the rest of g01-g04.diff applies cleanly on top of the combined copy. The remote's pre-receive still runs on bead's push with --no-verify: test_protocol's pre-receive tests (test_rejected_fences_leave_attempt_…

#### G20 · Medium · robustness — Settings that let git reach the remote from the checkout do not apply to bead's sync.git: the checkout's own config (actions/checkout's http.<url>.extraheader token, credential helpers, core.sshCommand), global includes scoped by includeIf gitdir:/hasconfig:, and GIT_SSH, which bead's GIT_SSH_COMMAND default overrides; sync and every online claim then fail with REMOTE_UNREACHABLE where git itself works

**Mechanism.** Link.__init__ (bead:1600-1612) sets GIT_DIR=$BEAD_HOME/<ws>/sync.git and gives git only the URL. git then reads system and global config for sync.git: the checkout's .git/config (and config.worktree) is never read, and conditional includes are evaluated against sync.git's own gitdir and its (absent) remotes, so `includeIf "gitdir:~/work/"` and `includeIf "hasconfig:remote.*.url:..."` drop out. bead:1607 `env.setdefault("GIT_SSH_COMMAND", "ssh -o ConnectTimeout=15 ...")` outranks both core.sshCommand (even a global one) and GIT_SSH, because git consults GIT_SSH_COMMAND, then core.sshCommand, then GIT_SSH. Covers the lead's observation 4 (per-repo core.sshCommand) with the same root cause.

**Reproduction.** G20.py, scratch GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL, nothing leaves the machine (an `ssh` first on PATH that refuses, a 127.0.0.1 smart-HTTP server via git http-backend that requires one Authorization header). (1) actions/checkout layout: `git init ci; git remote add origin http://127.0.0.1:<p>/origin.git; git config --local http.<base>/.extraheader 'AUTHORIZATION: <token>'; git fetch origin main` (rc 0), then `bead init; bead create one; bead sync --json`. (2) GIT_SSH=user-ssh (a wrapper that runs the command locally), origin ssh://fakehost<path>: the user's `git fetch` rc 0, bead sync rc 1. (3) per-repo core.sshCommand=user-ssh: same. (4) `[includeIf "gitdir:<box>/work/"] path=<inc>` with `url.<origin>.insteadOf=git@github.com:corp/proj.git`; `git clone git@github.com:corp/proj.git work/proj` works; bead sync rc 1.

**Observed.** (1) {"error": {"code": "REMOTE_UNREACHABLE", "message": "cannot reach the coordination remote http://127.0.0.1:50055/origin.git: fatal: could not read Username for 'http://127.0.0.1:50055': terminal prompts disabled"}}; (2)/(3) "cannot reach the coordination remote ssh://fakehost/.../origin.git: plain ssh: blocked in this test\nfatal: Could not read from remote repository."; (4) "cannot reach the coordination remote git@github.com:corp/proj.git: plain ssh: blocked in this test\nfatal: Could not read from remote repository."

**Expected.** bead reaches the remote the way `git fetch`/`git push` in the checkout do; bead's ssh timeout default applies only when the user has no GIT_SSH_COMMAND, GIT_SSH or core.sshCommand.

**Proposed fix.** New transport_config(): `git config --null --get-regexp '^(core\.sshcommand|ssh\.variant|http\..+|credential\..+|url\..+\.(insteadof|pushinsteadof))$'` evaluated where bead runs (the checkout: its own config, includeIf/hasconfig includes and global), replayed to sync.git's git through GIT_CONFIG_COUNT/KEY/VALUE (appended after any the caller set). Resettable lists (credential[.<url>].helper, http[.<url>].extraheader) are first emptied with "" under their exact key, then every value is given in the checkout's order: the effective lists equal the checkout's (no header or helper sent twice when an include applies to both, which is the regression the verifier found in the reporter's diff: a doubled extraheader got 401); single-valued keys end on the checkout's last value (precedence kept); a key with no value is carried as "true" (a bare `[http] sslVerify` stays true). The GIT_SSH_COMMAND default is set only when none of GIT_SSH_COMMAND, GIT_SSH or core.sshCommand is present (NET_TIMEOUT still bounds the user's command). Checked with a probe: checkout vs sync.git env give http.sslverify true/true, core.sshcommand ssh-B/ssh-B (with ssh-A from an earlier includeIf), credential helpers [G, I, L]/[G, I, L]. Cost: one `git config` per Link. Carried values (an extraheader token included) travel in GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_n variables of bead's own git processes, as `git -c` would; they come from config the user can already read. Residual: a transport key that only sync.git sees (an include matching BEAD_HOME but not the checkout) is not removed unless it is a resettable list the checkout also has.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G20_TransportSettings` (4 tests). On e9eed92: FAILED (failures=4): e.g. "AssertionError: 1 != 0 : git reaches the remote from this checkout, bead does not: {\"error\": {\"code\": \"REMOTE_UNREACHABLE\", \"message\": \"cannot reach the coordination remote http://127.0.0.1:50055/origin.git: fatal: could not read Username for 'http://127.0.0.1:50055': terminal prompts disabled\"}}" With the diff: Ran 4 tests, OK (G20 copy and the combined git-env copy). The same test against a variant without the resets fails on the X-Guard header sent twice (the server answers 401), so it guards the verifier's regression.

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Run against one copy with all six git-env diffs applied together (G17+G19+G20+G26+G27+G62, hand-merged: work-git-env/all/bead, diff work-git-env/git-env-combined.diff), from the repo root with BEAD_UNDER_TEST set: tests.test_protocol "Ran 54 tests in 364.958s OK"; tests.test_storage "Ran 9 tests in 25.254s OK"; tests.test_commands first run "Ran 97 tests in 467.734s FAILED (errors=1)": the error is Review6.test_remote_url_shapes_that_are_already_safe failing in its first line, support.Sandbox.clone's plain `git clone` of the fresh sandbox origin (exit 128) before any bead call, under load average ~168 from other runs; that test then passed 3/3 alone and a full rerun gave "Ran 97 tests in 259.754s OK". No shipped test needs adapting. Textual conflicts (checked pairwise with patch --dry-run): G17, G19, G20 and G62 stack cleanly (applied in that sequence without fuzz); G27 conflicts with G17, G19, G20 and G26 in either order, G26 conflicts with G19 and G27, and G20 does not apply after G26 (G26 does apply after G20): same env and `git init` lines, bead:1604-1625, so the combined copy was merged by hand. G17.diff is identical to hunk 1 of final/g01-g04.diff (apply only one); the rest of g01-g04.diff applies cleanly on top of the combined copy. Independent check (check-git-env): git-env-combined.diff applied to a fresh pristine copy reproduces work-git-env/all/bead byte for byte; a…

<details><summary>Proposed diff (G20, applies to e9eed92 with <code>patch -p1</code>)</summary>

```diff
--- a/bead
+++ b/bead
@@ -1589,7 +1589,25 @@
 
 
 # --------------------------------------------------------------------- sync
+
+TRANSPORT_KEYS = r"^(core\.sshcommand|ssh\.variant|http\..+|credential\..+|url\..+\.(insteadof|pushinsteadof))$"
+RESETTABLE = re.compile(r"^(credential\.(.+\.)?helper|http\.(.+\.)?extraheader)$")  # an empty value clears the list
+
 
+def transport_config() -> list[tuple[str, str]]:
+    """How git reaches remotes from here: the caller's repository config, its includeIf includes and the
+    global ones, which sync.git (elsewhere, with no remotes) does not all see. Given at command-line
+    precedence in this order, these settings make sync.git's git reach the remote as the caller's does:
+    lists that can be reset are emptied first, so nothing is sent twice."""
+    proc = run_git(["config", "--null", "--get-regexp", TRANSPORT_KEYS], check=False)
+    rows = []
+    for item in proc.stdout.decode(errors="replace").split("\0") if proc.returncode == 0 else []:
+        if item:
+            key, newline, value = item.partition("\n")
+            rows.append((key, value if newline else "true"))  # a key without a value means true
+    return [(key, "") for key in dict.fromkeys(key for key, _ in rows) if RESETTABLE.match(key)] + rows
+
+
 class Link:
     """The coordination ref on one remote, reached through a private bare repository.
 
@@ -1604,7 +1622,14 @@
         env = {k: v for k, v in os.environ.items() if k not in ("GIT_WORK_TREE", "GIT_INDEX_FILE", "GIT_COMMON_DIR")}
         env.update(GIT_DIR=str(self.git_dir), GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT="0", GIT_AUTHOR_NAME="bead",
                    GIT_AUTHOR_EMAIL="bead@localhost", GIT_COMMITTER_NAME="bead", GIT_COMMITTER_EMAIL="bead@localhost")
-        env.setdefault("GIT_SSH_COMMAND", "ssh -o ConnectTimeout=15 -o ServerAliveInterval=15 -o ServerAliveCountMax=2")  # a dead remote must fail, not hang
+        carried = transport_config()
+        count = int(env["GIT_CONFIG_COUNT"]) if env.get("GIT_CONFIG_COUNT", "").isdigit() else 0
+        for index, (key, value) in enumerate(carried, count):
+            env[f"GIT_CONFIG_KEY_{index}"], env[f"GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_{index}"] = key, value
+        if carried:
+            env["GIT_CONFIG_COUNT"] = str(count + len(carried))
+        if "GIT_SSH_COMMAND" not in env and "GIT_SSH" not in env and all(key != "core.sshcommand" for key, _ in carried):
+            env["GIT_SSH_COMMAND"] = "ssh -o ConnectTimeout=15 -o ServerAliveInterval=15 -o ServerAliveCountMax=2"  # a dead remote must fail, not hang
         self.env = env
         self.remote_counts: dict[str, int] = {}
         self.forks: list[str] = []
```
</details>

#### G17 · Medium · robustness — Under a translated git (LANG/LC_ALL=de_DE.UTF-8 or fr_FR.UTF-8) Link.fetch does not recognise "couldn't find remote ref", so the first sync of every new tracker, and every claim before anyone has published, fails with REMOTE_UNREACHABLE

**Mechanism.** Link.__init__ (bead:1604-1608) builds sync.git's environment from os.environ, so LANG/LC_ALL/LANGUAGE reach every git it runs. Link.fetch (bead:1624-1625) fetches refs/beads/journal; while nobody has published, git exits 128 with "fatal: couldn't find remote ref refs/beads/journal", and bead:1627 recognises that case only by the English bytes b"find remote ref" to return None. A translated git prints "Schwerwiegend: Konnte Remote-Referenz refs/beads/journal nicht finden." (de) or the French equivalent, so bead:1629-1630 raise REMOTE_UNREACHABLE. synchronise (bead:1850) and coordinated (bead:1892) both fetch first, so `sync` and every online claim fail until a machine with an untranslated git publishes. The push classifier at bead:1780-1781 also matches English text; its main markers "[rejected]" / "[remote rejected]" are not translated (checked: a non-fast-forward push under de_DE still prints " ! [rejected] ... (fetch first)"), but "cannot lock ref" and "failed to update ref" are.

**Reproduction.** In a tests.support Sandbox: Machine a with a.extra LANG=LC_ALL=de_DE.UTF-8; `bead init --prefix lo`; `bead create x`; `bead sync --json` (rc 1); `bead claim <id> --json` (rc 1). G17.py skips when git has neither German nor French messages (probe: `git --git-dir <origin> fetch <origin> refs/beads/none` in that locale).

**Observed.** AssertionError: 1 != 0 : first sync under de_DE.UTF-8: {"error": {"code": "REMOTE_UNREACHABLE", "message": "cannot reach the coordination remote .../bp-test-8_xscyf4/origin.git: Schwerwiegend: Konnte Remote-Referenz refs/beads/journal nicht finden."}}

**Expected.** The first sync publishes the tracker and claims work, whatever the user's locale.

**Proposed fix.** LC_ALL=C in Link.env (one line; identical to hunk 1 of final/g01-g04.diff, so only one of the two should be applied). What it does to other messages bead shows: git's text that bead embeds from sync.git commands (REMOTE_UNREACHABLE "cannot reach the coordination remote X: <git stderr>", GIT_ERROR from Link.git) is now English instead of the user's language; bead's own text was already English, so these messages stop being mixed. Seen under de_DE for an unreachable remote: pristine "...missing.git: Schwerwiegend: '...missing.git' does not appear to be a git repository\x0aSchwerwiegend: Konnte nicht vom Remote-Repository lesen.\x0a...", patched "...missing.git: fatal: '...missing.git' does not appear to be a git repository\x0afatal: Could not read from remote repository.\x0a...". ssh's own messages ("ssh: connect to host fakehost port 22: Connection refused") are unchanged. LC_ALL=C also overrides LANGUAGE (checked: LANGUAGE=de LC_ALL=C git fetch prints English). Not affected: the `git init` at bead:1621 (raw environment, output not parsed) and the checkout-side git calls in find_workspace (not parsed; see G62). Side effect to know: with ssh's common `SendEnv LC_*`, a server that accepts it runs its git in the C locale too (its "remote:" lines then come in English).

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G17_TranslatedGit`. On e9eed92: FAIL test_first_sync_and_claim_work_under_a_translated_git: AssertionError: 1 != 0 : first sync under de_DE.UTF-8: {"error": {"code": "REMOTE_UNREACHABLE", "message": "cannot reach the coordination remote .../origin.git: Schwerwiegend: Konnte Remote-Referenz refs/beads/journal nicht finden."}} With the diff: Ran 1 test, OK (G17 copy and the combined git-env copy)

No shipped test needs adapting. G17.diff is identical to hunk 1 of final/g01-g04.diff (apply only one); the rest of g01-g04.diff applies cleanly on top of the combined copy. #### G24 · Medium · robustness — A URL-derived workspace id changes when the repository is renamed or moved (or its URL is respelled): a machine that updates origin and every fresh clone under the new URL are refused by their own origin's tracker with WORKSPACE_MISMATCH, which names no remedy, and no command adopts it

**Mechanism.** find_workspace hashes normalize_remote(origin) (bead:210-212). After a rename/move (org/old -> org/new, or another spelling such as ssh://git@ssh.github.com:443/...) the hash differs. The new id's store is empty: read commands give NOT_INITIALIZED (bead:1247-1249) and Link.fetch refuses the published manifest with 'belongs to workspace X, not Y' (bead:1640-1641), naming no remedy. `init --tracked` mints a new uuid (bead:1965) rather than adopting X; only an undocumented BEAD_WORKSPACE=X recovers.

**Reproduction.** a: `bead init --prefix rn; bead create one; bead sync`; `mv origin.git renamed.git`; a: `git remote set-url origin renamed.git`; `bead info`, `bead show X`, `bead sync --json`; fresh clone c of renamed.git: `bead sync --json`.

**Observed.** before: workspace bb044fcf48efd3ae | after set-url: workspace 60e725181ec5958f | a show exit 1 {"error": {"code": "NOT_INITIALIZED", ...}} | a sync exit 1 {"error": {"code": "WORKSPACE_MISMATCH", "message": "refs/beads/journal on renamed.git belongs to workspace bb044fcf48efd3ae, not 60e725181ec5958f"}} | fresh clone c sync exit 1 (same WORKSPACE_MISMATCH)

**Expected.** When the id was derived from the URL, the derived store is empty, and the refusing remote is this checkout's own origin, sync adopts the published workspace and remembers the mapping for later commands. Otherwise (a remote named with --remote, a store with data) the refusal stands and names the remedy.

**Proposed fix.** Verifier's guarded diff, unchanged in logic: Workspace.derived records a URL-derived id; ~/.../moved.json maps derived ids to adopted ones and find_workspace applies it; Link.fetch's WORKSPACE_MISMATCH carries the found id and now says 'set BEAD_WORKSPACE=<found> or commit a .beadroll.json holding {"workspace": "<found>"}'; synchronise, on the first fetch only, calls adopt_published, which adopts only when the id is derived and unmapped, the remote equals this checkout's origin (resolved as remote() resolves it), and the derived store has no segments, claims or pending attempts. Changes from the verifier's version: the found id is validated by Workspace() inside a try (an invalid id from a remote manifest keeps the original WORKSPACE_MISMATCH instead of becoming CONFIG), and the hint names the exact .beadroll.json content. Remaining gaps: adoption happens in sync only (a claim as a fresh clone's first network command still gets the mismatch, now with the hint); a machine that already ran `bead init` under the new id is not adopted; an empty sync.git is left under the derived id.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G24_RenamedRepositoryLosesItsTracker` (2 tests). On e9eed92: AssertionError: 1 != 0 : {"error": {"code": "WORKSPACE_MISMATCH", "message": "refs/beads/journal on .../renamed.git belongs to workspace 05af3094219ce979, not c03e2021d849b753"}} (both tests). FAILED (failures=2). With the diff: Ran 2 tests, OK (also OK against the combined group copy). The verifier's insteadOf-based rename tests (old URL still redirecting; foreign --remote guard) also pass: Ran 3 tests OK.

Load average was 80-180 during these runs; no flakes seen. +def adopt_published(ws: Workspace, remote: str, error: BpError) -> Workspace | None:
```diff
+    """A URL-derived id that finds this checkout's own origin already holding a tracker under another id
+    (the repository was renamed, or its URL is spelled differently here) adopts that tracker, once, while
+    nothing has been written under the derived id. A remote named any other way is never adopted."""
+    found = getattr(error, "found", None)
+    if error.code != "WORKSPACE_MISMATCH" or not ws.derived or ws.id != ws.derived or not isinstance(found, str):

+    origin = without_password(ws.origin or "")[0]
+    if not origin or remote != (os.path.realpath(origin) if os.path.exists(origin) else origin):
+        return None
+    if segment_files(ws) or claim_files(ws) or pending_files(ws):
+        return None
+    try:
+        adopted = Workspace(found, ws.name, ws.origin, ws.config)  # validates the id (A07)
+    except BpError:
+        return None
+    adopted.derived = ws.derived
+    write_atomic(home() / "moved.json", json.dumps({**moved_workspaces(), ws.derived: found}, indent=1).encode())
+    return adopted
+
+

+            if adopted is None:

+            return synchronise(adopted, remote, actor)
         with locked(ws):
```

#### G26 · Medium · robustness — sync.git is created in this machine's default object format, not the project's: a SHA-256 project, or a SHA-256 default (init.defaultObjectFormat, GIT_DEFAULT_HASH, Git 3's announced default) against a SHA-1 project, can never publish, and a sync.git once made in the wrong format stays wrong

**Mechanism.** Link.fetch (bead:1619-1623) runs `git init --quiet --bare <sync.git>` without --object-format, so the hash follows init.defaultObjectFormat / GIT_DEFAULT_HASH, not the project. Before anyone has published, fetch returns None via "couldn't find remote ref" (bead:1627); the push of a commit in the other format then fails client-side ("src refspec <sha> does not match any"), which matches none of the patterns at bead:1780-1781, so publish returns "uncertain" (bead:1783): sync raises REMOTE_UNREACHABLE "the push got no answer" (bead:1863), a claim ends UNRESOLVED (bead:1904). Once a journal exists, every fetch fails with "fatal: mismatched algorithms: client sha256; server sha1" (bead:1629). bead:1619 never re-checks an existing sync.git, so one run under GIT_DEFAULT_HASH=sha256 poisons the store for good.

**Reproduction.** G26.py. (1) `git init --bare --object-format=sha256` origin, clone, commit, push; `bead init; bead create one; bead sync --json`. (2) scratch global `[init] defaultObjectFormat = sha256` against the sandbox's SHA-1 origin (the user's own `git fetch` works): `bead init; bead create one; bead sync`. (3) replace the store's sync.git by a SHA-256 one (what a run under GIT_DEFAULT_HASH=sha256 leaves), then sync with no such setting, before and after a journal exists. Also a probe: machine b's first `bead sync` with GIT_DEFAULT_HASH=sha256, then sync and claim without it.

**Observed.** (1),(2),(3): "AssertionError: 1 != 0 : SHA-256 project: {\"error\": {\"code\": \"REMOTE_UNREACHABLE\", \"message\": \"the push got no answer; nothing was lost, run sync again\"}}" (likewise "SHA-256 default, SHA-1 project" and "no journal published yet"). Probe: "b first sync with GIT_DEFAULT_HASH=sha256: 1 {\"error\": {\"code\": \"REMOTE_UNREACHABLE\", \"message\": \"cannot reach the coordination remote .../origin.git: fatal: mismatched algorithms: client sha256; server sha1\"}}" / "b sync without it: 1 (same)" / "b sync.git format: sha256" / "b claim: 1 (same)".

**Expected.** sync.git has the project's object format, so sync and claims work; a sync.git made earlier in another format is made again (it holds nothing that is not also in events/ or on the remote).

**Proposed fix.** object_format(): `git rev-parse --show-object-format` where bead runs (accepted only if sha1/sha256). Once per Link, fetch compares it with sync.git's format and, if they differ, renames sync.git aside and deletes it; sync.git is then created with --object-format=<project's>. Locale-independent (no parsing of "mismatched algorithms"), and it also repairs stores before anyone has published, which the reporter's message-based rebuild missed. Adds `import shutil`. Cost: two quick git calls per command. Residual: outside a repository (BEAD_WORKSPACE) or with a coordination remote of another format than the checkout (.beadroll.json / --remote naming another repository) the machine default still decides; two processes that both find a wrong-format sync.git at once can make one of them fail once (the rename happens outside the store lock, as the existing creation does).

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G26_ObjectFormat` (3 tests). On e9eed92: FAILED (failures=3): "AssertionError: 1 != 0 : SHA-256 project: {\"error\": {\"code\": \"REMOTE_UNREACHABLE\", \"message\": \"the push got no answer; nothing was lost, run sync again\"}}"; "... SHA-256 default, SHA-1 project: ..."; "... no journal published yet: ..." With the diff: Ran 3 tests, OK (G26 copy and the combined git-env copy); the probe gives rc 0 for all four steps and sync.git format sha1.

G17.diff is identical to hunk 1 of final/g01-g04.diff (apply only one); the rest of g01-g04.diff applies cleanly on top of the combined copy.
