#### G27 · Medium · robustness — A sync.git that `git init` did not finish (an empty directory, or HEAD without objects/) is never completed, so every sync and claim fails with REMOTE_UNREACHABLE 'not a git repository' forever; GIT_WORK_TREE or GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY at first contact, or an init killed midway, leave it so

**Mechanism.** Link.fetch (bead:1619) creates sync.git only when the directory is absent and runs the init (bead:1621) with the raw os.environ, not self.env. With GIT_WORK_TREE in the environment git creates the directory and then dies ("GIT_WORK_TREE ... not allowed without specifying GIT_DIR"); bead:1622-1623 raise GIT_ERROR. A killed init leaves the same empty directory. From then on the directory exists, so it is never initialised again, and every fetch (bead:1624) fails with "fatal: not a git repository", raised as REMOTE_UNREACHABLE at bead:1629 (documented as 'nothing is lost; retry later'). Link.env (bead:1604) strips only GIT_WORK_TREE/GIT_INDEX_FILE/GIT_COMMON_DIR: with GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY set at first contact, init and every object write go to the caller's directory, sync.git is left without objects/, and every later command without the variable fails the same way.

**Reproduction.** G27.py: machine a publishes; machine b's first `bead sync --json` runs with GIT_WORK_TREE=<checkout> (rc 1 GIT_ERROR), then `bead sync`/`bead claim` without it; or b's sync.git is replaced by an empty directory; or b's first sync runs with GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY=<dir>; or b's sync.git/objects is removed.

**Observed.** "first sync with GIT_WORK_TREE set: {\"error\": {\"code\": \"GIT_ERROR\", \"message\": \"fatal: GIT_WORK_TREE (or --work-tree=<directory>) not allowed without specifying GIT_DIR (or --git-dir=<directory>)\"}}"; "sync after finding sync.git empty: {\"error\": {\"code\": \"REMOTE_UNREACHABLE\", \"message\": \"cannot reach the coordination remote .../origin.git: fatal: not a git repository: '.../home-b/c80572134a2bfe77/sync.git'\"}}"; GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY: "Lists differ: ['c92790ab05656e186fb40cd4a096bd9de5e6fe', ...] != []" (sync.git's objects written into the caller's directory); "sync after finding sync.git without objects/: ... fatal: not a git repository: '.../home-b/e0164785db5fcc0f/sync.git'"

**Expected.** A missing or unfinished sync.git is (re)initialised, and the caller's repository-location variables never reach it.

**Proposed fix.** Link.fetch initialises sync.git whenever HEAD, objects/ or refs/ is missing (not only when the directory is absent); `git init --bare` completes an existing empty or partial directory in place and is idempotent, so no rmtree/rename is needed and two racing processes do not delete each other's work. The init runs with self.env, and Link.env strips every repository-location variable (REPO_LOCATION_ENV: `git rev-parse --local-env-vars` less the GIT_CONFIG* settings, plus GIT_NAMESPACE and GIT_QUARANTINE_PATH). Not addressed (pre-existing, separate): two first-ever syncs racing on a fresh store can still fail once with GIT_ERROR ("cannot mkdir ... File exists").

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G27_UnfinishedSyncGit` (4 tests). On e9eed92: FAILED (failures=4): "AssertionError: 1 != 0 : first sync with GIT_WORK_TREE set: {\"error\": {\"code\": \"GIT_ERROR\", ...}}"; "AssertionError: 1 != 0 : sync after finding sync.git empty: {\"error\": {\"code\": \"REMOTE_UNREACHABLE\", \"message\": \"... fatal: not a git repository: '.../sync.git'\"}}"; "AssertionError: Lists differ: ['c92790ab...', ...] != []"; "AssertionError: 1 != 0 : sync after finding sync.git without objects/: ..." With the diff: Ran 4 tests, OK (G27 copy and the combined git-env copy)

#### G28 · Medium · robustness — Unlocked readers (show/list/ready/export/prime/info) delete pending attempts and receipts they could not read (EACCES, or settled by a locked sync between glob and read), and on a read-only store one junk pending file makes every read fail with IO

**Mechanism.** read_attempt (bead:434-441) returns [] for any OSError, FileNotFoundError and PermissionError included. load_pending (690-701) treats [] as 'not an attempt' and unlinks the pending .json and its .command receipt (699-700). load_pending runs without the store lock from view() (1148-1158, call at 1152; used by show/list/ready/claims/export/prime, by every command's final view, and by synchronise's pre-lock view at 1847) and from cmd_info (2526). Under the lock, Link.merge confirms an attempt by unlinking only pending/<id>.json (1692) and keeps the receipt for settle_commands (1805-1829), whose only job is to recognise a retry of the accepted command. So: (a) a reader that cannot read an attempt (mode 000) deletes it and its receipt; (b) a reader that globbed pending/ before a locked sync confirmed the attempt reads FileNotFoundError and deletes the receipt the sync kept; (c) on a read-only store a reader's unlink of a junk pending file raises EACCES and every read command exits IO (contradicting F16). The locked sweep (synchronise 1852, settle_pending 1835) also treats an unreadable attempt as junk and deletes it with its receipt.

**Reproduction.** Sandbox machine a: init, create X, sync, claim X; `BEAD_TEST_CRASH_AFTER_PUSH=1 bead release X --json` (exit 99: accepted, ack lost; pending/<id>.json and .command remain). (a) chmod 000 pending/<id>.json; `bead show X --json`; chmod 600; `bead release X --json`. (b) instead run `bead show X` in-process with pending_files wrapped so that, after the glob, `bead sync` runs to completion; then `bead release X`. (c) write pending/01900000-0000-7000-8000-0000000000aa.json containing `{`, `chmod -R a-w` the store, run show/list/ready/export/info --json. (d) as (a) but `bead sync --json` instead of show.

**Observed.** Pristine: (a) after show, pending/ holds neither file (test: 'Tuples differ: (False, False) != (True, True) : a read-only command deleted an accepted attempt it could not read, and its receipt'); the retry fails. (b) the sync exits 0, confirms the attempt and keeps the .command; after the reader: 'AssertionError: False is not true : a reader (show, no lock) deleted the receipt the locked sync kept'. (c) all five readers: 'AssertionError: 1 != 0 : {"error": {"code": "IO", "message": "Permission denied: .../home-a/<ws>/pending/01900000-0000-7000-8000-0000000000aa.json"}}'. (d) 'sync (attempt mode 000): 0 {"pulled": 1, ...}', 'pending now: []', 'retry release: 1 {"error": {"code": "NOT_OWNER", "message": "gp-m1vc is not claimed by codex:a-thread-0001@8d629c80"}}'.

**Expected.** Only the holder of the store lock sweeps pending/, and only files it has read and found not to be attempts. A file a reader cannot read, or that vanished between glob and read (settled by the lock holder, which keeps the receipt), is skipped, never deleted. Under the lock an unreadable attempt is an IO error naming the file, not junk. Readers on a read-only store keep working (F16).

**Proposed fix.** Workspace gets `holding` (False), set True by `locked()` for the duration of the lock. load_pending reads each pending file itself: FileNotFoundError -> skip; any other OSError -> raise when holding (IO naming the file), skip otherwise; the bytes are parsed by read_attempt (new optional `data` argument, so nothing is read twice); a parsed non-attempt is swept only when holding. load_pending keeps its one-argument signature because tests/test_storage.py's pause-view driver replaces it with `def load_pending(ws)`. Deliberate behaviour change: an unreadable pending attempt now makes sync/claim fail with IO naming the file (before, it was silently swept with its receipt and the retry failed); plain local writes are unaffected (Tx.state does not call load_pending). Not changed, noted: settle_commands (1812-1816) still unlinks a receipt it cannot read under the lock; readers silently omit an unreadable attempt from `info`'s pending_ownership.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G28_OnlyTheLockHolderSweepsPending` (4 tests). On e9eed92: Ran 4 tests, FAILED (failures=9): 'Tuples differ: (False, False) != (True, True) : sync (exit 0) swept away an accepted attempt it could not read, and its receipt'; same tuple ': a read-only command deleted an accepted attempt it could not read, and its receipt' (subtests update --claim --status closed, release); 'False is not true : a reader (show, no lock) deleted the receipt the locked sync kept'; five subtests '1 != 0 : {"error": {"code": "IO", "message": "Permission denied: .../pending/01900000-0000-7000-8000-0000000000aa.json"}}'. With the diff: G28.diff alone: Ran 4 tests in 11.670s OK; all five pending-io diffs: Ran 4 tests in 12.209s OK.

**Shipped suites with the diff.** All five pending-io diffs (G12, G28, G29, G37, G53) applied together to one copy (work-pending-io/all/bead; they apply cleanly in either order with an identical result, no conflicts among them), run from the repo root with BEAD_UNDER_TEST=<that copy> and scratch BEAD_HOME/TMPDIR: tests.test_protocol 'Ran 54 tests in 211.758s OK'; tests.test_commands 'Ran 97 tests in 280.893s OK' (includes test_f12_unreadable_pending_file_is_not_fenced_forever, test_f13_local_state_files_are_strict_json, test_f16_read_only_store_write_is_a_structured_error); tests.test_storage 'Ran 9 tests in 26.985s OK' (its pause-view driver replaces load_pending with a one-argument function, which is why G28 keeps that signature); tests.test_replay 'Ran 60 tests in 214.925s OK'. tests.test_stress (randomised 6-machine models, 2400 sequential and 3000 concurrent commands with readers racing writers) 'Ran 2 tests in 1046.906s OK'. The lead's LeadClose tests (test_lead.py, 3 tests) pass on the combined copy and all fail on pristine. Sweep (tests/test_review7.py run_schedule): the 24 close/delete x crash-before-push schedules, which include the 10 G12_SCHEDULES, 0 failures on the combined copy; the 10 G12_SCHEDULES on pristine: 10 failures ('UNPUBLISHED R close ... -> rc 0 (json carries no warning): remote status in_progress, local status closed'). After G12 lands, Sweep.test_sweep's comment and G12_SCHEDULES ('…

#### G29 · Medium · robustness — An OSError after the remote accepted (meta.json write, or the publish of an owner's close) is reported as IO 'nothing half-done', with the receipt already dropped

**Mechanism.** coordinated (bead:1874-1940): on an accepted push it runs confirm_ownership (1931), drop_pending (1932, removes pending .json AND the .command receipt), then ws.save_meta (1933). The receipt branch drops at 1895 then saves meta at 1896; the recognised-retry branch saves meta at 1900 after settle_commands already unlinked the receipt (1824). save_meta (150-152) raises OSError on a full volume; run() (2869) turns it into IO exit 1, which PRIME (2619) documents as 'nothing half-done'. The retry then rebuilds against the accepted state: release -> NOT_OWNER, claiming close -> VALIDATION 'is closed'. Separately, publish_soon (1943-1952) catches only BpError, so an OSError inside synchronise (merge's write_atomic of a pulled segment on a full volume, or its final save_meta at 1868) escapes from transition (2285) / cmd_update (2258) after the close was recorded locally: exit 1 IO instead of 'recorded locally, not yet published'.

**Reproduction.** Wrapper (runpy) makes write_atomic raise ENOSPC for meta.json, or for another replica's *.jsonl. (1) a: create X, sync, claim X; `release X --json` with the meta fault; an observer syncs; retry `release X`. Same with `update Y --claim --status closed`. (2) `BEAD_TEST_CRASH_AFTER_PUSH=1 release X` (exit 99), then the retry with the meta fault, then another retry. (3) a claims X; b creates an issue and syncs; `close X -r done` with the foreign-segment fault.

**Observed.** Pristine: (1) 'release took effect on the remote, yet bead answered {"error": {"code": "IO", "message": "No space left on device: .../home-a/<ws>/meta.json"}}; its retry then answered (1, '{"error": {"code": "NOT_OWNER", "message": "gf-f6ir is not claimed by codex:a-thread-0001@b4eec061"}}')'; update --claim --status closed: IO, retry '{"error": {"code": "VALIDATION", "message": "gf-idtq is closed"}}'. (2) the recognised retry answers IO, the next retry NOT_OWNER. (3) 'Error: No space left on device: .../events/7ae9c50b/000001.jsonl' exit 1 while a's local status is closed. Source 37 also reproduced (3) on a real 16 MB HFS+ volume: 'exit=1 {"error": {"code": "IO", "message": "[Errno 28] No space left on device"}}', local closed, remote in_progress.

**Expected.** Once the remote has accepted, bookkeeping (meta.json's last_sync) cannot fail the command; once the close is recorded locally, a failed publish is reported as 'recorded locally, not yet published (IO: ...)', exit 0, like any other failed publish.

**Proposed fix.** Workspace.note_sync(remote, **values) writes meta best-effort (suppresses OSError; only last_sync is left behind). synchronise (keeping name=ws.name) and the three post-acceptance save_meta calls in coordinated use it. publish_soon also catches OSError and returns 'recorded locally, not yet published (IO: <strerror>)'. Not changed, noted: an OSError from confirm_ownership itself (1931) still reports IO, but there the pending attempt and receipt survive, so the retry is recognised. With --json the publish_soon note is dropped (G37), so G29 and G37 should land together.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G29_FailureAfterAcceptanceIsNotNothingHalfDone` (3 tests). On e9eed92: Ran 3 tests, FAILED (failures=4): '1 != 0 : release took effect on the remote, yet bead answered {"error": {"code": "IO", ...meta.json"}}; its retry then answered (1, ...NOT_OWNER...)'; the same for update --claim --status closed (retry VALIDATION 'is closed'); '1 != 0 : the retry recognised the accepted release, yet answered {"error": {"code": "IO", ...}}'; 'Tuples differ: (1, False) != (0, True) : the close was recorded, yet bead answered: Error: No space left on device: .../events/<replica>/000001.jsonl'. With the diff: G29.diff alone: Ran 3 tests in 9.046s OK; all five pending-io diffs: Ran 3 tests in 11.206s OK.

**Shipped suites with the diff.** All five pending-io diffs (G12, G28, G29, G37, G53) applied together to one copy (work-pending-io/all/bead; they apply cleanly in either order with an identical result, no conflicts among them), run from the repo root with BEAD_UNDER_TEST=<that copy> and scratch BEAD_HOME/TMPDIR: tests.test_protocol 'Ran 54 tests in 211.758s OK'; tests.test_commands 'Ran 97 tests in 280.893s OK' (includes test_f12_unreadable_pending_file_is_not_fenced_forever, test_f13_local_state_files_are_strict_json, test_f16_read_only_store_write_is_a_structured_error); tests.test_storage 'Ran 9 tests in 26.985s OK' (its pause-view driver replaces load_pending with a one-argument function, which is why G28 keeps that signature); tests.test_replay 'Ran 60 tests in 214.925s OK'. tests.test_stress (randomised 6-machine models, 2400 sequential and 3000 concurrent commands with readers racing writers) 'Ran 2 tests in 1046.906s OK'. The lead's LeadClose tests (test_lead.py, 3 tests) pass on the combined copy and all fail on pristine. #### G09 · Medium · robustness — `for receipt in stale` (bead 1910) rebinds the in-flight `receipt` tuple to a Path, so a coordinated command whose push loses an ordinary race after a settled receipt crashes with TypeError instead of retrying

**Mechanism.** coordinated sets `receipt = None` at 1907, then `for receipt in stale: receipt.unlink(...)` (1910-1911) leaves `receipt` bound to the last stale PosixPath. On outcome "rejected" (1935-1936) nothing reassigns it (only "uncertain" does, 1938). After the sleep (1939), round 2 evaluates `if receipt and attempt_path(ws, receipt[0]).exists()` (1894): TypeError: 'PosixPath' object is not subscriptable. run() catches only BpError and OSError (2867-2870), so the user gets a traceback with exit 1 and the claim is never made. It needs a settled receipt of this actor (any lost acknowledgement later settled by sync or a merge) and a push that loses an ordinary publish race; claim, takeover, release and update --claim all go through coordinated.

**Reproduction.** tests.support: a: init, create X, Y, sync; b: sync; a: `claim X` with BEAD_TEST_CRASH_AFTER_PUSH=1 (rc 99); a: `sync` (one settled .command left). In-process (support.load_module, a's env and checkout): `bp.run(['claim', Y, '--json'])` with Link.publish wrapped so that before A's first 'claim' push machine b runs `create` and `sync` (A's push becomes a non-fast-forward, outcome "rejected"). Test: final/G09.py.

**Observed.** AssertionError: claim crashed after an ordinary refused push: TypeError: 'PosixPath' object is not subscriptable (raised at bead line 1894 in coordinated)

**Expected.** A refused push means another round: `claim Y` succeeds on round 2 (rc 0), as it does when the actor has no settled receipt.

**Proposed fix.** Rename the loop variable (`for old in stale: old.unlink(...)`) so the in-flight `receipt` is never rebound. This is the minimal fix for the crash alone; it leaves G08 open (the stale receipts are still dropped before the push). G08's diff removes the loop entirely and subsumes this hunk: G08.diff and G09.diff conflict textually (same two lines), and receipts-combined.diff carries G08's version. The G09 test passes on the G08 copy as well.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G09_ReceiptLoopVariableShadowsTheInFlightReceipt`. On e9eed92: FAILED (failures=1): AssertionError: claim crashed after an ordinary refused push: TypeError: 'PosixPath' object is not subscriptable With the diff: Ran 1 test ... OK (G09 copy); also OK on the G08 copy and the combined copy.

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Against the G09 copy: test_protocol 'Ran 54 tests in 185.009s OK' (rerun). Two earlier attempts under load average 120-170 did not pass, both in timing-dependent shipped tests that cannot reach the renamed loop (no settled receipt exists in them): the first hung in Protocol3.test_recovered_atomic_update_is_not_applied_again_after_a_later_observed_edit (its mocked global time.sleep ran `bead sync` while the test process held the store lock; I killed my own run), the second failed ActualTransportReview.test_timed_out_accepted_release_returns_success ('actual receive-pack did not finish', BEAD_NET_TIMEOUT=0.1). That test then passed 5/5 on the G09 copy and 5/5 on e9eed92 run singly. No shipped test needs adapting. Suites were run from an unchanged scratch copy of the repo's tests/ (work-receipts/suite/tests), cwd in scratch, with BEAD_UNDER_TEST set to the patched copy and BEAD_HOME/TMPDIR under work-receipts/. With all six receipts diffs applied together (receipts-combined.diff, work-receipts/combined/bead): test_protocol 'Ran 54 tests in 130.036s OK', test_commands 'Ran 97 tests in 196.742s OK', test_storage 'Ran 9 tests in 13.007s OK', test_replay 'Ran 60 tests in 52.644s OK'; all six G test files run together as one module: 12 failures on e9eed92, 'Ran 12 tests ... OK' on the combined copy.

#### G64 · Medium · robustness — Once this machine's own journal has a chain break, every later local write is inert: create dies with a KeyError traceback, and update/comments/close exit 0, append an event and change nothing

**Mechanism.** replay (bead:906-931) marks an event inert when it does not build on its predecessor in its own journal, and does not advance seen[replica] for it; every later own event depends on that one through the frontier, so it fails the same check (bead:921) and is inert too. writing() (bead:1235-1244) never looks at chain_breaks, so writes go on being appended. cmd_create then reads tx.state.issues[uid] (bead:2079) for an issue replay never created: KeyError, uncaught (run() catches BpError and OSError only, bead:2867-2870). Triggers: a copied machine identity (the designed JOURNAL_FORK case; sync refuses, local writes are not refused), or a write made while the own journal directory could not be listed (G52).

**Reproduction.** Machine a: init, create 'before the break'. Append to a's own segment a copy of its last event with a new id and deps [] (an event that does not build on its predecessor). Then `bead create 'after the break' --json`, and `bead update <key> --title renamed --json`.

**Observed.** create: Traceback ... line 2079, in cmd_create issue = tx.state.issues[uid] KeyError: '01a11852-968a-72cd-992c-d9bb619ffa92'. update: exit 0, title unchanged.

**Expected.** A write on a machine whose own journal no longer forms one chain fails with JOURNAL_FORK (as sync already does) and appends nothing.

**Proposed fix.** writing() refuses with JOURNAL_FORK when tx.replica is in tx.state.chain_breaks, before anything is appended. (coordinated() already reaches the same check through Link.merge.)

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G64_WritesOnABrokenOwnChain`. On e9eed92: AssertionError: 'Traceback' unexpectedly found in 'Traceback ... line 2079, in cmd_create ... KeyError: ...' : create on a broken own chain crashed With the diff: Ran 1 test OK

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Each alone and G63+G64+G65 applied together (patch -p1, no conflicts), run from the repo root with scratch BEAD_HOME/TMPDIR: G63+G64: tests.test_replay Ran 60 OK, tests.test_storage Ran 9 OK, tests.test_protocol Ran 54 OK, tests.test_commands Ran 97 OK; G63+G64+G65: tests.test_protocol Ran 54 OK, tests.test_commands Ran 97 OK. No shipped test needs adapting.

<details><summary>Proposed diff (G64, applies to e9eed92 with <code>patch -p1</code>)</summary>

```diff
--- a/bead
+++ b/bead
@@ -1237,6 +1237,9 @@
     """A local write. Ownership events made here (offline or with no remote) need no confirmation."""
     with locked(ws):
         tx = Tx(ws, actor)
+        if tx.replica in tx.state.chain_breaks:  # everything written now would be inert
+            raise BpError("JOURNAL_FORK", "this machine's journal no longer forms one chain: the same machine "
+                          "identity has written from two places. Give one of them a new identity.")
         yield tx
         if tx.pending:
             append_events(ws, tx.replica, tx.pending)
```
</details>

#### G30 · Medium · robustness — Deeply nested JSON is accepted on import and emit and bricks Python 3.12/3.13 machines on replay, and one line nested deeper than json can parse is an unscoped RecursionError anywhere bead reads JSON

**Mechanism.** Write side: import_records (bead:1987-2035) checks record shape but never nesting. Unknown fields go verbatim into the create event's `extra` (bead:2012-2013, probe 2020-2024, tx.emit 2025), and dependency `metadata` goes into dep_add `meta` (2027-2033). Tx.emit (bead:1196-1216) checks only event_problem/payload_problem and UTF-8 encodability. Python 3.14's json parses about 116,000 levels; 3.12 and 3.13 (README: Python 3.12+) parse only about 10,000. So a 3.14 machine publishes an event that every 3.12/3.13 machine fails to read. Read side: loads_strict (bead:68-70) is plain json.loads, and json raises RecursionError, which is not a ValueError. Every reader catches only ValueError: read_segment (bead:370-371), read_attempt (447-449), complete_prefix (389-391; json.loads), the import parse (1990-1991), find_workspace's .beadroll.json (201-203) and Link.fetch's manifest (1636-1638; json.loads, catches ValueError/AttributeError). run() (bead:2855-2870) catches only BpError and OSError, and help/prime catch only BpError around find_workspace (2857-2861). So a single too-deep line in any segment, attempt file, torn tail, manifest or tracked config is a traceback on every command that reads it, instead of the scoped 'line is not valid JSON' report. An append-only segment is never rewritten, so this lasts for good (verifier: `delete` on the writer does not help).

**Reproduction.** (1) Any interpreter: after `bead init --prefix ex; bead create x`, write '['*1_000_000 + ']'*1_000_000 + '\n' to $BEAD_HOME/<ws>/events/fffffff0/000001.jsonl, then run `bead list --json`. (2) Mixed crew: machine a (python3 = 3.14.7) runs `bead import deep.jsonl`, a record whose unknown field nests 12,000 arrays, then `bead sync`. Machine b runs the same bead under uv cpython 3.12.10 (`python3.12 bead sync --json`). (3) Planted on the bare remote: a commit on refs/beads/journal whose manifest.json nests 1,000,000 deep, then `bead sync`. (4) A committed .beadroll.json nested 1,000,000 deep, then `bead list` / `bead help`. All of these are in G30.py.

**Observed.** e9eed92: `bead list` -> rc 1, traceback ending `RecursionError: Stack overflow (used 16352 kB) while decoding a JSON array from a unicode string` (read_segment bead:370 -> loads_strict bead:70). `bead import` of a 20,000-deep (and a 200-deep) record -> rc 0 `{"imported": 1}`. b on 3.12 after a's import+sync -> `sync` rc 1 `RecursionError: maximum recursion depth exceeded while decoding a JSON array from a unicode string`, and the same for every later command. `sync` against the deep manifest -> traceback (RecursionError) instead of MANIFEST_MISSING. A deep .beadroll.json -> list and help both traceback.

**Expected.** bead never writes an event that a supported Python (or another machine) cannot read back: nesting is bounded on the write path and an import over the bound fails whole with VALIDATION. A line or file too deep for this interpreter is one unreadable line (reported in `info` integrity), a torn tail, MANIFEST_MISSING or CONFIG, never a traceback.

**Proposed fix.** (a) loads_strict turns RecursionError into ValueError ('nested deeper than this Python can parse'), so every existing `except ValueError` reader scopes it. That covers read_segment, read_attempt, the import parse, .beadroll.json, meta/replica/aliases files and receipts. complete_prefix and the Link.fetch manifest parse (plain json.loads) also catch RecursionError. (b) MAX_NESTING = 100 plus an iterative too_deep(). Tx.emit refuses any event nested deeper than 100 with VALIDATION, which covers dep_add metadata. import_records refuses a fresh record nested deeper than 98 (its create event holds it two levels down), naming the record, before anything is written. Real exports nest at most 3 deep (verifier 14: 2,662 records). Residual, by design: a deep event that an unpatched 3.14 bead already published is still unreadable on 3.12/3.13. With the fix it is reported, not a crash, but that machine's later events then wait on it there. Re-run: b (patched, 3.12) gives `list` rc 0 but misses a's later issue, with waiting_events 1 and integrity ['line is not valid JSON']. Recovering that would need parsing the line, so it is out of scope. Making the read bound deterministic (the verifier-15 alternative) was rejected: it would make events of depth 101..~10,000, which every version reads today, inert, and break their writer's chain on upgraded machines.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G30_DeepJsonIsBoundedAndScoped` (4 tests). On e9eed92: FAILED (failures=7): `AssertionError: bead list with a deep segment line crashed: RecursionError: Stack overflow (used 16352 kB) while decoding a JSON array from a unicode string`; `AssertionError: 0 == 0 : import of deep-20000 was accepted: {"imported": 1}` (also deep-200 and deep-dep-metadata); `AssertionError: sync against a deep manifest crashed: RecursionError: Stack overflow ...`; `AssertionError: .../python3.12: sync after a's import crashed: RecursionError: maximum recursion depth exceeded while decoding a JSON array from a unicode string`. With bead itself run on 3.12 (PATH shim): FA… With the diff: Ran 4 tests OK (bead on python3 3.14.7). With bead on 3.12.10 through a PATH shim: Ran 4 tests OK (skipped=1: the cross-version test needs a writer deeper than the reader).

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Run from the repo root with BEAD_UNDER_TEST=<scratch>/tree-G30/bead (the patched copy, with infra/compat_real.py copied beside it so test_compat's real-export test runs) and a scratch BEAD_HOME/TMPDIR: tests.test_replay: Ran 60 tests OK; tests.test_storage: Ran 9 tests OK; tests.test_compat: Ran 3 tests OK (real exports imported, round-tripped, br/bv); tests.test_commands: Ran 97 tests OK; tests.test_protocol: Ran 54 tests OK. No shipped test needs adapting.

<details><summary>Proposed diff (G30, applies to e9eed92 with <code>patch -p1</code>)</summary>

```diff
--- a/bead
+++ b/bead
@@ -66,8 +66,27 @@
 
 
 def loads_strict(text):
-    """JSON as the standard defines it: NaN and Infinity are refused, not smuggled into exports."""
-    return json.loads(text, parse_constant=_reject_constant)
+    """JSON as the standard defines it: NaN and Infinity are refused, not smuggled into exports.
+    Nesting deeper than this Python can parse is unreadable JSON like any other, not a crash."""
+    try:
+        return json.loads(text, parse_constant=_reject_constant)
+    except RecursionError:
+        raise ValueError("nested deeper than this Python can parse") from None
+
+
+MAX_NESTING = 100  # arrays and objects in one event; json on Python 3.12/3.13 parses only about 10,000
+
+
+def too_deep(value, limit: int = MAX_NESTING) -> bool:
+    """Whether a JSON value nests arrays or objects more than `limit` deep. Iterative: safe on any input."""
+    stack = [(value, 1)]
+    while stack:
+        item, depth = stack.pop()
+        if isinstance(item, (dict, list)):
+            if depth > limit:
+                return True
+            stack.extend((child, depth + 1) for child in (item.values() if isinstance(item, dict) else item))
+    return False
 
 
 def home() -> Path:
@@ -388,7 +407,7 @@
     try:
         json.loads(data[cut:])
         return data + b"\n"
-    except ValueError:
+    except (ValueError, RecursionError):
         return data[:cut]
 
 
@@ -1202,6 +1221,8 @@
         if problem:  # the same rules replay applies, before the event is minted
             raise BpError("VALIDATION", "the clock is beyond the year 2100; fix it before writing"
                           if "timestamp" in problem else problem)
+        if too_deep(event):  # every supported Python must be able to read back what any machine writes
+            raise BpError("VALIDATION", f"data nests more than {MAX_NESTING} levels deep")
         try:
             encode_events([event])
         except UnicodeEncodeError:
@@ -1635,7 +1656,7 @@
         try:
             parsed = json.loads(manifest.stdout)
             found = parsed.get("workspace")
-        except (ValueError, AttributeError):
+        except (ValueError, AttributeError, RecursionError):
             raise BpError("MANIFEST_MISSING", f"{self.ref} on {self.url} has an unreadable manifest") from None
         if found != self.ws.id:
             raise BpError("WORKSPACE_MISMATCH", f"{self.ref} on {self.url} belongs to workspace {found}, not {self.ws.id}")
@@ -2015,6 +2036,9 @@
                     if isinstance(d, dict) and isinstance(d.get("depends_on_id"), str) and d["depends_on_id"] not in all_ids]

+            raise BpError("VALIDATION", f"record {rec['id']!r} cannot be imported: it nests more than "
```

#### G34 · Medium · robustness — Non-UTF-8 bytes in git/ps output (branch name, origin URL, origin/HEAD target, ls-tree path, parent argv) turn ordinary commands, help and prime included, into UnicodeDecodeError tracebacks

**Mechanism.** Strict decodes of OS bytes: find_workspace decodes remote.origin.url (bead:206) and hashes it with a strict encode (212); --show-toplevel (197) and --git-common-dir (217) are decoded the same way. Workspace.remote decodes `remote get-url` (171). capture_where decodes the branch (1168) and runs in every Tx. merged_into_default decodes the origin/HEAD target (1460) and runs on show of a closed issue. git_text (104) decodes `ls-tree -r` for Link.merge (1673-1674); with core.quotePath=false git prints non-UTF-8 names raw. nearest_harness reads `ps` with text=True (288), a strict locale decode, when both harness variables are set. UnicodeDecodeError is not a BpError or OSError, so run() (bead:2867-2870) lets it escape. help/prime/onboard wrap find_workspace in `except BpError` only (2857-2861). One Latin-1 byte in the origin URL therefore breaks every command in the checkout, help included, and BEAD_WORKSPACE does not help because the decode runs first. The reporter's diff missed case (b): its surrogateescape-decoded origin still crashed at the strict encode on bead:212. Its git_text errors='replace' change also made Link.merge pull a foreign `events/caf\xe9/` folder under the invented name `events/caf\ufffd/` and republish it (verifier 17).

**Reproduction.** (a) packed-refs `refs/heads/caf\xe9` + `git symbolic-ref HEAD refs/heads/caf\xe9`; `bead create x`. (b) `git config remote.origin.url $'https://example.invalid/caf\xe9.git'`; `bead help` / `prime` / `init` / `create` / `info`. (c) close an issue, `git symbolic-ref refs/remotes/origin/HEAD refs/remotes/origin/caf\xe9`, `bead show KEY`. (d) plant events/caf\xe9/1.jsonl on refs/beads/journal of the local bare remote, then `bead sync` with GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL setting core.quotePath=false (a scratch file). (e) CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID and CODEX_THREAD_ID both set, no BEAD_AGENT, run `sh -c '"$0" "$1" --json; :' bead help $'r\xe9sum\xe9.txt'`.

**Observed.** e9eed92, each rc 1 with a traceback: (a) `UnicodeDecodeError: 'utf-8' codec can't decode byte 0xe9 in position 3` (capture_where); (b) help, prime, init, create and info all `... byte 0xe9 in position 27` (find_workspace); (c) `... position 23` (merged_into_default); (d) `... position 145` (git_text via Link.merge); (e) `... position 161` (nearest_harness / subprocess text decode). The ASCII-argv control in (e) passes.

**Expected.** Bytes from git or ps that are not UTF-8 never produce a traceback. Bytes that name something (the URL, refs, paths) are kept exactly for git and hashing. Display text gets U+FFFD. A non-UTF-8 branch is not recorded in events. What merge sees does not depend on core.quotePath.

**Proposed fix.** git_text, --show-toplevel, --git-common-dir and the origin/HEAD name decode with os.fsdecode (encoded back with os.fsencode for the local id). The origin URL and `remote get-url` decode with utf-8/surrogateescape, so git gets the exact bytes back, and the workspace id hashes them with surrogateescape (unchanged for UTF-8 URLs). The workspace display name goes through a new shown(), which turns undecodable bytes into U+FFFD, so the init event still encodes (F07). capture_where records no branch when the name is not UTF-8. `ps` is read with errors='replace'. Link.merge reads `ls-tree -r -z` as bytes and skips names that are not UTF-8 (bead never writes one), so core.quotePath no longer changes the result.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G34_NonUtf8GitAndPsOutputIsNotATraceback` (5 tests). On e9eed92: FAILED (failures=10): `AssertionError: create on branch caf\xe9 crashed: UnicodeDecodeError: 'utf-8' codec can't decode byte 0xe9 in position 3: invalid continuation byte`; `AssertionError: bead help crashed: UnicodeDecodeError: ... position 27` (likewise prime/init/create/info); `AssertionError: show of a closed issue crashed: ... position 23`; `AssertionError: sync with core.quotePath=false crashed: ... position 145`; `AssertionError: help under a parent whose argv holds b'r\xe9sum\xe9.txt' crashed: ... position 161`. With the diff: Ran 5 tests OK.

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Run from the repo root with BEAD_UNDER_TEST=<scratch>/tree-G34/bead (the patched copy, with infra/compat_real.py copied beside it so test_compat's real-export test runs) and a scratch BEAD_HOME/TMPDIR: tests.test_storage: Ran 9 tests OK; tests.test_protocol: Ran 54 tests OK; tests.test_commands: Ran 97 tests OK. No shipped test needs adapting.

```diff
--- a/bead
+++ b/bead
@@ -101,9 +101,14 @@

-    return run_git(args, **kwargs).stdout.decode().strip()
+    return os.fsdecode(run_git(args, **kwargs).stdout.strip())
 
 
+def shown(text: str) -> str:

+    return text.encode("utf-8", "surrogateescape").decode("utf-8", "replace")
```

#### G35 · Medium · robustness — BEAD_HOME is used verbatim: an unexpanded `~/...` (as settings files and service definitions pass it) or a relative path puts the whole store (journal, lock, meta, machine identity) inside the checkout, and each subdirectory sees a different, empty store

**Mechanism.** home() bead:73-74 `return Path(os.environ.get("BEAD_HOME") or ...)` has no expanduser and no absolute-path check. Every store path is then relative to the process cwd: Workspace.dir (140), replica_id (244/248), locked (1221), cmd_machines (2535) and Link.git_dir (1602, handed to git as GIT_DIR). Commands run in the checkout write `~/...` or `state/...` there; from src/ the same relative path names another, empty store, so readers say NOT_INITIALIZED. This breaks bead:4, README:14-16 and REVIEWING §1 ('Nothing is ever written inside a checkout'); bead's own output prints the unexpanded `~/.beads/...` path, hiding where the store really is. A routine `git clean -fd` deletes the store with its machine identity (seen in probe.py: the next run minted a new machine id), and `git add -A` commits it.

**Reproduction.** Sandbox clone with an empty src/; with BEAD_HOME='~/.beads' and HOME=<fake>: `bead init --prefix ex --json`, `bead create 'from top' --json` at the top, `bead list --json` from src/, `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all`; again with BEAD_HOME='state/beads'. Script: work-store-files/probe.py g35; test: G35.py.

**Observed.** == BEAD_HOME='~/.beads' / init at top: rc 0 {"workspace": "80aacc7c69a92c69", "prefix": "ex", "path": "~/.beads/80aacc7c69a92c69", ...} / create at top: rc 0 / list from src/: rc 1 {"error": {"code": "NOT_INITIALIZED", "message": "no tracker for this repository yet: run `bead init` or `bead sync`"}} / git status: ['?? ~/.beads/80aacc7c69a92c69/events/f27305e7/000001.jsonl', '?? ~/.beads/80aacc7c69a92c69/lock', '?? ~/.beads/80aacc7c69a92c69/meta.json', '?? ~/.beads/replica.json'] / fake HOME contents: [] == BEAD_HOME='state/beads' / init rc 0 "path": "state/beads/80aacc7c69a92c69" / list from src/: rc 1 NOT_INITIALIZED / git status: ['?? state/beads/80aacc7c69a92c69/events/e95579cb/000001.jsonl', '?? state/beads/80aacc7c69a92c69/lock', '?? state/beads/80aacc7c69a92c69/meta.json', '?? state/beads/replica.json'].

**Expected.** `~` expands to the user's home; a relative BEAD_HOME is refused with a structured CONFIG error; nothing is written inside the checkout and every subdirectory sees the same store.

**Proposed fix.** home() expands `~` (os.path.expanduser) and raises BpError CONFIG for a non-absolute value; an empty value still means the default. Patched: `~/.beads` -> store at <fake HOME>/.beads, `list` from src/ rc 0 with the issue, git status []; 'state/beads' -> rc 1 {"error": {"code": "CONFIG", "message": "BEAD_HOME must be an absolute path, not 'state/beads': the store never lives in a checkout"}} for init/create/list, nothing written. `--help` still works (rc 0); `prime` now fails with that CONFIG error instead of printing context (it calls replica_id outside its own try), which is the right answer for a store it cannot locate. `~nosuchuser/x` stays unexpanded and is refused.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G35_StoreNeverLandsInTheCheckout` (2 tests). On e9eed92: FAIL test_g35_tilde_bead_home_means_the_users_home: `AssertionError: Lists differ: ['?? ~/beads/a1009ee7c38cedd1/events/de436[117 chars]son'] != []` : the store was written inside the checkout; FAIL test_g35_relative_bead_home_never_writes_into_the_checkout: `AssertionError: Lists differ: ['?? state/beads/4ea4270ea2c9c80c/events/0[133 chars]son'] != []`. Ran 2 tests, FAILED (failures=2). With the diff: Ran 2 tests in 0.658s OK (G35 alone); OK on the combined copy. Checker: pristine 2 failures and patched OK reproduced; with BEAD_HOME=state/beads the patched copy gives rc 0 for --help/help/onboard and CONFIG for prime/info/machines, as stated.

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Against this diff alone: G35 test_storage rc=0 19s: Ran 9 tests in 18.432s OK ; G35 test_commands rc=0 227s: Ran 97 tests in 227.578s OK . Combined copy (all six store-files diffs, applied in order G04 G35 G47 G48 G50 G52 with `git apply`): ALL test_storage rc=0 24s: Ran 9 tests in 22.895s OK ; ALL test_protocol rc=0 212s: Ran 54 tests in 211.977s OK ; ALL test_commands rc=0 304s: Ran 97 tests in 304.071s OK ; ALL test_replay rc=0 114s: Ran 60 tests in 113.549s OK . No shipped test needed adapting. Not run: test_compat (its real-exports test looks for infra/compat_real.py beside BEAD_UNDER_TEST and reads the owner's real tracker exports) and test_stress (slow models). No shipped test uses a relative or `~` BEAD_HOME. Independent check (check-store-files, Python 3.14.7, git 2.55.0): all six diffs apply with `patch -p1` to fresh pristine copies, singly and combined in either order (G04..G52 and G52..G04 give identical files). Combined copy (with the revised G47.diff): test_storage Ran 9 OK; test_commands Ran 97 in 254s OK; test_replay Ran 60 in 161s OK; test_protocol Ran 54 OK when run alone (120s). In a parallel run at load average ~185, test_protocol had 1 failure, test_timed_out_accepted_release_returns_success (BEAD_NET_TIMEOUT=0.1 timing test, also seen flaking by the triage-33 verifier); that test passed 3/3 alone on the combined copy and 2/2 on pristine, so it is load, no…

<details><summary>Proposed diff (G35, applies to e9eed92 with <code>patch -p1</code>)</summary>

```diff
--- a/bead
+++ b/bead
@@ -71,7 +71,13 @@
 
 
 def home() -> Path:
-    return Path(os.environ.get("BEAD_HOME") or Path.home() / ".local" / "state" / "beadroll")
+    value = os.environ.get("BEAD_HOME")
+    if not value:
+        return Path.home() / ".local" / "state" / "beadroll"
+    path = Path(os.path.expanduser(value))  # settings files and service definitions pass `~` unexpanded
+    if not path.is_absolute():
+        raise BpError("CONFIG", f"BEAD_HOME must be an absolute path, not {value!r}: the store never lives in a checkout")
+    return path
 
 
 def run_git(args, *, env=None, data=None, check=True, timeout=None, reap=True):
```
</details>

#### G36 · Medium · robustness — A command whose terminal hung up (EIO on write) or that was started with stdout/stderr closed exits 120 or 1 with a Python traceback even after it committed, and a failed command loses its own status

**Mechanism.** Only EPIPE is treated as 'the reader went away'. main (bead:2818-2826) catches only BrokenPipeError; run's output block (bead:2871-2881) catches only BrokenPipeError (2880); the overview path prints and flushes with no handler (2852-2853); report_error (bead:2885-2894) catches only BrokenPipeError (2892). (1) A write to a PTY slave whose master has closed fails with EIO, a plain OSError: after the command has committed, print (2876/2878) raises it, a traceback is printed, and interpreter shutdown fails to flush stdout again, so the status is 120. cmd_export writes inside the command (bead:2050), so its EIO is caught by run's `except OSError` (2869) and reported as IO, then shutdown fails the flush: 120. A failed command on a hung-up terminal (show of a missing id: 1; a USAGE error: 2) also ends 120. `CMD --help`: argparse swallows the write error and raises SystemExit, and the shutdown flush then fails: 120 (the same happens on a closed pipe, EPIPE, an E07 residual). (2) Started with fd 1 or 2 closed (`>&-`), Python sets sys.stdout or sys.stderr to None: print() silently does nothing and the explicit sys.stdout.flush() (2873/2879) or sys.stderr.flush() (2891) raises AttributeError: traceback, exit 1 after a committed create, and a USAGE error's 2 becomes 1, with print(file=None) sending the error text to stdout. With a controlling terminal and default SIGHUP the process is killed quietly instead, so the EIO route shows when bead is not the terminal's session leader or ignores SIGHUP (a PTY handed to a child by a harness, setsid, nohup). Section 2 decides 'a closed stdout exits 0 quietly' and F06 that a failed command keeps its status; both are implemented for EPIPE only.

**Reproduction.** In a support.Sandbox checkout: master, slave = os.openpty(); os.close(master); subprocess.run([BEAD, 'create', 'x'], stdout=slave, stderr=PIPE) -> rc 120 and the issue exists. Same for list, list --json, the overview (no args), export, create --help; show ex-nope and no-such-command with stdout and stderr on the slave -> 120. Closed descriptors: sh -c 'exec bead create x >&-' -> rc 1, AttributeError traceback, issue exists; sh -c 'exec bead no-such-command 2>&-' -> rc 1 with the error text on stdout. Test: G36.py.

**Observed.** pristine e9eed92, Python 3.14.7: create on the hung-up PTY: 'exit 120, stderr ends: ...line 2878, in run\n print("\\n".join(visible(line) for line in lines))\n ... OSError: [Errno 5] Input/output error\nException ignored while flushing sys.stdout:\nOSError: [Errno 5] Input/output error\n' (the issue was created). export: 'exit 120, stderr ends: Error: [Errno 5] Input/output error\nException ignored while flushing sys.stdout:\nOSError: [Errno 5] Input/output error'. create --help: 'exit 120 ... Exception ignored while flushing sys.stdout: OSError: [Errno 5]'. `bead show ex-nope` on a hung-up terminal exited 120, not its own status 1; `bead no-such-command` exited 120, not 2. stdout closed: 'exit 1 ... line 2879, in run\n sys.stdout.flush()\n ... AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'flush''. stderr closed: usage error exit 1 with stdout b"Error: argument command: invalid choice: 'no-such-command' ...". Closed pipe control: `create --help` into a closed pipe: 120 with 'Exception ignored while flushing sys.stdout: BrokenPipeError'; list/create into a closed pipe: 0 (E07 works).

**Expected.** As decided for a closed pipe (section 2, E07) and F06: a command whose reader went away, through a hung-up terminal or a descriptor closed at exec, exits 0 without Python noise once it has done its work, and a failed command keeps its own status (1, or 2 for USAGE). A committed create must not exit non-zero, or a retry wrapper creates it twice. EIO from a regular file (failing disk) must stay a failure.

**Proposed fix.** main replaces a None sys.stdout/sys.stderr (descriptor closed at exec) with a /dev/null stream. New emit(text, stream) prints and flushes, converting OSError through new hung_up(error, stream): EIO on a character device (a hung-up terminal) becomes BrokenPipeError, anything else (EIO from a regular file, ENOSPC, ...) is re-raised unchanged. emit replaces the print/flush pairs in the overview path, run's output block (including the result-less flush) and report_error, so the existing BrokenPipeError handling (silence + status) applies. cmd_export converts its own stdout write error with hung_up. parse_args' SystemExit (COMMAND --help) is caught and the page delivered with emit(None) before returning its code, so a closed pipe or hung-up terminal there exits 0 too. Differences from the reporter's and verifier's diffs: the EIO conversion is limited to character devices (stat.S_ISCHR on fstat) instead of any stream (reporter: a failing USB disk would exit 0) or isatty() (verifier: on Linux isatty() on a hung-up tty can itself fail, since the hung-up tty's TCGETS ioctl returns EIO; not verified here, macOS only); export keeps sys.stdout.write and only wraps its error, so output bytes are unchanged and another group's export hunk is less likely to conflict. Adds `import stat`. Checked in-process: emit on a regular file whose write raises EIO -> OSError EIO; on the hung-up PTY slave -> BrokenPipeError; on a pipe whose write raises EIO -> OSError. Not covered (out of scope, unchanged): stdout to a file that cannot grow (EFBIG/ENOSPC) still ends 120.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G36_HungUpTerminalAndClosedDescriptors` (4 tests). On e9eed92: Ran 4 tests, FAILED (failures=10): e.g. "AssertionError: exit 120, stderr ends: '...line 2878, in run ... OSError: [Errno 5] Input/output error\nException ignored while flushing sys.stdout:...'", "AssertionError: `bead show ex-nope` on a hung-up terminal exited 120, not its own status 1", "AssertionError: exit 1, stderr ends: '...line 2879, in run\n sys.stdout.flush()...AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'flush''" With the diff: Ran 4 tests in 2.183s OK

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Run against one copy with all four output-group diffs applied (G36, G59, G60, G61 in that order; patch -p1 applied each cleanly, no conflicts), Python 3.14.7, from the repo root with BEAD_UNDER_TEST set to that copy and scratch BEAD_HOME/TMPDIR: tests.test_commands 'Ran 97 tests in 153.538s OK'; tests.test_protocol 'Ran 54 tests in 153.301s OK'; tests.test_storage 'Ran 9 tests in 23.329s OK'; tests.test_replay 'Ran 60 tests in 121.485s OK'. No shipped test needs adapting. The E07 closed-pipe test and the F06 closed-stderr test are in test_commands and pass. Extra probe: create --help into a closed pipe 120 (pristine) -> 0 (patched); list and create into a closed pipe stay 0.

<details><summary>Proposed diff (G36, applies to e9eed92 with <code>patch -p1</code>)</summary>

```diff
--- a/bead
+++ b/bead
@@ -27,6 +27,7 @@
 import re
 import secrets
 import socket
+import stat
 import subprocess
 import sys
 import time
@@ -2047,7 +2048,10 @@
     if args.output:
         write_atomic(Path(args.output), text.encode(), private=False)
         return {"exported": len(st.issues), "path": args.output}, [f"Exported {len(st.issues)} issues to {args.output}"]
-    sys.stdout.write(text)
+    try:
+        sys.stdout.write(text)
+    except OSError as error:
+        raise hung_up(error, sys.stdout) from None
     return None, None
 
 
@@ -2816,6 +2820,9 @@
 
 
 def main(argv=None) -> int:
+    for name in ("stdout", "stderr"):  # started with the descriptor closed (`>&-`): nobody is reading
+        if getattr(sys, name) is None:
+            setattr(sys, name, open(os.devnull, "w", encoding="utf-8"))
     for stream in (sys.stdout, sys.stderr):  # the output contract is UTF-8, whatever the locale says
         with contextlib.suppress(Exception):
             stream.reconfigure(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
@@ -2826,6 +2833,26 @@
         return 0
 
 
+def emit(text: str | None, stream=None):
+    """Print one block (or only flush, for None) to stdout or `stream`, the terminal's hang-up included."""
+    stream = stream or sys.stdout
+    try:
+        if text is not None:
+            print(text, file=stream)
+        stream.flush()
+    except OSError as error:
+        raise hung_up(error, stream) from None
+
+
+def hung_up(error: OSError, stream) -> OSError:
+    """A terminal that hung up fails with EIO where a closed pipe fails with EPIPE: both mean nobody is
+    reading, and both become BrokenPipeError. EIO from a file is a failed write and stays one."""
+    with contextlib.suppress(OSError, ValueError):
+        if error.errno == errno.EIO and stat.S_ISCHR(os.fstat(stream.fileno()).st_mode):
+            return BrokenPipeError(errno.EPIPE, "the terminal hung up")
+    return error
+
+
 def silence(stream):
     """Point a broken stream at /dev/null so that neither we nor the interpreter's shutdown try it again."""
     with contextlib.suppress(OSError, ValueError):
@@ -2849,11 +2876,14 @@
             del head[index]
             break
     if not head + tail or head + tail in (["-h"], ["--help"]):
-        print(json.dumps({"help": OVERVIEW}) if as_json else OVERVIEW.rstrip("\n"))
-        sys.stdout.flush()
+        emit(json.dumps({"help": OVERVIEW}) if as_json else OVERVIEW.rstrip("\n"))
         return 0
     try:
-        args = build_parser().parse_args(head + tail)
+        try:
+            args = build_parser().parse_args(head + tail)
+        except SystemExit as done:  # COMMAND --help: argparse wrote the page; deliver it like any output
+            emit(None)
+            return done.code or 0
         if getattr(args, "workspace_free", False):
             try:
                 ws = find_workspace()
@@ -2870,13 +2900,9 @@
         return report_error(BpError("IO", f"{error.strerror or error}: {error.filename}" if error.filename else str(error)), as_json)
     try:
         if result is None and lines is None:
-            sys.stdout.flush()
+            emit(None)
             return 0
-        if as_json:
-            print(json.dumps(result, ensure_ascii=False))
-        else:
-            print("\n".join(visible(line) for line in lines))
-        sys.stdout.flush()
+        emit(json.dumps(result, ensure_ascii=False) if as_json else "\n".join(visible(line) for line in lines))
     except BrokenPipeError:
         silence(sys.stdout)
     return 0
@@ -2884,11 +2910,8 @@
 
 def report_error(error: BpError, as_json: bool) -> int:
     try:
-        if as_json:
-            print(json.dumps({"error": {"code": error.code, "message": str(error)}}), file=sys.stderr)
-        else:
-            print(f"Error: {visible(str(error))}", file=sys.stderr)
-        sys.stderr.flush()
+        emit(json.dumps({"error": {"code": error.code, "message": str(error)}}) if as_json
+             else f"Error: {visible(str(error))}", sys.stderr)
     except BrokenPipeError:
         silence(sys.stderr)  # the status is the command's, not the pipe's
     return 2 if error.code == "USAGE" else 1
```

#### G31 · Medium · robustness — bead cannot import its own export (or a br export) when any text holds U+2028, U+2029 or U+0085: import splits records with str.splitlines()

**Mechanism.** cmd_export writes json.dumps(..., ensure_ascii=False) (bead:2046), so U+2028/U+2029/U+0085 stay raw inside JSON strings, which is valid JSON and valid JSONL; br writes them raw too. import_records reads `read_utf8(path, path).splitlines()` (bead:1990). str.splitlines() also breaks on U+2028, U+2029, U+0085, \x0b, \x0c and \x1c-\x1e, so one record becomes fragments and the whole import fails VALIDATION ('not valid JSONL: Unterminated string'). `init --import` uses the same function (bead:1974). Journals and attempts are split on b'\n' (bead:365, 443), so import is the only affected reader.

**Reproduction.** `bead create 'line\u2028sep'` (and 'para\u2029sep', 'nel\u0085x'), each with a description and a comment; `bead export -o f`; on a second machine `bead init --prefix zz; bead import f --json`. Also `bead init --prefix bx --import issues.jsonl` where issues.jsonl is a br-shaped record whose title holds a raw U+2028 (CRLF line ends, a blank line).

**Observed.** e9eed92: import rc 1 `{"error": {"code": "VALIDATION", "message": ".../export.jsonl is not valid JSONL: Unterminated string starting at: line 1 column 25 (char 24)"}}`; `init --import` rc 1 `... is not valid JSONL: Unterminated string starting at: line 1 column 27 (char 26)`. Nothing imported.

**Expected.** Records end at \n only; bead's own export (and br's) imports again with every title, description and comment intact. CRLF and blank lines keep working.

**Proposed fix.** import_records splits on "\n" instead of splitlines() (a trailing \r is JSON whitespace, blank rows are still skipped). Note (not changed): the shipped test helpers and infra/compat_real.py / infra/roundtrip.py also parse exports with splitlines(), so they could not have caught this. Link.merge's `ls-tree` parse uses splitlines() too, but G34 replaces that with -z.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G31_ExportWithUnicodeLineSeparatorsImportsAgain` (2 tests). On e9eed92: FAILED (failures=2): `AssertionError: 1 != 0 : bead could not import its own export: {"error": {"code": "VALIDATION", "message": ".../export.jsonl is not valid JSONL: Unterminated string starting at: line 1 column 25 (char 24)"}}` and `AssertionError: 1 != 0 : init --import refused a valid br export: ... Unterminated string starting at: line 1 column 27 (char 26)`. With the diff: Ran 2 tests OK.

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Run from the repo root with BEAD_UNDER_TEST=<scratch>/tree-G31/bead (the patched copy, with infra/compat_real.py copied beside it so test_compat's real-export test runs) and a scratch BEAD_HOME/TMPDIR: tests.test_commands: Ran 97 tests OK; tests.test_compat: Ran 3 tests OK (real exports). No shipped test needs adapting.

```diff
--- a/bead
+++ b/bead
@@ -1987,7 +1987,8 @@
 def import_records(tx: Tx, path: str) -> int:
     """Load a Beads JSONL export, keeping ids, timestamps, relationships and unknown fields."""
     try:
-        records = [loads_strict(row) for row in read_utf8(path, path).splitlines() if row.strip()]
+        # records end at "\n" only: U+2028, U+2029 and U+0085 are ordinary text inside a JSON string
+        records = [loads_strict(row) for row in read_utf8(path, path).split("\n") if row.strip()]

         raise BpError("VALIDATION", f"{path} is not valid JSONL: {error}") from None
     for number, rec in enumerate(records, 1):
```

#### G32 · Medium · robustness — Booleans and out-of-range integers are accepted for priority/estimated_minutes on import, create and update, so bead exports records br refuses

**Mechanism.** fields_problem (bead:529-542) checks `isinstance(value, FIELD_TYPES[name])` (bead:536), and isinstance(True, int) is True. The priority range check (538) passes True/False (they equal 1/0). estimated_minutes has no range at all, and `--estimate` is `type=int` (bead:2703). Tx.emit (bead:1201) and the import probe (2021) apply only these rules. export_records (bead:1553ff) re-emits the values verbatim. br 0.5.7 rejects the export: `estimated_minutes: cannot be negative`, `invalid type: boolean, expected i32`, `invalid value: integer 3000000000, expected i32`. After that failed import br cannot even list the workspace (verifier 19). `bead list` shows `[PTrue]`, and `list -p 1` matches it. The reporter's fix (tighten fields_problem) is wrong: fields_problem also runs in replay (payload_problem, bead:939) and read_attempt (bead:451). An upgraded machine would then drop issues and confirmed claim attempts that e9eed92 wrote. Verifier 19 got two confirmed owners that way, and the guard in G32.py catches it (`Lists differ: [] != ['ex-0', 'ex-1', 'ex-2']` against that patch).

**Reproduction.** `bead import` of records with priority:true, priority:false, estimated_minutes:false/-7/2147483648; `bead create e --estimate=-7` and `--estimate=2147483648`; `bead update KEY --estimate=-7` and `bead update KEY --claim --estimate=-7` (after a sync).

**Observed.** e9eed92: every case rc 0, e.g. `import of {'priority': True} was accepted: {"imported": 1}` and `create --estimate=-7 was accepted: {"id": "ex-nml6", ... "estimated_minutes": -7 ...}`. `update --claim --estimate=-7` claimed the issue with the bad estimate: `Tuples differ: (-7, 'in_progress') != (0, 'open')`.

**Expected.** A write (import, create, update, including an update made with a claim) refuses a JSON boolean where an integer is required, and an estimated_minutes outside 0..2147483647, with VALIDATION, refused whole. Histories already holding such values still replay identically on every version.

**Proposed fix.** New write_problem(event), used only where bead mints events: in Tx.emit (beside event_problem/payload_problem) and in the import probe, so import still names the record. It refuses bool priority/estimated_minutes and an estimate outside 0..2^31-1. Replay and read_attempt keep the looser rule. Not changed: an existing history holding such values still exports them.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G32_IntegerFieldsStayBeadsReadable`. On e9eed92: FAILED (failures=10): e.g. `AssertionError: 0 == 0 : import of {'priority': True} was accepted: {"imported": 1}`, `AssertionError: 0 == 0 : create --estimate=-7 was accepted: ...`, `AssertionError: Tuples differ: (-7, 'in_progress') != (0, 'open')`. With the diff: Ran 1 test OK. Against the reporter's fields_problem patch, the same test fails at its guard: `AssertionError: Lists differ: [] != ['ex-0', 'ex-1', 'ex-2']`.

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Run from the repo root with BEAD_UNDER_TEST=<scratch>/tree-G32/bead (the patched copy, with infra/compat_real.py copied beside it so test_compat's real-export test runs) and a scratch BEAD_HOME/TMPDIR: tests.test_replay: Ran 60 tests OK; tests.test_compat: Ran 3 tests OK (real exports); tests.test_commands: Ran 97 tests OK. No shipped test needs adapting.

#### G38 · Medium · robustness — Import of a record whose `comments` is not a list of objects tracebacks (AttributeError/TypeError) before validation runs, instead of failing with VALIDATION

**Mechanism.** import_records builds the comment list with `c.get(...)` over `rec.get('comments') or []` (bead:2016-2017). That runs before the probe whose payload_problem would reject a malformed comment (bead:2020-2024, 565-569). A string or a list holding a string or null hits `.get` and raises AttributeError; an int or bool is not iterable and raises TypeError. Neither is a BpError or OSError, so run() (bead:2867-2870) prints a traceback and --json callers get non-JSON stderr. `init --import` crashes the same way. Nothing is written in either case, because `writing` appends only after the body returns.

**Reproduction.** `bead import f.jsonl --json` where one record has `"comments"` set to ["text"], {"id":1}, 5, "abc", true or [null]; and `bead init --prefix bx --import f.jsonl --json` with comments ["x"].

**Observed.** e9eed92: rc 1 with tracebacks `AttributeError: 'str' object has no attribute 'get'`, `TypeError: 'int' object is not iterable`, `TypeError: 'bool' object is not iterable`, `AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'get'`.

**Expected.** A structured VALIDATION error naming the record (`<path>:<n>: record 'ex-bad' has malformed comments`); the import fails whole, as section 2 says.

**Proposed fix.** The first validation pass in import_records refuses a record whose comments are not a list of objects, next to the existing dependency check. Falsy values (false, 0, "", {}) still mean no comments, the same convention as dependencies and labels.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G38_ImportCommentsOfTheWrongShapeAreValidation` (2 tests). On e9eed92: FAILED (failures=7): e.g. `AssertionError: import with comments as number crashed: TypeError: 'int' object is not iterable`, `AssertionError: init --import with comments as a list of strings crashed: AttributeError: 'str' object has no attribute 'get'`. With the diff: Ran 2 tests OK.

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Run from the repo root with BEAD_UNDER_TEST=<scratch>/tree-G38/bead (the patched copy, with infra/compat_real.py copied beside it so test_compat's real-export test runs) and a scratch BEAD_HOME/TMPDIR: tests.test_commands: Ran 97 tests OK; tests.test_compat: Ran 3 tests OK (real exports). No shipped test needs adapting.

#### G50 · Low · data-loss — `machines --name` treats an aliases.json it cannot read or parse (a trailing comma, a BOM, a list, EACCES) as empty and replaces it with a file holding only the new name: every other machine name is lost, with exit 0

**Mechanism.** cmd_machines bead:2536-2541: `aliases = {}`, then `with contextlib.suppress(OSError, ValueError): aliases = loads_strict(...)` and a non-dict becomes {}; 2541-2542 `aliases[ident] = alias; write_atomic(path, ...)` replaces the file (os.replace needs only the directory to be writable, so even a mode-0 file is replaced). F13 made aliases.json strict for reading only; the write path still discards what it could not read. The names are local (not tracker data) and can be re-entered.

**Reproduction.** Sandbox, `bead init --prefix ex`; write $BEAD_HOME/aliases.json as '{"abcd0001": "desktop", "abcd0002": "ci",}' (or a list, or chmod 0); `bead machines --name abcd0003=new --json`; read aliases.json. Script: work-store-files/probe.py g50; test: G50.py.

**Observed.** `trailing comma: machines --name rc 0 | aliases.json now '{\n "abcd0003": "new"\n}'` / `list: machines --name rc 0 | aliases.json now '{\n "abcd0003": "new"\n}'` / `EACCES: machines --name rc 0 | aliases.json now '{\n "abcd0003": "new"\n}'`; the test also shows the same for a UTF-8 BOM.

**Expected.** `machines --name` refuses with a structured error and leaves a file it could not read untouched (or keeps its entries); a missing file still starts empty and a valid file is merged.

**Proposed fix.** Read aliases.json with try/except: FileNotFoundError -> {}; any other OSError or ValueError (including UnicodeDecodeError) -> BpError IO "<path> cannot be read (<error>); repair it before adding a name"; a non-object -> BpError IO "... does not hold a JSON object ...". Patched: rc 1 with that error for trailing comma, BOM, list and EACCES, file byte-identical; a valid file is merged; a missing file is created; plain `machines` (read) is unchanged. IO vs CONFIG for a parse error is arguable (F13 used IDENTITY for replica.json).

On e9eed92: FAIL test_g50_hand_edited_file_with_a_trailing_comma_is_not_overwritten [trailing comma]: `AssertionError: '"desktop"' not found in '{\n "abcd0003": "new"\n}' : trailing comma: abcd0001=desktop lost; aliases.json is now '{\n "abcd0003": "new"\n}'`; the same for [byte order mark], test_g50_wrong_shape_is_not_overwritten (list) and test_g50_unreadable_file_is_not_overwritten (EACCES). #### G39 · Low · privacy — A password inside a transport::URL remote (https::https://user:pw@host/...) is kept: stored in meta.json and .beadroll.json, shown by info, and handed to git, which sends it

**Mechanism.** without_password's pattern (bead:110 `(?i)^([a-z][a-z0-9+.-]*://)(.*)$`) does not match git's `<transport>::<address>` form, so `https::https://alice:pw@host/x` returns unchanged with False. cmd_init stores it (bead:1960, 1968, 1976), Workspace.remote returns it (bead:172), info prints it, and Link.fetch/publish pass it to git (bead:1624, 1772); git's transport_get hands `https://alice:pw@host/x` to git-remote-https, which sends `alice:pw` as Basic auth (seen on a local recording server). For an origin in this form, find_workspace also joins it onto the checkout (G05), which shows the password inside a bogus path.

**Reproduction.** `bead init --prefix ex --remote https::https://alice:supersecret@example.invalid/org/repo.git`, then meta.json and `bead info --json`; with `--tracked`, .beadroll.json; `bead init --remote http::http://alice:secret-g39@127.0.0.1:<port>/org/repo.git` against a local HTTP server that answers 401, then `bead sync` and look at the Authorization headers it got. Test: final/G39.py (scratch GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL, GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1, GIT_ASKPASS='').

**Observed.** e9eed92: `meta: { "name": "origin", "remote": "https::https://alice:supersecret@example.invalid/org/repo.git" }`, `info remote: https::https://alice:supersecret@example.invalid/org/repo.git`, no `password was dropped` note; .beadroll.json `https::https://alice:supersecret@example.invalid/org/repo.git`; the recording server received `['Basic YWxpY2U6c2VjcmV0LWczOQ==']` (alice:secret-g39). Origin form: `info remote: <box>/g39o-main/https::https:/alice:supersecret@example.invalid/org/repo.git`.

**Expected.** The password is dropped exactly as for the plain `scheme://` spelling, keeping the `transport::` prefix: `https::https://...` without the secret in meta.json, .beadroll.json and info, the same note as the plain URL, and git never receives it.

**Proposed fix.** without_password accepts an optional `transport::` prefix before `scheme://` (`(?i)^((?:[a-z][a-z0-9+.-]*::)?([a-z][a-z0-9+.-]*)://)(.*)$`) and decides the http(s) token rule by the inner scheme. Only transport-prefixed scheme:// forms change; `ext::...`, `fd::3`, `codecommit::region://name` without '@' are untouched. The origin form additionally needs G05's hunk (otherwise find_workspace turns it into a path); with G05 + G39 together `info` shows `https::https://example.invalid/org/repo.git`. Residual: normalize_remote does not strip a transport prefix, so the derived workspace id for such an origin still includes the prefix (and, through it, the password; ids differ from the plain URL). Conflicts with G06 and G40 (all rewrite without_password); final/urls-combined.diff holds the merged function.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G39_PasswordInATransportUrl` (3 tests). On e9eed92: FAIL (failures=3): `AssertionError: 'supersecret' unexpectedly found in 'https::https://alice:supersecret@example.invalid/org/repo.git' : the password is in .beadroll.json`; `AssertionError: Lists differ: ['Basic YWxpY2U6c2VjcmV0LWczOQ=='] != []` (git was handed the password); `AssertionError: 'supersecret' unexpectedly found in 'https::https://alice:supersecret@...githttps::https://alice:supersecret@...git' : the password was kept`. With the diff: work-urls/G39/bead: Ran 3 tests in 2.045s OK; combined copy: OK.

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Per-diff selection run from the repo (BpTest.test_worktrees_and_clones_share_one_tracker_and_checkouts_stay_clean, BpTest.test_claim_needs_the_remote_unless_offline_is_asked_for, BpTest.test_tracked_workspace_id_and_a_separate_coordination_remote, CompatAuditCLI.test_workspace_config_cannot_escape_home, CompatAuditCLI.test_changed_workspace_config_does_not_delete_old_store, Review5CLI.test_e02_remote_credentials_are_not_disclosed_or_world_readable, Review6.test_f03_credential_urls_are_not_stored, Review6.test_remote_url_shapes_that_are_already_safe, plus all of test_replay) against work-urls/G39/bead: 'Ran 68 tests in 201.260s OK'. With all five urls diffs applied together (final/urls-combined.diff = G05 + G21 + a hand merge of G06/G39/G40 + G06's test hunk; BEAD_UNDER_TEST=work-urls/all/bead, test_commands taken from that copy): test_commands 'Ran 97 tests in 305.739s OK'; test_protocol 'Ran 54 tests in 227.513s OK'; test_replay 'Ran 60 tests in 109.659s OK'; test_storage 'Ran 9 tests in 30.635s OK' (load average ~190 during the runs). No shipped test needs adapting.

<details><summary>Proposed diff (G39, applies to e9eed92 with <code>patch -p1</code>)</summary>

```diff
--- a/bead
+++ b/bead
@@ -107,15 +107,16 @@
 def without_password(url: str) -> tuple[str, bool]:
     """A remote URL with any password removed from its user-info part, and whether one was there.
     bead never stores or uses an embedded password: git's credential helpers are the place for it."""
-    match = re.match(r"(?i)^([a-z][a-z0-9+.-]*://)(.*)$", url, re.S)
-    if not match or "@" not in match.group(2):
+    # git's `<transport>::<address>` form hands the address, password included, to the remote helper
+    match = re.match(r"(?i)^((?:[a-z][a-z0-9+.-]*::)?([a-z][a-z0-9+.-]*)://)(.*)$", url, re.S)
+    if not match or "@" not in match.group(3):
         return url, False
-    scheme, rest = match.group(1), match.group(2)
+    scheme, rest = match.group(1), match.group(3)
     authority, _, tail = rest.rpartition("@")
     if ":" in authority:  # user:password, whatever the password contains
         user = authority.split(":", 1)[0]
         return (f"{scheme}{user}@{tail}" if user else f"{scheme}{tail}"), True
-    if scheme.lower() in ("http://", "https://"):  # https://TOKEN@host is how access tokens are written
+    if match.group(2).lower() in ("http", "https"):  # https://TOKEN@host is how access tokens are written
         return f"{scheme}{tail}", True
     return url, False  # ssh://user@host: a user, not a secret
```
</details>

#### G47 · Low · privacy — The store is not 'private by its own modes (0700/0600)': under umask 022, 289 of 316 store paths in a full two-machine run are group/other-readable (events/, events/<m>/, claims/, pending/, lock, *.torn-*, *.fork-*, all of sync.git including FETCH_HEAD with the remote URL and the loose objects holding every event), `machines --name` as the first command creates BEAD_HOME 0755, and torn/fork copies named by whole seconds overwrite each other

**Mechanism.** Only BEAD_HOME and <ws> are chmodded (private_dir 221-224, called from locked() 1221-1222, Link() 1601, replica_id() 248, i.e. only on writes) and only the files bead opens with 0600 are private. Everything else follows the umask: locked() 1223 `ws.events_dir.mkdir` and 1224 `open(lock, "a+")`; append_events 476 `path.parent.mkdir`; write_atomic 230 `path.parent.mkdir(parents=True)` (events/<m>/, claims/, and BEAD_HOME itself when cmd_machines 2542 runs first); coordinated 1917 `pending` mkdir; repair_tail 402 and Link.merge 1697/1721 `Path.write_bytes` for *.torn-*/*.fork-* (full copies of a segment or attempt); Link.fetch 1621 `git init --bare` and 1624 `git fetch` (dirs 0755, files 0644, objects 0444, FETCH_HEAD 0644 holding the remote URL). README:132-134 and the F04 resolution state '0700 directories, 0600 files'. Secondary: the *.torn-*/*.fork-* suffix is int(time.time()), so a second fragment or fork of the same file within one second overwrites the first. In practice the data stays unreachable while BEAD_HOME and <ws> are 0700 (true after any write command), so this is a defence-in-depth/documentation gap; a read command never re-tightens a relaxed store.

**Reproduction.** work-store-files/sweep.py (full sequence per umask 022/002/077: `machines --name` first in a fresh home; init --tracked --remote; create x2; comments add; claim; release; claim; takeover; close; claim with BEAD_TEST_CRASH_AFTER_PUSH=1 + retry; claim with BEAD_TEST_CRASH_BEFORE_PUSH=1 + sync; torn tail + create; foreign fork via a second machine + sync; export -o; export -o onto a directory; export -o under RLIMIT_FSIZE; SIGKILL before os.replace for export -o and for a claim) then table.py; results in final/modes-table.md. Test: G47.py.

**Observed.** e9eed92, umask 022: 289 of 316 store paths carry a group/other bit (events 2/2, events/<m> 4/4, claims/ 4/4, pending 2/2, lock 2/2, *.torn-* 1/1, *.fork-* 2/2, sync.git loose objects 112/112 `-r--r--r--`, rest of sync.git 160/160); umask 002: 293/320 (`drwxrwxr-x`, `-rw-rw-r--`); umask 077: 0/314. `-rw-r--r-- <ws>/lock`, `-rw-r--r-- events/<m>/000001.jsonl.fork-1791404435`, `-rw-r--r-- events/<m>/000001.jsonl.torn-1791404433`, `-rw-r--r-- sync.git/FETCH_HEAD` containing "054672993d...\t\t'refs/beads/journal' of <sandbox>/origin\n". After `machines --name deadbeef=laptop` in a fresh BEAD_HOME: `drwxr-xr-x .`, `-rw------- aliases.json`; `list` (rc 1), `info` (rc 0), `ready` (rc 1) leave it drwxr-xr-x, `init` makes it drwx------. In-process: two repair_tail calls within one second leave only `[b'{"second']`.

**Expected.** Every directory bead creates under BEAD_HOME is 0700 and every file 0600 (git objects 0400) whatever the umask, as README:132-134 and F04 state; torn and fork copies get unique names.

**Proposed fix.** New private_dirs() creates a directory and its missing parents with mode 0700 (`mkdir(mode=0o700, exist_ok=True)`, so a concurrent creator is fine and a regular file in the way still raises FileExistsError); private_dir, write_atomic(private=True), append_events, locked() and coordinated's pending/ use it. The lock is opened with mode 0600. *.torn-*/*.fork-* are written with write_atomic (0600) and named by time.time_ns(). sync.git is created with `git init --shared=0600` (git keeps 0700 dirs, 0600 files, 0400 objects and packs, also after gc) and fetched with `--no-write-fetch-head` (FETCH_HEAD was the one path --shared leaves 0644; bead never reads it; needs git >= 2.29, below bead's existing 2.31 floor). Measured with G47: 0 loose store paths under 022 (0/307), 002 (0/314) and 077 (0/314); BEAD_HOME after `machines --name` first is drwx------. Not done: no migration of an existing store (old sync.git, 0755 dirs and 0644 lock keep their modes); missing parents of BEAD_HOME are now created 0700 (XDG-consistent) instead of with the umask; `--template=` is left to the template-hooks finding. The diff is generated with `diff -U2` so that it and G48.diff (adjacent lines in write_atomic) apply in either order with `git apply`. Checker change: the first version suppressed every FileExistsError in private_dirs, so private_dir() on a path that is a regular file (BEAD_HOME naming a file) went on to chmod that file 0700 and reported `Not a directory` instead of `File exists`; the diff now uses exist_ok=True, and BEAD_HOME=<regular file> `create` gives `IO File exists: <file>` with the file left 0644, as before.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G47_StoreIsPrivateByItsOwnModes` (3 tests). On e9eed92: FAIL test_g47_every_store_path_is_owner_only_under_umask_022: `AssertionError: Lists differ: ['drwxr-xr-x 5c362bc3a96ec27d/sync.git', '[4718 chars]c21'] != []` First list contains 75 additional elements : 75 store paths are not owner-only; FAIL test_g47_machines_name_as_first_command_makes_a_private_home: `AssertionError: Lists differ: ['drwxr-xr-x .'] != []`; FAIL test_g47_two_torn_fragments_in_one_second_are_both_kept: `AssertionError: Lists differ: [b'{"second'] != [b'{"first', b'{"second']`. Checker: pristine 3 failures (76 loose paths in that run; the count varies with the number of git objects) and patched OK reproduced, also under umask 002. The diff was revised (exist_ok=True in private_dirs) and re-verified.

Independent check (check-store-files, Python 3.14.7, git 2.55.0): all six diffs apply with `patch -p1` to fresh pristine copies, singly and combined in either order (G04..G52 and G52..G04 give identical files). In a parallel run at load average ~185, test_protocol had 1 failure, test_timed_out_accepted_release_returns_success (BEAD_NET_TIMEOUT=0.1 timing test, also seen flaking by the triage-33 verifier); that test passed 3/3 alone on the combined cop…

```diff
--- a/bead
+++ b/bead
@@ -219,7 +219,17 @@
 
 
+def private_dirs(path: Path):
+    """Create `path` and any missing parents as 0700, whatever the umask: nothing in the store follows it."""
+    missing = []
+    while not path.is_dir() and path != path.parent:
+        missing.append(path)
+        path = path.parent
+    for folder in reversed(missing):
+        folder.mkdir(mode=0o700, exist_ok=True)  # a race loser is fine; a file in the way still raises
+
+
 def private_dir(path: Path):
     """A directory only its owner can enter: the store and everything beneath it."""
-    path.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
+    private_dirs(path)
     os.chmod(path, 0o700)
 
@@ -228,5 +238,8 @@
     """Written whole, then renamed into place. Store files are 0600; a file written into the
     caller's checkout (`private=False`) follows the caller's umask like any other file there."""
-    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
+    if private:
+        private_dirs(path.parent)
+    else:
+        path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
     tmp = path.with_name(f".{path.name}.{os.getpid()}.tmp")
     fd = os.open(tmp, os.O_WRONLY | os.O_CREAT | os.O_TRUNC, 0o600 if private else 0o666)
@@ -400,5 +413,5 @@

+        write_atomic(path.with_name(f"{path.name}.torn-{time.time_ns()}"), data[len(fixed):])
     write_atomic(path, fixed)
 
@@ -474,5 +487,5 @@

         segment.write(encode_events(events))
```
