#### G42 · Low · wrong-readiness — `dep remove` (and `update --parent`) of an imported dependency names the copy of the key the remover saw; when a concurrent import of the same key sorts first and keeps the key, the import binds to that copy after the merge and the dependency is live again on every machine

**Mechanism.** The second create of an existing key is renamed with free_key (apply_event 989-993 at e9eed92). materialise_dangling (862-878) binds a dangling key K to st.by_key[K], i.e. the copy whose create comes first in causal order. In the remover's own view the import is bound to its copy B2 (tag = the importing issue's uid, 877). cmd_dep emits only dep_remove {to: B2} (2438) and update --parent only {to, type: parent-child} (2239). The uid branch (1108-1113) deletes tags on edges to B2 and records retracted[(B2, type)]. After the merge the import is bound to the other copy B3 and nothing removes its tag on (B3, kind).

**Reproduction.** m1: `init --prefix ex`, import ex-a (depends on ex-b, not an issue), `create Q`, sync; m2, m3 sync. m3 imports ex-b, then m2 imports ex-b (no sync between: m3's copy has the earlier id and keeps the key). m2 runs `dep remove ex-a ex-b` (by uid, its own copy); sync m1, m2, m3 twice; `show ex-a`. Variant: the dependency is parent-child and m2 runs `update ex-a --parent Q`. Both in G42.py.

**Observed.** Pristine e9eed92, G42.py: "AssertionError: Lists differ: [{'id': 'ex-b', 'title': 'B', 'status': 'open', 'dependency_type': 'blocks'}] != [] : the imported dependency m2 took back is live again, bound to the other copy of ex-b" (m2 showed [] right after the removal; exports of all three machines equal); update --parent: "AssertionError: Lists differ: ['ex-7xvb', 'ex-b'] != ['ex-7xvb'] : the imported parent m2 moved ex-a away from is a parent again after the merge".

**Expected.** Once a writer has taken back an imported dependency on a key, it stays taken back whichever copy of that key keeps the name after the merge.

**Proposed fix.** Route both emitters through a remove_edge helper: when an edge being removed carries the issue's own uid tag (only materialise_dangling writes that, so the edge is an imported dependency on key st.key(to)), emit dep_remove {key: st.key(to), type} as well as {to}. Needs G14's key-branch hunk (pop the import's tag from the edge to by_key[key]); G42.diff therefore includes that hunk and is a superset of G14.diff (apply one of them, or dangling-combined.diff). With only the CLI hunks (work-dangling/G42cmdonly) or only G14's hunk, both G42 tests still fail. Limits: removals already in a journal (only the {to} event) are not repaired; mixed-version caveat as in G14.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G42_UidRetractionMissesTwinOfImportedKey` (2 tests). On e9eed92: FAILED (failures=2): AssertionError: Lists differ: [{'id': 'ex-b', 'title': 'B', 'status': 'open', 'dependency_type': 'blocks'}] != [] : the imported dependency m2 took back is live again, bound to the other copy of ex-b With the diff: Ran 2 tests in 6.134s OK (work-dangling/G42/bead); also OK on combined

**Shipped suites with the diff.** This diff alone (work-dangling/G42/bead, includes G14's hunk): test_replay Ran 60 tests in 164.244s OK; test_commands Ran 97 tests in 459.969s OK. Combined copy (work-dangling/combined/bead = dangling-combined.diff, all seven fixes): test_replay Ran 60 OK; test_commands Ran 97 OK; test_protocol Ran 54 OK; test_storage Ran 9 OK; test_compat CompatAuditCommentIds + CompatAuditRefValidation Ran 2 OK (CompatAuditRealCompatibility not run: it reads the real exports under ~/Documents). test_stress not run (it never imports, so no dangling dependency exists there). No shipped test needs adapting.

<details><summary>Proposed diff (G42, applies to e9eed92 with <code>patch -p1</code>)</summary>

```diff
--- a/bead
```

#### G46 · Low · wrong-readiness — Import accepts a record that depends on itself; the issue is then blocked by itself and never ready, while `dep add X X` and br refuse the same edge

**Mechanism.** import_records' pre-write validation (bead 1993-2000 at e9eed92) checks only the shape of each dependency. The record's own id is in all_ids (2009), so the dependency is not dangling, and the dep_add loop (2027-2033) emits dep_add {to: its own uid}. cmd_dep refuses issue uid == target uid with CYCLE (2431) and materialise_dangling never binds a dangling entry to its own issue (869), but import never checks. Section 2's rule is that an import with one invalid dependency fails whole.

**Reproduction.** `init --prefix ex`; import a file with {ex-ok} and {ex-a depending on ex-a} (types blocks, related, parent-child); `list --all`, `ready`, `dep cycles`, `dep add ex-a ex-a`; br in a scratch workspace on the same record. work-dangling/observe.py; test in G46.py.

**Observed.** Pristine: import {"imported": 1}; show blocked True, not in ready; dep cycles {'cycles': [['ex-s']], 'count': 1}; `dep add ex-a ex-a`: {"error": {"code": "CYCLE", "message": "ex-a -> ex-a would create a dependency cycle"}}. br 0.5.7 (scratch): "code": "SELF_DEPENDENCY", "message": "Issue cannot depend on itself: ex-a". G46.py: '{"imported": 2}' for each of blocks, related, parent-child, and dep cycles count 2.

**Expected.** The import fails whole with VALIDATION and writes nothing, as `dep add` and br already refuse a self-dependency.

**Proposed fix.** In the pre-write validation loop: if any dependency's depends_on_id equals the record's id, raise VALIDATION "{path}:{n}: record 'ex-a' depends on itself". Covers every type, like dep add. Self dep_add events already in a journal (written by an older bead) still replay; import was the only emitter.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G46_ImportAcceptsSelfDependency`. On e9eed92: FAILED (failures=4): AssertionError: 0 == 0 : a blocks self-dependency was imported: {"imported": 2} (same for related and parent-child); AssertionError: 2 != 0 : imported self-dependencies form cycles With the diff: Ran 1 test in 0.853s OK (work-dangling/G46/bead); also OK on combined

**Shipped suites with the diff.** This diff alone (work-dangling/G46/bead): test_commands Ran 97 tests in 311.125s OK. Combined copy (work-dangling/combined/bead = dangling-combined.diff, all seven fixes): test_replay Ran 60 OK; test_commands Ran 97 OK; test_protocol Ran 54 OK; test_storage Ran 9 OK; test_compat CompatAuditCommentIds + CompatAuditRefValidation Ran 2 OK (CompatAuditRealCompatibility not run: it reads the real exports under ~/Documents). test_stress not run (it never imports, so no dangling dependency exists there). No shipped test needs adapting.

#### G40 · Low · robustness — without_password treats any '@' after the authority as user-info, so a URL with '@' in its path, query or after a port is rewritten to a different host (https://host/org/repo@v2.git -> https://v2.git) that bead stores and syncs with, claiming a password was dropped

**Mechanism.** without_password (bead:107-120) splits `rest` at its LAST '@' (bead:114 `rest.rpartition("@")`) without stopping at the end of the authority, then treats everything before it as user[:password] (bead:115-117) or, for http(s), as a token (bead:118-119). git (credential.c credential_from_url, urlmatch.c url_normalize, connect.c) only reads user-info before the first '/', '?' or '#' after `scheme://`. So https://host/org/repo@v2.git -> https://v2.git, ssh://host:2222/srv/a@b.git -> ssh://host@b.git, https://[::1]:8443/a@b -> https://[@b (then https://b on the second application), https://u:p@h/r@x -> https://u@x, file:///tmp/a:b@c/r.git -> file:///tmp/a@c/r.git, https://host/r?x=a@b -> https://b, each returning True so init prints `the password was dropped`. cmd_init stores the result (bead:1960, 1976), Workspace.remote uses it (bead:172) and Link fetches/pushes it (bead:1624, 1772): bead talks to a host git never would, and fails with REMOTE_UNREACHABLE. The workspace id is unaffected (normalize_remote handles these). git, checked with a local server and a fake ssh: `http://127.0.0.1:P/org/repo@v2.git` -> host 127.0.0.1, path /org/repo@v2.git; `http://u:p@127.0.0.1:P/r@x` -> Basic u:p to 127.0.0.1, path /r@x; `ssh://host.example:2222/srv/a@b.git` -> `-p 2222 host.example git-upload-pack '/srv/a@b.git'`; `ssh://[::1]:2222/a@b` -> `-p 2222 ::1 ... '/a@b'`; `file:///tmp/a:b@c/r.git` -> path '/tmp/a:b@c/r.git'.

**Reproduction.** `bead init --prefix ex --remote <url>` then meta.json, `bead info --json` and init's output, for each url above; end to end: a bare repo at `<box>/a:b@c-tracker.git`, `git remote set-url origin file://<box>/a:b@c-tracker.git` (git ls-remote works), `bead init`, `bead sync --json`. Test: final/G40.py.

**Observed.** e9eed92: `https://host.example/org/repo@v2.git -> meta https://v2.git info https://v2.git note=True`; `ssh://host.example:2222/srv/a@b.git -> meta ssh://host.example@b.git`; `https://[::1]:8443/a@b -> meta https://[@b info https://b`; `https://u:p@h/r@x -> meta https://u@x info https://x`; `file:///tmp/a:b@c/r.git -> meta file:///tmp/a@c/r.git`; `https://host.example/r?x=a@b -> meta https://b`; file origin sync: `{"error": {"code": "REMOTE_UNREACHABLE", "message": "cannot reach the coordination remote file://<box>/a@c-tracker.git: fatal: '...a@c-tracker.git' does not appear to be a git repository...`.

**Expected.** User-info is read only from the authority, as git reads it: the URLs without user-info are stored and used unchanged with no note; https://u:p@h/r@x keeps host h and path /r@x and drops only the credential; the file:// origin syncs.

**Proposed fix.** Limit user-info to the authority: the text after `scheme://` up to the first '/', '?' or '#'; within it the user-info runs to the last '@' (so a password holding an unencoded '@' is still dropped whole). Choice for F03's slash case: git does NOT read `https://alice:sec/ret@example.invalid/private.git` as user:password (credential.c sees the '/' before the '@' and takes `alice:sec` as host:port; curl rejects it with `URL rejected: Port number was not a decimal number between 0 and 65535` and contacts nothing; for ssh:// git runs ssh to host `alice:sec`). I kept F03's protection only for that unusable shape: when the authority is not a valid host[:port] (a ':' followed by a non-number, outside [IPv6] brackets) and an '@' follows later, the text up to the first '@' is taken as user:password. git cannot use such a URL at all, so nothing working changes, the probable secret stays out of .beadroll.json/meta.json, and Review6.test_f03_credential_urls_are_not_stored passes unchanged; every URL with a valid host[:port] authority is read exactly as git reads it. Conflicts with G06 and G39 (all rewrite without_password); final/urls-combined.diff holds the merged function, which is idempotent on the 32 shapes checked (work-urls/fncmp.py).

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G40_AtSignAfterTheAuthority` (3 tests). On e9eed92: FAIL (failures=7): `AssertionError: 'https://v2.git' != 'https://host.example/org/repo@v2.git' : meta.json holds a different remote`, `'ssh://host.example@b.git' != 'ssh://host.example:2222/srv/a@b.git'`, `'https://[@b' != 'https://[::1]:8443/a@b'`, `'file:///tmp/a@c/r.git' != 'file:///tmp/a:b@c/r.git'`, `'https://b' != 'https://host.example/r?x=a@b'`, `AssertionError: False is not true : the host became 'https://u@x'`, and for the origin `'file://<box>/a@c-tracker.git' != 'file://<box>/a:b@c-tracker.git'`. With the diff: work-urls/G40/bead: Ran 3 tests in 4.092s OK; combined copy: OK.

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Per-diff selection run from the repo (BpTest.test_worktrees_and_clones_share_one_tracker_and_checkouts_stay_clean, BpTest.test_claim_needs_the_remote_unless_offline_is_asked_for, BpTest.test_tracked_workspace_id_and_a_separate_coordination_remote, CompatAuditCLI.test_workspace_config_cannot_escape_home, CompatAuditCLI.test_changed_workspace_config_does_not_delete_old_store, Review5CLI.test_e02_remote_credentials_are_not_disclosed_or_world_readable, Review6.test_f03_credential_urls_are_not_stored, Review6.test_remote_url_shapes_that_are_already_safe, plus all of test_replay) against work-urls/G40/bead: 'Ran 68 tests in 211.081s OK' (includes test_f03 slash case and test_e02). With all five urls diffs applied together (final/urls-combined.diff = G05 + G21 + a hand merge of G06/G39/G40 + G06's test hunk; BEAD_UNDER_TEST=work-urls/all/bead, test_commands taken from that copy): test_commands 'Ran 97 tests in 305.739s OK'; test_protocol 'Ran 54 tests in 227.513s OK'; test_replay 'Ran 60 tests in 109.659s OK'; test_storage 'Ran 9 tests in 30.635s OK' (load average ~190 during the runs). No shipped test needs adapting.

<details><summary>Proposed diff (G40, applies to e9eed92 with <code>patch -p1</code>)</summary>

```diff
--- a/bead
+++ b/bead
@@ -108,10 +108,17 @@
     """A remote URL with any password removed from its user-info part, and whether one was there.
     bead never stores or uses an embedded password: git's credential helpers are the place for it."""
     match = re.match(r"(?i)^([a-z][a-z0-9+.-]*://)(.*)$", url, re.S)
-    if not match or "@" not in match.group(2):
+    if not match:
         return url, False
     scheme, rest = match.group(1), match.group(2)
-    authority, _, tail = rest.rpartition("@")
+    end = len(re.match(r"[^/?#]*", rest).group())  # as git reads a URL, user-info is only in the authority
+    if "@" in rest[:end]:
+        authority, _, host = rest[:end].rpartition("@")
+        tail = host + rest[end:]
+    elif "@" in rest[end:] and not re.fullmatch(r"(\[[^\]]*\]|[^:]*)(:[0-9]*)?", rest[:end]):
+        authority, _, tail = rest.partition("@")  # no host[:port] git could use: `user:pass/word@host`
+    else:
+        return url, False  # an '@' in the path, query or fragment is not user-info
     if ":" in authority:  # user:password, whatever the password contains
         user = authority.split(":", 1)[0]
         return (f"{scheme}{user}@{tail}" if user else f"{scheme}{tail}"), True
```
</details>

#### G41 · Low · robustness — A remote whose refs/beads/journal names objects it no longer has is reported as REMOTE_UNREACHABLE ('nothing is lost; retry later'), and the message never names the damaged ref

**Mechanism.** Link.fetch maps every fetch failure except 'find remote ref' to REMOTE_UNREACHABLE 'cannot reach the coordination remote <url>: <git>' (bead:1626-1630). That includes 'upload-pack: not our ref <sha>' (the ref's commit is missing) and 'remote: fatal: bad tree object <sha>' (its tree is missing), where the remote answered. PRIME (bead:2616-2619) defines REMOTE_UNREACHABLE as 'nothing is lost; retry later', but no retry can succeed until someone repairs the remote, and the message names neither the ref nor the repair. A `git gc --prune=now` racing a push produced this state in the reporter's soaks. The pushing machine's side (its push gets '[remote rejected] (missing necessary objects)', which bead:1780-1782 retries 12 times as a race before SYNC_CONFLICT) is G18's mechanism and is fixed by G18's diff (source 50's full test passes on the combined copy, fails on G41 alone only at the pusher).

**Reproduction.** Sandbox, machines a and b: a init, sync; b sync; a create work, sync (b has not fetched it); delete the remote's loose object for the tip commit (or for its tree): origin.git/objects/<sha[:2]>/<sha[2:]>; then on b `bead sync --json` and `bead claim KEY --json`. G41.py runs both states.

**Observed.** commit lost: 'AssertionError: 'REMOTE_UNREACHABLE' == 'REMOTE_UNREACHABLE' : sync: a remote that answered is reported unreachable ('nothing is lost; retry later'): cannot reach the coordination remote .../origin.git: fatal: remote error: upload-pack: not our ref 49956c21... fatal: git upload-pack: not our ref 49956c21...'; tree lost: '... remote: fatal: bad tree object 72a34c25... error: git upload-pack: git-pack-objects died with error. fatal: git upload-pack: aborting due to possible repository corruption on the remote side. ... fatal: protocol error: bad pack header'.

**Expected.** A distinct code saying the remote's ref is damaged, naming the ref and the remote, that retrying will not help, and the repair.

**Proposed fix.** In Link.fetch's failure branch, if a line git relayed from the remote (starting with `remote:` or containing `upload-pack:`) says 'not our ref', 'bad (tree|blob|commit) object', 'unable to read <sha>' or 'does not point to a valid object', raise REMOTE_DAMAGED: 'refs/beads/journal on <url> names objects that remote no longer has; retrying will not help. Every machine keeps its own journal: delete the ref there (`git update-ref -d refs/beads/journal` in that repository), then run `bead sync` on every machine to publish again. git said: <git>'. PRIME lists REMOTE_DAMAGED. Deliberately not matched: 'possible repository corruption' alone (upload-pack prints it whenever pack-objects dies, e.g. killed) and lines from the local git (a damaged local sync.git must not send people to delete the remote's ref). The verifier checked that deleting the ref and syncing every machine converges with acknowledged claims intact; resetting it is not possible from the remote alone when the commit itself is lost. Patterns are English: the lead's G03 (LC_ALL=C for Link's git) makes them locale-proof.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G41_DamagedRemoteIsNotAnUnreachableOne`. On e9eed92: FAIL (lost='commit'): AssertionError: 'REMOTE_UNREACHABLE' == 'REMOTE_UNREACHABLE' : sync: a remote that answered is reported unreachable ('nothing is lost; retry later'): cannot reach the coordination remote .../origin.git: fatal: git upload-pack: not our ref e21943b034...; FAIL (lost='tree'): same with 'remote: fatal: bad tree object 126b73d01eabea3...' (Ran 1 test, FAILED (failures=2)) With the diff: Ran 1 test in 2.289s OK (also OK on the combined copy)

**Shipped suites with the diff.** G41 copy: tests.test_protocol Ran 54 tests in 105.964s OK. No shipped test needs a change. Source 50's original test (which also checks the pusher) fails on the G41 copy alone only at the pusher ('SYNC_CONFLICT' == 'SYNC_CONFLICT'), fails on the G18 copy alone at the fetch side, and passes on the combined copy. All five diffs applied together (push-outcomes-combined.diff, bead + tests/test_protocol.py): three textual conflicts when applied one after another with patch(1), resolved by hand in the combined diff: G16 x G41 (both edit Link.fetch's failure branch; G41's hunk fails after G16), G11 x G18 (both rewrite Link.publish's classification; combined: --porcelain status line decides, '[remote rejected]' with a non-race reason is 'refused', last_error = status summary + git's stderr), G41 x G18 (both add a line to PRIME's error codes; combined lists REMOTE_REFUSED and REMOTE_DAMAGED). Combined copy (bead from push-outcomes-combined.diff; tests from the same diff, i.e. tests/test_protocol.py with the G11 and G18 hunks): tests.test_protocol Ran 54 tests in 203.591s OK; tests.test_commands Ran 97 tests in 248.353s OK; tests.test_storage Ran 9 tests OK; tests.test_replay Ran 60 tests OK; tests.test_review7 (the lead's sweep slice, untracked file as of now) Ran 1 test OK. All five G tests and the triage tests of sources 01, 02, 03, 05 and 50 pass on the combined copy. Not run: test_…

#### G43 · Low · robustness — Export writes a renamed record's still-unresolved imported dependency with the other record's id as issue_id, so br refuses the whole file

**Mechanism.** export_records (bead 1574 at e9eed92) emits issue['dangling'] verbatim; those are the raw imported dependency dicts (import_records 2014) carrying the issue_id they had in the source file. Resolved edges are rebuilt with issue['id'] (1576-1579). When the same record is imported on two machines, replay renames the second create (free_key, 992), so its exported dangling dependency names the other record.

**Reproduction.** m1 `init --prefix ex`, sync; m2 sync; both import the same record ex-a with dependency {issue_id: ex-a, depends_on_id: 'external:other:ex-9', type: blocks}; sync m1, m2, m1, m2; `export`; load the export into a fresh scratch br workspace (`git init`, `br init --prefix ex`, copy to .beads/issues.jsonl, `br sync --import-only --json`, HOME=scratch). work-dangling/br_check.py; test in G43.py.

**Observed.** Pristine export: {"id":"ex-af",...,"dependencies":[{"issue_id":"ex-a","depends_on_id":"external:other:ex-9",...}]}; br: rc 4 "VALIDATION_FAILED", "Validation failed: dependency.issue_id: dependency issue_id 'ex-a' does not match import issue 'ex-af'". Patched export: "issue_id":"ex-a6" on record ex-a6; br: rc 0 {"created":2,...}. G43.py pristine: "AssertionError: Lists differ: ['ex-a'] != ['ex-ap']".

**Expected.** Every exported dependency names its own record (issue_id == id), as resolved edges already do, so br loads the export.

**Proposed fix.** export_records emits [{**d, "issue_id": issue["id"]} for d in issue["dangling"]] (new dicts; replay state untouched; nothing else reads a dangling entry's issue_id).

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G43_RenamedRecordExportsForeignIssueId`. On e9eed92: FAILED (failures=1): AssertionError: Lists differ: ['ex-a'] != ['ex-ap'] : record ex-ap exports a dependency whose issue_id names another record With the diff: Ran 1 test in 1.819s OK (work-dangling/G43/bead); also OK on combined

</details>

#### G44 · Low · robustness — A dependency imported without created_at/created_by exports them as null when its target arrives in a later import or from another machine, while a one-shot import stamps them; br refuses the null

**Mechanism.** dep_add applies {"created_at": when, "created_by": who, **meta} (bead 1103 at e9eed92), so a one-shot import (import_records 2027-2033) is stamped. materialise_dangling (876) builds meta only from the keys present in the imported dependency, with no defaults, and export_records (1577-1579) writes meta.get('created_at') / meta.get('created_by') -> null.

**Reproduction.** Machine 'split': `init --prefix ex`; import a.jsonl (ex-a depends on ex-b, dependency without created_at/created_by); import b.jsonl (ex-b). Machine 'one': import both in one file. Compare the exported dependency. Two-machine variant: x imports a.jsonl, y imports b.jsonl, sync twice. br check as in G43 (work-dangling/br_check.py). Tests in G44.py.

**Observed.** Pristine split export: {'issue_id': 'ex-a', 'depends_on_id': 'ex-b', 'type': 'blocks', 'created_at': None, 'created_by': None, 'metadata': '{}', 'thread_id': ''}; br: rc 7 "CONFIG_ERROR", "Invalid JSON at line 1: invalid type: null, expected an RFC 3339 formatted date and time string at line 1 column 257". Two-machine variant: same nulls on both machines. Patched: 'created_at': '2026-10-07T20:20:56.867Z', 'created_by': 'codex:split-thread-0001@53c68b11'; br rc 0 {"created":2,...}.

**Expected.** A materialised dependency gets the same defaults a dep_add gets (the importing create's time and actor) unless it carries its own values; incremental, synced and one-shot exports are all valid for br (timestamps may differ by milliseconds).

**Proposed fix.** materialise_dangling: meta = {"created_at": event_time(issue["uid"]), "created_by": st.who(issue["uid"]), **present keys}. Deterministic on every machine (derived from the create event), nothing new is persisted, existing stores get it on the next replay. An explicit null in the input still exports null (input fidelity, as in one-shot).

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G44_MaterialisedDependencyExportsNullMeta` (2 tests). On e9eed92: FAILED (failures=2): AssertionError: None is not an instance of <class 'str'> : incremental import exported a null created_at: {'issue_id': 'ex-a', 'depends_on_id': 'ex-b', 'type': 'blocks', 'created_at': None, 'created_by': None, 'metadata': '{}', 'thread_id': ''} With the diff: Ran 2 tests in 2.916s OK (work-dangling/G44/bead); also OK on combined

**Shipped suites with the diff.** This diff alone (work-dangling/G44/bead): test_replay Ran 60 tests in 164.274s OK; test_commands Ran 97 tests in 460.836s OK (includes the Q06/F08/F09 import tests). Combined copy (work-dangling/combined/bead = dangling-combined.diff, all seven fixes): test_replay Ran 60 OK; test_commands Ran 97 OK; test_protocol Ran 54 OK; test_storage Ran 9 OK; test_compat CompatAuditCommentIds + CompatAuditRefValidation Ran 2 OK (CompatAuditRealCompatibility not run: it reads the real exports under ~/Documents). test_stress not run (it never imports, so no dangling dependency exists there). No shipped test needs adapting.

```diff

+++ b/bead
@@ -873,7 +873,8 @@
         if any(st.saw(removal, issue["uid"]) for key in (edge, (target, None))
                for removal in issue["retracted"].get(key, ())):
             continue  # taken back before the target existed, by someone who had seen this import
-        meta = {k: dep[k] for k in ("created_at", "created_by", "metadata", "thread_id") if k in dep}
+        meta = {"created_at": event_time(issue["uid"]), "created_by": st.who(issue["uid"]),  # as dep_add stamps it
+                **{k: dep[k] for k in ("created_at", "created_by", "metadata", "thread_id") if k in dep}}
```

#### G48 · Low · robustness — write_atomic leaves its `.NAME.PID.tmp` behind when the write, fsync or rename fails: a full or partial export copy as an untracked dotfile in the user's checkout (`export -o`), partial receipts/attempts under pending/ in the store, never swept; the IO error names the temporary file instead of the target

**Mechanism.** write_atomic bead:227-237 creates `.{name}.{pid}.tmp` (231-232) and has no cleanup if fh.write/fsync (EFBIG, ENOSPC, EIO) or os.replace (EISDIR when the target is a directory) raises. cmd_export (2048, private=False) puts it in the caller's checkout with umask modes, holding the tracker's content; coordinated (1918-1919) leaves it under pending/. No later write removes it (the name carries the pid) and nothing reads it (globs `*.jsonl`, `claims/*.json`, `*.json`, `*.command` never match), so it is inert clutter in the store but an untracked plaintext copy in the checkout that `git add -A` would commit. run() (2869-2870) reports `error.filename`, which for a failed os.replace is the source, so the user is told about a file they never named. A SIGKILL between write and rename also leaves a copy; no in-process fix covers that (measured in modes-table.md).

**Reproduction.** Sandbox, `bead init --prefix ex`, 6 x `bead create`; `mkdir out; bead export -o out --json`; `sh -c 'ulimit -f 1 && exec bead export -o big.jsonl --json'`; `sh -c 'ulimit -f 16 && exec bead update KEY --claim --description-file <100 KB file> --json'` then `bead sync`; list `.*.tmp` in the checkout and the store. Scripts: work-store-files/probe.py g48, sweep.py steps 7-8; test: G48.py.

**Observed.** `export -o out (a directory): rc 1 {"error": {"code": "IO", "message": "Is a directory: .out.77823.tmp"}}` / `export -o out (human): rc 1 Error: Is a directory: .out.77892.tmp` / `checkout leftovers: ['.out.77823.tmp', '.out.77892.tmp']` / git status: ['?? .out.77823.tmp', '?? .out.77892.tmp', ...]. Sweep, umask 022: `export -o under RLIMIT_FSIZE=200: rc 1 {"error": {"code": "IO", "message": "[Errno 27] File too large"}}` leaving `-rw-r--r-- .limited.jsonl.<pid>.tmp`. Store: test run left `pending/.01a11802-092b-7488-9e1d-4315e65c745a.command.20497.tmp` after the failed claim and a later successful sync.

**Expected.** A failed write leaves nothing beside its target (F16: 'nothing half-written'), the target is unchanged, and the error names the path the user gave ('Is a directory: out').

**Proposed fix.** write_atomic wraps write/fsync/rename in try/except BaseException, unlinks the temporary file (suppressing OSError) and re-raises; run() names `error.filename2 or error.filename`, so a failed rename names its target. Patched: `{"error": {"code": "IO", "message": "Is a directory: out"}}`, `Error: Is a directory: out`, no leftovers in checkout or store. A SIGKILL before the rename still leaves a copy (no in-process fix). The EFBIG message still names no path ('[Errno 27] File too large'). replica_id's own `.replica.PID.tmp` (249-259) is untouched (it is removed in a finally around os.link; a write failure there would still leak it). Generated with `diff -U2` so that it and G47.diff apply in either order with `git apply`.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G48_FailedAtomicWriteLeavesNothingBehind` (3 tests). On e9eed92: FAIL test_g48_export_onto_a_directory_leaves_no_copy_and_names_the_target: `AssertionError: Lists differ: ['.out.20176.tmp'] != []`; FAIL test_g48_export_that_cannot_be_written_whole_leaves_nothing: `AssertionError: Lists differ: ['.big.jsonl.20325.tmp'] != []`; FAIL test_g48_failed_store_write_leaves_no_temporary_file: `AssertionError: Lists differ: ['pending/.01a11802-092b-7488-9e1d-4315e65c745a.command.20497.tmp'] != []`. Ran 3 tests, FAILED (failures=3). With the diff: Ran 3 tests in 2.828s OK (G48 alone); OK on the combined copy. Checker: pristine 3 failures and patched OK reproduced.

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Against this diff alone: G48 test_storage rc=0 20s: Ran 9 tests in 19.249s OK ; G48 test_commands rc=0 236s: Ran 97 tests in 236.335s OK . Combined copy (all six store-files diffs, applied in order G04 G35 G47 G48 G50 G52 with `git apply`): ALL test_storage rc=0 24s: Ran 9 tests in 22.895s OK ; ALL test_protocol rc=0 212s: Ran 54 tests in 211.977s OK ; ALL test_commands rc=0 304s: Ran 97 tests in 304.071s OK ; ALL test_replay rc=0 114s: Ran 60 tests in 113.549s OK . No shipped test needed adapting. Not run: test_compat (its real-exports test looks for infra/compat_real.py beside BEAD_UNDER_TEST and reads the owner's real tracker exports) and test_stress (slow models). test_storage.py:272 expects a `.replica.*.tmp` after an interrupted replica write; replica_id is not touched. Independent check (check-store-files, Python 3.14.7, git 2.55.0): all six diffs apply with `patch -p1` to fresh pristine copies, singly and combined in either order (G04..G52 and G52..G04 give identical files). Combined copy (with the revised G47.diff): test_storage Ran 9 OK; test_commands Ran 97 in 254s OK; test_replay Ran 60 in 161s OK; test_protocol Ran 54 OK when run alone (120s). In a parallel run at load average ~185, test_protocol had 1 failure, test_timed_out_accepted_release_returns_success (BEAD_NET_TIMEOUT=0.1 timing test, also seen flaking by the triage-33 verifier); that test passed 3/3 alone…

```diff
-    with os.fdopen(fd, "wb") as fh:
```

#### G49 · Low · robustness — A non-string remote in meta.json, or a replica.json that is valid JSON but not an object, ends in a Python traceback (info, sync, claim, release, an owner's close after it was recorded; claims, machines, log)

**Mechanism.** Workspace.meta (bead:143-148) only checks that meta.json is an object, so {"remote": 5} reaches Workspace.remote, where re.fullmatch(r"[A-Za-z0-9._-]+", value) (bead:168) raises TypeError; main (bead:2855-2870) catches only BpError and OSError. publish_soon resolves the remote at bead:1945, after transition() has already written the close (bead:2285), so the close is recorded and then the command dies. machine_aliases (bead:269-279) suppresses OSError, ValueError, KeyError and TypeError, but own.get('id') on a list/int/None/str raises AttributeError (bead:273).

**Reproduction.** a: `bead init --prefix ex`; `bead create x`; `bead sync`; write '{"remote": 5}' (or ["x"], true, {"a": 1}) to <BEAD_HOME>/<ws>/meta.json; run `bead info|sync|claim X|release X --json`; restore, `bead claim X`, write '{"remote": 5}', `bead close X`. Write '["x"]' (or 5, null, "s") to <BEAD_HOME>/replica.json; run `bead claims|machines|log --json`.

**Observed.** File ".../bead", line 168, in remote: if re.fullmatch(r"[A-Za-z0-9._-]+", value) ... TypeError: expected string or bytes-like object, got 'int' (also 'list', 'bool', 'dict'; 17 commands, including close via line 2285 -> publish_soon line 1945); File ".../bead", line 273, in machine_aliases ... AttributeError: 'list' object has no attribute 'get' (also 'int', 'NoneType', 'str'; 12 commands).

**Expected.** No traceback: a structured error naming the file, or the value ignored. A non-string remembered remote must not be silently replaced by origin (that changes the coordination endpoint), and a close that was recorded must not be reported as failed.

**Proposed fix.** remote(): a resolved value that is not a string (only meta.json can supply one) raises CONFIG naming meta.json and the remedy. machine_aliases(): `isinstance(own, dict) and ...`. publish_soon(): resolve the remote inside the try, so an owner's close is reported as 'recorded locally, not yet published (CONFIG)' with exit 0. This is the verifier's narrower diff minus its meta() filtering of non-string name/last_sync (cosmetic: they are only displayed, no traceback). Not covered here, and covered by G03 instead: a falsy non-string remote (0, false, []) still falls through to origin silently.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G49_LocalStateShapesTraceback` (2 tests). On e9eed92: FAILED (failures=29): e.g. AssertionError: 'Traceback' unexpectedly found in 'Traceback ... line 168, in remote ... TypeError: expected string or bytes-like object, got 'int'' : meta={"remote": 5} ['info']; ... line 273, in machine_aliases ... AttributeError: 'list' object has no attribute 'get' : replica=["x"] ['claims'] With the diff: Ran 2 tests, OK (also OK against the combined group copy).

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Against work-remote-selection/G49/bead (G49.diff alone): tests.test_commands 'Ran 97 tests in 271.924s OK' (test_f13 included); tests.test_storage 'Ran 9 tests in 16.686s OK'. No shipped test needs adapting. Load average was 80-180 during these runs; no flakes seen. #### G51 · Low · robustness — A .beadroll.json that is a looping or dangling symlink is silently ignored (workspace falls back to the origin-derived one); an empty "workspace":"" is likewise accepted and ignored

**Mechanism.** find_workspace gates the config read on (root/'.beadroll.json').exists() (bead:199). Path.exists() returns False for a symlink that is dangling (ENOENT) or looping (ELOOP), so the whole block is skipped and config = {}. The id then falls back to the origin-URL hash (bead:211-212) and the config's remote is dropped (bead:164). Separately, checked_config (bead:179-187) accepts an empty string, and bead:210 treats config.get('workspace') == '' as absent, so "workspace":"" silently means the origin-derived workspace.

**Reproduction.** Tracked config {"workspace":"w-tracked"}; init; create. Replace .beadroll.json with a looping symlink (os.symlink('.beadroll.json', cfg)) or a dangling one, and run `bead info`/`list`/`sync --json`. Separately, a fresh repo with .beadroll.json = {"workspace":""} and `bead info --json`.

**Observed.** `info` exits 0 reporting workspace '67b57471295a7be1' with workspace_source 'origin url' instead of 'w-tracked'; following bead's own `bead init` hint under a dangling link creates a second tracker and `sync` publishes refs/beads/journal to origin. Empty workspace: info exits 0 with workspace_source 'origin url'.

**Expected.** A .beadroll.json entry that exists as a directory entry but cannot be resolved or read is a CONFIG error naming the cause; an empty tracked `workspace`/`ref`/`remote` string is a CONFIG error.

**Proposed fix.** In find_workspace, before the exists() gate, treat a symlink that does not resolve (is_symlink() and not exists()) as a CONFIG error rather than 'no config'. In checked_config, reject an empty string for any str-typed key (workspace/ref/remote). A regular file, and a symlink that resolves to a file, are unaffected; help/prime/onboard still work because main catches BpError for workspace-free commands.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G51_UnusableTrackedConfigIsAnError` (3 tests). On e9eed92: AssertionError: Tuples differ: (0, None) != (1, 'CONFIG') : info with a loop .beadroll.json link: rc=0 workspace='67b57471295a7be1' (also: a second tracker published refs/beads/journal; and an empty tracked workspace was accepted, workspace_source='origin url') With the diff: OK (info/list/sync return CONFIG for loop and dangling links; following the init hint under a dangling link publishes nothing to origin; an empty workspace is refused with CONFIG)

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Checker, combined copy (G01 + corrected G02 + G13 + G51, all applied cleanly with patch -p1, no conflicts), run from a copy of tests/ with scratch BEAD_HOME/TMPDIR: run sequentially, tests.test_protocol: Ran 54 tests OK; tests.test_commands: Ran 97 tests OK. Also tests.test_storage: Ran 9 tests OK; tests.test_replay: Ran 60 tests OK. In an earlier run of six suites in parallel, test_protocol test_timed_out_accepted_release_returns_success failed on BOTH the pristine and the patched copy (timing under load) and test_commands HelpAndPrime hit a `git clone` exit 128 in setUp; both pass when rerun alone on both copies. tests.test_compat: 2 OK, 1 not runnable here (test_all_available_real_exports_and_br_bv_consumers looks for infra/compat_real.py next to the bead copy), unrelated to these diffs. No shipped test needs adapting.

#### G54 · Low · robustness — The stale-twin rule (F11) in Link.merge also deletes the receipt of the attempt the remote accepted, so the retry of that accepted command is not recognised

**Mechanism.** Link.merge 1683-1687: when our pending twin's bytes differ from the remote copy (1684) and the local claims file matches the remote (1685), merge calls drop_pending(self.ws, [read_attempt(target)]) (1686), which unlinks both pending/<id>.json and pending/<id>.command (1790-1794). The normal confirm path (1690-1694) unlinks only the .json and keeps the receipt for settle_commands (1805-1827). With the receipt gone, build runs against the accepted state and cmd_release raises NOT_OWNER. The precondition needs a crash between confirm_ownership and drop_pending (the Q03 window) plus local damage to the pending twin; no code path writes a differing twin.

**Reproduction.** tests.support Machine a: init, create X, claim X. In-process cmd_release(['release', X]) with drop_pending mocked to raise after confirm_ownership (claims file written; pending .json and .command left). Append b'\n' to the pending .json. Subprocess `release X --json`. Test: final/G54.py.

**Observed.** AssertionError: 1 != 0 : retry of the accepted release not recognised: {"error": {"code": "NOT_OWNER", "message": "ex-flfn is not claimed by codex:g54-twin-thread-0001@ec728a12"}}

**Expected.** The attempt was accepted, so the twin goes and the receipt stays, exactly as on the normal confirm path; the retry exits 0 (as it does without the damage).

**Proposed fix.** In that branch unlink only the twin (pending[attempt_id]); the receipt stays for settle_commands. F11's 'dropped, not forked' is unchanged (test_f11_stale_pending_twin_does_not_fork_a_matching_attempt passes).

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G54_StaleTwinRuleDropsTheReceipt`. On e9eed92: FAILED (failures=1): AssertionError: 1 != 0 : retry of the accepted release not recognised: ... "NOT_OWNER" ... With the diff: Ran 1 test ... OK (G54 copy); also OK on the combined copy.

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Against the G54 copy: test_commands 'Ran 97 tests in 243.692s OK' (includes test_f11_stale_pending_twin_does_not_fork_a_matching_attempt); test_storage 'Ran 9 tests in 16.644s OK'. No shipped test needs adapting. Suites were run from an unchanged scratch copy of the repo's tests/ (work-receipts/suite/tests), cwd in scratch, with BEAD_UNDER_TEST set to the patched copy and BEAD_HOME/TMPDIR under work-receipts/. With all six receipts diffs applied together (receipts-combined.diff, work-receipts/combined/bead): test_protocol 'Ran 54 tests in 130.036s OK', test_commands 'Ran 97 tests in 196.742s OK', test_storage 'Ran 9 tests in 13.007s OK', test_replay 'Ran 60 tests in 52.644s OK'; all six G test files run together as one module: 12 failures on e9eed92, 'Ran 12 tests ... OK' on the combined copy.

```diff

+++ b/bead
@@ -1683,7 +1683,7 @@
                     if attempt_id in pending:  # the remote has our attempt: it counts, whole
                         if pending[attempt_id].read_bytes() != theirs:
                             if target.exists() and target.read_bytes() == theirs:  # a leftover twin of a confirmed attempt
-                                drop_pending(self.ws, [read_attempt(target)])
```

#### G55 · Low · robustness — A receipt holding valid JSON that is not an object makes every coordinated command of every actor using that store crash with AttributeError, and sync never sweeps it

**Mechanism.** settle_commands 1812-1817: `recorded = loads_strict(receipt.read_text(...))` catches only OSError and ValueError (1814-1816, which unlink the receipt), then `recorded.get("actor")` (1817) raises AttributeError for a list, number, string, bool or null, before the actor filter. coordinated calls settle_commands (1898) for claim/takeover (2120), release (2158) and update --claim (2252); run() catches only BpError and OSError (2867-2870), so every such command of every actor in that store is a traceback. load_pending (690-702) sweeps only *.json and sync never calls settle_commands, so the receipt stays forever. bead itself only writes json.dumps of a dict (1802, 1918): damage or a foreign writer only.

**Reproduction.** tests.support Machine a: init, create X, Y, sync; write pending/01900000-0000-7000-8000-0000000000bb.command containing '[]'; `sync` (rc 0, receipt kept); `claim X --json` as another thread; `claim Y --json`. Test: final/G55.py.

**Observed.** claim rc 1, stderr traceback ending 'line 1817, in settle_commands\n if recorded.get("actor") != actor:\n ... AttributeError: 'list' object has no attribute 'get''

**Expected.** A receipt that is not a JSON object can answer nothing: it is removed as an unparsable one already is (1814-1816), and claims proceed.

**Proposed fix.** Treat a non-dict like an unreadable receipt: on OSError/ValueError set recorded = None, then unlink and continue when not isinstance(recorded, dict).

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G55_ReceiptThatIsNotAnObjectBlocksEveryClaim`. On e9eed92: FAILED (failures=1): AssertionError: 'Traceback' unexpectedly found in 'Traceback ... line 1817, in settle_commands ... AttributeError: 'list' object has no attribute 'get'' With the diff: Ran 1 test ... OK (G55 copy); also OK on the combined copy.

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Against the G55 copy: test_commands 'Ran 97 tests in 243.413s OK'. No shipped test needs adapting. Suites were run from an unchanged scratch copy of the repo's tests/ (work-receipts/suite/tests), cwd in scratch, with BEAD_UNDER_TEST set to the patched copy and BEAD_HOME/TMPDIR under work-receipts/. With all six receipts diffs applied together (receipts-combined.diff, work-receipts/combined/bead): test_protocol 'Ran 54 tests in 130.036s OK', test_commands 'Ran 97 tests in 196.742s OK', test_storage 'Ran 9 tests in 13.007s OK', test_replay 'Ran 60 tests in 52.644s OK'; all six G test files run together as one module: 12 failures on e9eed92, 'Ran 12 tests ... OK' on the combined copy.

```diff
--- a/bead
+++ b/bead
@@ -1812,6 +1812,8 @@
         try:
             recorded = loads_strict(receipt.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
         except (OSError, ValueError):
+            recorded = None
+        if not isinstance(recorded, dict):  # unreadable, or not a receipt bead wrote: it can answer nothing
             receipt.unlink(missing_ok=True)
             continue
         if recorded.get("actor") != actor:
```

#### G56 · Low · robustness — Reading '-' with standard input closed (`<&-`) crashes comments add -f -, create/update --description-file - and import - with AttributeError instead of a structured error

**Mechanism.** read_utf8 (bead:1271-1277) does `sys.stdin.buffer.read()` for '-' (1274). When fd 0 is closed at startup, CPython sets sys.stdin to None, so `.buffer` raises AttributeError. read_utf8 catches only UnicodeDecodeError, and run() (bead:2867-2870) catches only BpError/OSError. When fd 0 is open but unreadable (`0>/dev/null`) the same commands already give {"code": "IO"} (verifier 22). Section 2: 'OS errors are structured (IO)'. Nothing is written.

**Reproduction.** `sh -c 'bead comments add KEY -f - --json <&-'` (likewise `update KEY --description-file -`, `create t --description-file -`, `import -`).

**Observed.** e9eed92: rc 1, traceback `AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'buffer'` for all four commands.

**Expected.** rc 1 with a JSON error envelope (IO, like the unreadable-fd-0 case), nothing written; a piped stdin keeps working.

**Proposed fix.** read_utf8 raises BpError('IO', 'standard input is closed, so - cannot be read') when the path is '-' and sys.stdin is None. The test accepts IO or VALIDATION.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G56_ClosedStdinIsAStructuredError`. On e9eed92: FAILED (failures=4): `AssertionError: comments add -f - with stdin closed crashed: AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'buffer'` (likewise update/create --description-file -, import -). With the diff: Ran 1 test OK.

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Run from the repo root with BEAD_UNDER_TEST=<scratch>/tree-G56/bead (the patched copy, with infra/compat_real.py copied beside it so test_compat's real-export test runs) and a scratch BEAD_HOME/TMPDIR: tests.test_commands: Ran 97 tests OK. No shipped test needs adapting.

<details><summary>Proposed diff (G56, applies to e9eed92 with <code>patch -p1</code>)</summary>

```diff
--- a/bead
+++ b/bead
@@ -1270,6 +1270,8 @@
 
 def read_utf8(path: str, what: str) -> str:
     """A text argument from a file or stdin, decoded as UTF-8 whatever the locale says."""
+    if path == "-" and sys.stdin is None:  # fd 0 was closed when bead started
+        raise BpError("IO", "standard input is closed, so - cannot be read")
     try:
         raw = sys.stdin.buffer.read() if path == "-" else Path(path).read_bytes()
         return raw.decode("utf-8")
```
</details>

#### G57 · Low · robustness — A journal or attempt line whose text holds a lone surrogate replays unreported, then `export -o` dies with UnicodeEncodeError and `export` to stdout silently writes '?'

**Mechanism.** json.loads(bytes) decodes with 'surrogatepass', so an escaped \ud800, or raw bytes ED A0 80, becomes a lone surrogate. read_segment (bead:363-376) and read_attempt (434-458) accept it, and event_problem/payload_problem check only types. cmd_export -o encodes strictly (bead:2048). UnicodeEncodeError is a ValueError, but it is raised outside replay's guard, and run() (2867-2870) catches only BpError/OSError, so the result is a traceback. stdout was reconfigured with errors='replace' (bead:2821), so `export` to stdout writes '?' and the two exports disagree. `info` integrity stays empty. Tx.emit refuses such text (bead:1205-1208) and no bead version writes it, so only a hand-edited journal or a foreign writer produces it. Synced to other machines, it breaks `export -o` on every one of them. The reporter's fix (an encode check in event_problem) costs +24-36% replay (verifier 23), so it was not used.

**Reproduction.** After `bead init; bead create x`, write a create event whose fields.title is 'bad\ud800title' (json.dumps escapes it) to events/fffffff0/000001.jsonl, and the same with raw bytes ED A0 80 to events/fffffff1/000001.jsonl. Run `bead export -o out.jsonl --json`, `bead export`, `bead info --json`. In-process: bp.read_attempt() of a claim whose actor.thread is 't\ud800'.

**Observed.** e9eed92: `export -o` rc 1, traceback `UnicodeEncodeError: 'utf-8' codec can't encode character '\ud800' in position 243: surrogates not allowed` (the position depends on where the bad record falls in export order, which varies with the random key of the ordinary issue: an independent re-run saw position 27). read_attempt returns an event that cannot be written: `'utf-8' codec can't encode character '\ud800' in position 131: surrogates not allowed`.

**Expected.** The text is scoped: reported in `info` integrity and readable on every machine. `export -o` and `export` to stdout succeed and write the same bytes.

**Proposed fix.** New loads_line() used by read_segment and read_attempt. When a line could hold a lone surrogate (a raw 0xED byte or a \uD800-\uDFFF escape), each lone surrogate is replaced by U+FFFD, deterministically, and reported as 'text is not valid UTF-8; it reads as U+FFFD' with the event id and line. The event still applies, so no chain breaks, no issue disappears and no claim is lost. read_segment checks the whole segment once first, so ordinary segments pay one regex search: about 2 ms per 8 MB (20k events) measured on this heavily loaded host, against hundreds of ms for replay. Caveat: these line-level reports, like the existing 'line is not valid JSON' reports, are dropped from integrity when Journal.settle rebuilds (see extra findings).

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G57_LoneSurrogateInAJournalIsScoped` (2 tests). On e9eed92: FAILED (failures=2): `AssertionError: export -o crashed: UnicodeEncodeError: 'utf-8' codec can't encode character '\ud800' in position 243: surrogates not allowed` (the position depends on where the bad record falls in export order, which varies with the random key of the ordinary issue: an independent re-run saw position 27) and `AssertionError: "'utf-8' codec can't encode character '\\ud800' in position 131: surrogates not allowed" is not None : an attempt event read back holds text that cannot be written`. With the diff: Ran 2 tests OK.

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Run from the repo root with BEAD_UNDER_TEST=<scratch>/tree-G57/bead (the patched copy, with infra/compat_real.py copied beside it so test_compat's real-export test runs) and a scratch BEAD_HOME/TMPDIR: tests.test_replay: Ran 60 tests OK; tests.test_storage: Ran 9 tests OK; tests.test_commands: Ran 97 tests OK. No shipped test needs adapting.

<details><summary>Proposed diff (G57, applies to e9eed92 with <code>patch -p1</code>)</summary>

```diff
--- a/bead
+++ b/bead
@@ -358,16 +358,40 @@
 
 def canonical(event: dict) -> str:
     return json.dumps(event, sort_keys=True, ensure_ascii=False, separators=(",", ":"))
+
+
+LONE_SURROGATE = re.compile("[\ud800-\udfff]")
+SURROGATE_ESCAPE = re.compile(rb"\\u[dD][89a-fA-F]")
+
+
+def suspect(data: bytes) -> bool:
+    """Whether bytes could hold a lone surrogate: only a raw 0xED byte or a \\uD800-\\uDFFF escape can carry one."""
+    return b"\xed" in data or SURROGATE_ESCAPE.search(data) is not None
+
+
+def loads_line(line: bytes, origin: str, number: int, problems: list | None):
+    """One journal or attempt line. Text that is not valid UTF-8 (a lone surrogate, escaped or as raw
+    bytes; bead refuses to write one) can be neither printed nor exported: it reads as U+FFFD, reported."""
+    event = loads_strict(line)
+    if suspect(line):
+        text = json.dumps(event, ensure_ascii=False)
+        if LONE_SURROGATE.search(text):
+            event = json.loads(LONE_SURROGATE.sub("\ufffd", text))
+            if problems is not None:
+                eid = event.get("id") if isinstance(event, dict) else None
+                problems.append({"event": eid if isinstance(eid, str) else None, "origin": f"{origin}:{number + 1}",
+                                 "problem": "text is not valid UTF-8; it reads as U+FFFD"})
+    return event
 
 
 def read_segment(data: bytes, origin: str = "", problems: list | None = None) -> list:
     """Parse one segment. A torn tail is skipped; any other unreadable line is reported and skipped."""
-    events, lines = [], data.split(b"\n")
+    events, lines, checked = [], data.split(b"\n"), suspect(data)
     for number, line in enumerate(lines):
         if not line.strip():
             continue
         try:
-            events.append(loads_strict(line))
+            events.append(loads_line(line, origin, number, problems) if checked else loads_strict(line))
         except ValueError:
             if number == len(lines) - 1:
                 break  # torn tail: another process is mid-append
@@ -444,7 +468,7 @@
         if not line.strip():
             continue
         try:
-            event = loads_strict(line)
+            event = loads_line(line, str(path), number, problems)
             problem = None if isinstance(event, dict) else "attempt line is not an event"
         except ValueError:
             event, problem = None, "line is not valid JSON"
```
</details>

#### G58 · Low · robustness — run_git waits for end-of-file on git's pipes, not for git: a helper's background child holding git's stderr turns a finished, applied push into 'the push got no answer'

**Mechanism.** run_git (bead:77-101) starts git with stdout and stderr on pipes (bead:82-83) and calls communicate(timeout) (bead:85), which returns only at EOF on both pipes. A descendant of git that inherited its stderr (a transport's or a hook's background job) keeps the pipe open after git exits; communicate raises TimeoutExpired at NET_TIMEOUT, and bead:86-96 kills the already-finished git and throws away its exit status and output, raising REMOTE_UNREACHABLE 'git push gave no answer'. publish then answers 'uncertain' for a push git reported applied (sync: REMOTE_UNREACHABLE 'the push got no answer'), a lost race becomes 'uncertain' instead of 'rejected' (claim: UNRESOLVED after 2 x NET_TIMEOUT), and a fetch becomes REMOTE_UNREACHABLE.

**Reproduction.** Sandbox; GIT_SSH_COMMAND=a fake ssh that execs git-upload-pack/git-receive-pack locally and, for receive-pack, first starts `(sleep 15) &` (it inherits stderr); BEAD_REMOTE=ssh://fakehost<origin>, BEAD_NET_TIMEOUT=4. init, create, sync (with sleep 0), comments add, then `bead sync --json` with the 15 s background job. G58.py.

**Observed.** 'AssertionError: 1 != 0 : git finished and the remote applied the push, but after 4.4s bead said: {"error": {"code": "REMOTE_UNREACHABLE", "message": "the push got no answer; nothing was lost, run sync again"}}' (the preceding assertion that the remote head moved passed).

**Expected.** git's exit status decides at once: sync exits 0 well within the timeout.

**Proposed fix.** git's stdout and stderr go to unlinked temporary files (stdin too when data is given), and bead waits for git itself in a daemon thread (child.wait() is a blocking waitpid; Popen.wait(timeout) would loop on time.sleep, which shipped tests replace with callbacks). On a timeout git is killed as before (with its process group when reap=True). The output is read with os.pread at offset 0, without moving the file offset that git's children share. The reporter's diff reads with seek(0)+read(): a child still running then writes at the offset bead just reset. Shown with a late writer after the seek: the reporter's run_git returned b'journal\n' for "fatal: couldn't find remote ref refs/beads/journal\n", so the first sync's 'find remote ref' check fails; I also saw it twice in this test's first sync under load ('cannot reach the coordination remote ...: journal\nfatal: the remote end hung up unexpectedly'). With pread, all 16 runs of this test after the change passed (some under load from parallel suites). Cost: every git call now needs a writable temporary directory and creates two or three temporary files.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G58_FinishedPushIsNotAnUnansweredOne`. On e9eed92: FAIL test_finished_push_is_not_reported_unanswered_when_a_helper_keeps_stderr: AssertionError: 1 != 0 : git finished and the remote applied the push, but after 4.2s bead said: {"error": {"code": "REMOTE_UNREACHABLE", "message": "the push got no answer; nothing was lost, run sync again"}} With the diff: Ran 1 test in 0.813s OK (also OK on the combined copy)

**Shipped suites with the diff.** G58 copy: tests.test_protocol Ran 54 tests in 117.572s OK; tests.test_storage Ran 9 tests OK. No shipped test needs a change. All five diffs applied together (push-outcomes-combined.diff, bead + tests/test_protocol.py): three textual conflicts when applied one after another with patch(1), resolved by hand in the combined diff: G16 x G41 (both edit Link.fetch's failure branch; G41's hunk fails after G16), G11 x G18 (both rewrite Link.publish's classification; combined: --porcelain status line decides, '[remote rejected]' with a non-race reason is 'refused', last_error = status summary + git's stderr), G41 x G18 (both add a line to PRIME's error codes; combined lists REMOTE_REFUSED and REMOTE_DAMAGED). Combined copy (bead from push-outcomes-combined.diff; tests from the same diff, i.e. tests/test_protocol.py with the G11 and G18 hunks): tests.test_protocol Ran 54 tests in 203.591s OK; tests.test_commands Ran 97 tests in 248.353s OK; tests.test_storage Ran 9 tests OK; tests.test_replay Ran 60 tests OK; tests.test_review7 (the lead's sweep slice, untracked file as of now) Ran 1 test OK. All five G tests and the triage tests of sources 01, 02, 03, 05 and 50 pass on the combined copy. Not run: test_compat, test_stress.

<details><summary>Proposed diff (G58, applies to e9eed92 with <code>patch -p1</code>)</summary>

```diff
--- a/bead
+++ b/bead
@@ -29,6 +29,8 @@
 import socket
 import subprocess
 import sys
+import tempfile
+import threading
 import time
 import uuid
 from datetime import datetime, timezone
@@ -78,23 +80,33 @@
     """Run git. On a timeout, `reap` kills git together with the helpers it started (a fetch has
     nothing in flight worth keeping). A push passes `reap=False`: only the client is killed, so
     whatever the receiving side is doing finishes or fails on its own; which one is the uncertain
-    outcome the ownership protocol then settles."""
-    child = subprocess.Popen(["git", *args], env=env, stdin=subprocess.PIPE if data is not None else subprocess.DEVNULL,
-                             stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.PIPE, start_new_session=True)
-    try:
-        out, err = child.communicate(input=data, timeout=timeout)
-    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
-        with contextlib.suppress(ProcessLookupError):
-            if reap:
-                os.killpg(child.pid, signal.SIGKILL)
-            else:
-                child.kill()
-        child.wait()  # not communicate(): a receiving side still holding our pipes must not hold us
-        for stream in (child.stdin, child.stdout, child.stderr):
-            if stream:
-                stream.close()
-        raise BpError("REMOTE_UNREACHABLE", f"git {args[0]} gave no answer within {timeout:.0f}s") from None
-    proc = subprocess.CompletedProcess(child.args, child.returncode, out, err)
+    outcome the ownership protocol then settles.
+
+    git's answer is its exit status, so bead waits for git, not for the end of its output: that goes
+    to unlinked temporary files, because a helper git started (a hook's or a transport's background
+    job) may keep git's stderr open long after git has exited."""
+    with contextlib.ExitStack() as files:
+        out, err = files.enter_context(tempfile.TemporaryFile()), files.enter_context(tempfile.TemporaryFile())
+        stdin = subprocess.DEVNULL
+        if data is not None:
+            stdin = files.enter_context(tempfile.TemporaryFile())
+            stdin.write(data)
+            stdin.seek(0)
+        child = subprocess.Popen(["git", *args], env=env, stdin=stdin, stdout=out, stderr=err, start_new_session=True)
+        waiter = threading.Thread(target=child.wait, daemon=True)  # a blocking wait, not a loop of sleeps
+        waiter.start()
+        waiter.join(timeout)
+        if waiter.is_alive():
+            with contextlib.suppress(ProcessLookupError):
+                if reap:
+                    os.killpg(child.pid, signal.SIGKILL)
+                else:
+                    child.kill()
+            waiter.join()
+            raise BpError("REMOTE_UNREACHABLE", f"git {args[0]} gave no answer within {timeout:.0f}s") from None
+        # Read without moving the file offset, which git's children share: one still running writes on after it.
+        captured = [os.pread(f.fileno(), os.fstat(f.fileno()).st_size, 0) for f in (out, err)]
+        proc = subprocess.CompletedProcess(child.args, child.returncode, *captured)
     if check and proc.returncode:
         raise BpError("GIT_ERROR", f"git {' '.join(args[:2])}: {proc.stderr.decode(errors='replace').strip()}")
     return proc
```
</details>

#### G63 · Low · robustness — Journal.settle() discards the line-level integrity reports read_segment and read_attempt made, whenever it rebuilds: one damaged line is reported or not depending on whether any duplicate, malformed or misplaced record is also present

**Mechanism.** load_journal (bead:678-687) passes journal.invalid to read_segment (bead:363-376) and read_attempt (bead:434-457), which append 'line is not valid JSON' / attempt problems to it. Journal.add (bead:611-624) sets _settled=False for any duplicate id, malformed event or misplaced copy, and settle() (bead:632-670) then rebuilds with `self.invalid = list(self._unidentified)` (bead:639), dropping every report that did not come from add().

**Reproduction.** Machine a: init, create one. Write events/fffffff0/000001.jsonl = 'not json\n{}\n' in a's store: `bead info --json` lists 'line is not valid JSON'. Replace '{}' with a copy of a's first own event line: `bead info --json` integrity is [].

**Observed.** AssertionError: 'line is not valid JSON' not found in [] : the damaged line is no longer reported once a copied event forces a rebuild: []

**Expected.** A line bead cannot read is reported by info and counted by sync whatever else the journals hold.

**Proposed fix.** settle() keeps the entries it did not produce: `self.invalid = [entry for entry in self.invalid if entry['event'] is None]` (unidentified records and unreadable lines/files carry event None; everything settle() derives names its event).

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G63_SettleKeepsLineLevelIntegrityReports`. On e9eed92: AssertionError: 'line is not valid JSON' not found in [] : the damaged line is no longer reported once a copied event forces a rebuild: [] With the diff: Ran 1 test OK

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Each alone and G63+G64+G65 applied together (patch -p1, no conflicts), run from the repo root with scratch BEAD_HOME/TMPDIR: G63+G64: tests.test_replay Ran 60 OK, tests.test_storage Ran 9 OK, tests.test_protocol Ran 54 OK, tests.test_commands Ran 97 OK; G63+G64+G65: tests.test_protocol Ran 54 OK, tests.test_commands Ran 97 OK. No shipped test needs adapting.

```diff
+++ b/bead

         if self._settled:
             return
         self.events, self.order, self.quarantined, self._referenced = {}, {}, {}, set()
-        self.invalid = list(self._unidentified)
+        self.invalid = [entry for entry in self.invalid if entry["event"] is None]  # unreadable lines and files stay reported
         placed = []
         for eid, records in self._raw.items():
             own = [r for r in records if event_problem(r[0]) is None and r[0]["replica"] == r[1]]
```

#### G37 · Low · usability — With --json, an owner's close/delete/update --status closed whose publish failed answers exactly like a published one: the 'recorded locally, not yet published' warning is dropped

**Mechanism.** publish_soon (bead:1943-1952) returns the warning string; transition (2285-2286) and cmd_update (2257-2263) only add it to the human `lines`/`notes`. run() (2875-2876) prints `result` alone for --json, so the warning is lost; stderr is empty and the exit status 0. PRIME tells agents to use --json and that close 'publishes at once'.

**Reproduction.** a: init, create X and Y, sync, claim X and Y; rename the bare origin away; `close X --reason done` (text) and `close Y --reason done --json`; rename it back.

**Observed.** Pristine: text: 0 '✓ Closed gj-92os: one\nrecorded locally, not yet published (REMOTE_UNREACHABLE)'; json: 0, stderr '', keys ['assignee', 'branch', 'created_at', 'id', 'issue_type', 'priority', 'status', 'title', 'updated_at'], nothing about publishing.

**Expected.** The --json answer carries the same information as the text answer.

**Proposed fix.** When publish_soon returns a warning, each issue object in the answer gets "unpublished": "recorded locally, not yet published (CODE)" (the human text, unchanged). Backward compatible: the answer is still the same list of issue objects with the same keys; the new key appears only when the publish failed, so a published close is byte-for-byte unchanged and consumers that ignore unknown keys are unaffected. Rejected: wrapping the list in an object (breaks every consumer); a warning on stderr (in --json mode stderr carries {"error": ...} only on failure, and exit-0 stderr output confuses wrappers that treat stderr as failure). Per item because the list is the whole answer, and the sync publishes all of the command's writes or none. Also covers G29's new '(IO: ...)' note.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G37_JsonSaysWhenTheEndOfAClaimWasNotPublished`. On e9eed92: Ran 1 test, FAILED (failures=3): "AssertionError: 'not yet published' not found in '' : close --json: the publish failed, yet the answer carries no sign of it (stdout '[{"id": "gj-byh0", "title": "closed", "status": "closed", ...', stderr '')" and the same for delete and update. With the diff: G37.diff alone: Ran 1 test in 6.119s OK; all five pending-io diffs: Ran 1 test in 5.616s OK.

The lead's LeadClose tests (test_lead.py, 3 tests) pass on the combined copy and all fail on pristine. Sweep (tests/test_review7.py run_schedule): the 24 close/delete x crash-before-push schedules, which include the 10 G12_SCHEDULES, 0 failures on the combined copy; the 10 G12_SCHEDULES on pristine: 10 failures ('UNPUBLISHED R close ... -> rc 0 (json carries no warning): remote status in_progress, local status closed'). +        for r in result:
```diff
+            r["unpublished"] = warning  # --json is told too: the other machines do not know yet
     return result, [f"Updated {r['id']}: {r['title']}" for r in result] + notes

             result = [brief(st, st.resolve(key)) for key in args.ids]
```

#### G45 · Low · usability — An imported dependency on a key that is not an issue yet is invisible to show (JSON and human) and dep list, although dep remove accepts it and it blocks the issue as soon as the key is imported

**Mechanism.** detail() (bead 1489-1505 at e9eed92) builds 'dependencies' only from issue['deps'] (1501-1503); human show prints only item['dependencies'] (2319-2320); dep list reuses detail() (2462-2465). issue['dangling'] is read only by materialise_dangling, apply dep_remove (1105-1107), export_records (1574), import_records (2006) and cmd_dep remove (2424, 2435).

**Reproduction.** `init --prefix ex`; import ex-a whose dependencies name ex-b (not an issue); `show ex-a --json`, `show ex-a`, `dep list ex-a [--json]`, `ready`, `export`. work-dangling/observe.py; test in G45.py (the record lists ex-b twice, once with type null, to check dedup).

**Observed.** Pristine: show --json "dependencies: [] | keys: ['blocked', 'comments', 'created_at', 'created_by', 'dependencies', 'dependents', 'id', ...]"; human show '○ ex-a · EX-A [P2 · OPEN]\n last worked on branch main (ae053b0dcbc1)\n type task · created 2026-01-01 by seed\n'; dep list --json {'dependencies': [], 'dependents': []}, human 'No dependencies'; ready ['ex-a']; export deps [{'issue_id': 'ex-a', 'depends_on_id': 'ex-b', 'type': 'blocks'}].

**Expected.** show and dep list report pending imported dependencies (e.g. unresolved_dependencies [{id, dependency_type}], deduplicated), and human show prints 'depends on (blocks): ex-b (not an issue yet)'.

**Proposed fix.** detail() adds unresolved_dependencies (sorted, deduplicated (depends_on_id, type or 'blocks')) when issue['dangling'] is non-empty; human show and dep list (JSON and text) print them. Additive JSON key, present only when there is something to show. Patched human show: ' depends on (blocks): ex-b (not an issue yet)'; dep list --json gains 'unresolved_dependencies': [{'id': 'ex-b', 'dependency_type': 'blocks'}]. dep tree is left as is (it shows issues only).

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G45_UnresolvedImportedDependencyInvisible`. On e9eed92: FAILED (failures=1): AssertionError: 'ex-b' not found in '{"id": "ex-a", ... "dependencies": [], "dependents": [], ...}' : show --json hides the pending imported dependency With the diff: Ran 1 test in 0.958s OK (work-dangling/G45/bead); also OK on combined

#### G53 · Low · usability — An unreadable replica.json (EACCES) is reported as IDENTITY with advice to mint a new machine identity, which would orphan this machine's claims

**Mechanism.** replica_id (bead:242-266): one try block covers path.read_text and the parse and maps (OSError, ValueError, KeyError, TypeError) to IDENTITY '<path> is unreadable; restore it, or move it aside to mint a new machine identity' (266). actor_label (330-334) embeds the replica id, so a new identity is a different owner of every claim this machine holds (CLAIMED_BY_OTHER / NOT_OWNER afterwards).

**Reproduction.** a: init, create X, claim X; chmod 000 $BEAD_HOME/replica.json; `bead close X --json`; chmod 600; `bead close X --json`.

**Observed.** Pristine: '{"error": {"code": "IDENTITY", "message": ".../home-a/replica.json is unreadable; restore it, or move it aside to mint a new machine identity"}}'. Source 43: following the advice gives 'CLAIMED_BY_OTHER ... is claimed by codex:adv-thread-0001@ad2db0d0' on close and NOT_OWNER on release.

**Expected.** An OS error reading the identity is IO naming the file, and says not to replace it; only a readable but invalid identity is IDENTITY.

**Proposed fix.** Read replica.json as bytes in its own try: OSError -> IO '<strerror>: <path> (fix its access; do not replace it: the claims this machine holds belong to that identity)'. Decode and parse in the second try, so a non-UTF-8 file is still IDENTITY (the reporter's diff turned it into a UnicodeDecodeError traceback; the guard test covers it). The IDENTITY message now says 'is not a valid machine identity' and that claims held under the old identity then need `bead takeover`.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G53_UnreadableIdentityIsIo` (2 tests). On e9eed92: Ran 2 tests, FAILED (failures=1): "AssertionError: Tuples differ: ('IDENTITY', True) != ('IO', False) : a permission problem is reported as a damaged identity: {"error": {"code": "IDENTITY", "message": ".../home-a/replica.json is unreadable; restore it, or move it aside to mint a new machine identity"}}". The guard test (non-UTF-8 replica.json stays IDENTITY) passes on pristine and patched by design. With the diff: G53.diff alone: Ran 2 tests in 3.156s OK; all five pending-io diffs: Ran 2 tests in 3.409s OK.

#### G59 · Low · usability — On Python 3.14, argparse colours bead's help: `bead help CMD` on a terminal (or under FORCE_COLOR) shows literal \x1b[1;34m escape text, `--json help CMD` embeds ANSI codes, and `CMD --help` writes raw SGR to the terminal

**Mechanism.** class Parser(argparse.ArgumentParser) (bead:2674-2676) does not opt out of colour. Python 3.14's ArgumentParser defaults to color=True and _get_formatter() calls formatter._set_color(self.color), which colours when _colorize.can_colorize() holds (stdout is a TTY, or FORCE_COLOR / PYTHON_COLORS=1; NO_COLOR, PYTHON_COLORS=0 or TERM=dumb disable it). Subparsers inherit it through add_subparsers(parser_class=Parser) (bead:2681). cmd_help (bead:2632-2641) returns choices[name].format_help() split into lines; run() prints them through visible() (bead:2878), which escapes ESC, so the user sees '\x1b[1;34musage: \x1b[0m...'. With --json the raw codes are serialised into {"help": ...} (bead:2876). For `CMD --help`, argparse's print_help writes the coloured text directly. OVERVIEW and PRIME send agents to `bead help COMMAND`. Python 3.12/3.13 argparse has no colour, so the bug is 3.14-only (README supports 3.12+).

**Reproduction.** In a sandbox repo with python3 = 3.14.7: run `bead help create` with stdout on a pseudo-terminal (TERM=xterm-256color, no colour env vars); `FORCE_COLOR=1 bead help create | head -1`; `FORCE_COLOR=1 bead --json help create`; `bead create --help` on a pseudo-terminal. Test: G59.py.

**Observed.** pristine e9eed92, Python 3.14.7: on a PTY `help create` printed '\\x1b[1;34musage: \\x1b[0m\\x1b[1;35mbead create\\x1b[0m [\\x1b[32m-h\\x1b[0m] [\\x1b[36m--title \\x1b[33mTI...' (literal backslash escapes); `create --help` on a PTY wrote '\x1b[1;34musage: \x1b[0m\x1b[1;35mbead create\x1b[0m ...' (raw ESC); FORCE_COLOR=1 through a pipe gave the same escaped text as the PTY. Also: the shipped help tests fail on pristine when FORCE_COLOR=1 is set (`FORCE_COLOR=1 python3 -m unittest tests.test_commands.HelpAndPrime tests.test_commands.Review5PositiveControls`: FAILED (failures=28)); with the patch: OK. Under Python 3.12.10 the test skips (no argparse colour) and the patched bead prints plain help.

**Expected.** Help is plain text in every environment, as through a pipe and on 3.12: no escaped colour codes in `help CMD`, no ANSI codes inside --json, no terminal control sequences from `CMD --help`.

**Proposed fix.** Parser.__init__ calls super().__init__ and then sets self.color = False. Setting the attribute (rather than passing color=False) keeps 3.12/3.13 working, where the keyword does not exist; 3.14 reads self.color each time it builds a formatter, and add_subparsers/add_parser propagate it to every subcommand parser.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G59_HelpIsPlainText` (3 tests). On e9eed92: FAILED (failures=3), e.g. "AssertionError: help on a terminal shows escaped colour codes: '\\x1b[1;34musage: \\x1b[0m\\x1b[1;35mbead create\\x1b[0m ...'" and "AssertionError: create --help on a terminal wrote escape sequences: '\x1b[1;34musage: ...'" With the diff: Ran 3 tests in 0.768s OK (Python 3.14.7). With python3 = 3.12.10 first on PATH: Ran 3 tests OK (skipped=3), 'the python3 that runs bead has no argparse colour (before 3.14)'.

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Run against one copy with all four output-group diffs applied (G36, G59, G60, G61 in that order; patch -p1 applied each cleanly, no conflicts), Python 3.14.7, from the repo root with BEAD_UNDER_TEST set to that copy and scratch BEAD_HOME/TMPDIR: tests.test_commands 'Ran 97 tests in 153.538s OK'; tests.test_protocol 'Ran 54 tests in 153.301s OK'; tests.test_storage 'Ran 9 tests in 23.329s OK'; tests.test_replay 'Ran 60 tests in 121.485s OK'. No shipped test needs adapting. Also, under FORCE_COLOR=1: tests.test_commands.HelpAndPrime + tests.test_commands.Review5PositiveControls give 'FAILED (failures=28)' on pristine e9eed92 and 'OK' on the G59 copy (the shipped help tests themselves break in a FORCE_COLOR CI environment on 3.14).

<details><summary>Proposed diff (G59, applies to e9eed92 with <code>patch -p1</code>)</summary>

```diff
--- a/bead
+++ b/bead
@@ -2672,6 +2672,10 @@
 
 
 class Parser(argparse.ArgumentParser):
+    def __init__(self, *args, **kwargs):
+        super().__init__(*args, **kwargs)
+        self.color = False  # Python 3.14 colours help on a terminal or under FORCE_COLOR; bead's help is plain text
+
     def error(self, message):
         raise BpError("USAGE", message)
```
</details>

#### G60 · Low · usability — U+061C ARABIC LETTER MARK, the one Unicode Bidi_Control character missing from CONTROLS, reaches the terminal raw in human output and error messages

**Mechanism.** CONTROLS (bead:2809) is [\x00-\x1f\x7f-\x9f\u2028\u2029\u200e\u200f\u202a-\u202e\u2066-\u2069]. Unicode's Bidi_Control set is {U+061C, U+200E, U+200F, U+202A-U+202E, U+2066-U+2069}; U+061C (ALM, an invisible strong right-to-left mark) is the only member missing. visible() (bead:2812-2815) is the single escaping path for human output (run, bead:2878) and for error messages (report_error, bead:2890), so ALM in a title, description, comment or an id argument is printed raw on pipes and terminals alike, while RLM (U+200F) next to it is shown as \u200f. --json keeps raw text by design (section 2), and is unaffected.

**Reproduction.** In a sandbox repo: bead init --prefix ex; bead create $'alm\u061cmark rlm\u200fmark' -d $'desc\u061cd'; bead list; bead show ex-XXXX; bead show $'ex-\u061czz' (the error path). Test: G60.py (support.Sandbox/Machine).

**Observed.** pristine e9eed92, Python 3.14.7: list printed '○ ex-n5ss [P2] [task] - alm\u061cmark rlm\\u200fmark (main)\n' (ALM raw, RLM escaped); show printed ' description: desc\u061cd'; the error path printed 'Error: Issue not found: ex-\u061czz\n' with ALM raw. (Here \u061c denotes the raw character in the output; \\u200f is bead's literal escape text.)

**Expected.** U+061C is shown as the literal text \u061c, like the other bidi controls (E01/F05 decided human output never hands the terminal a bidi control).

**Proposed fix.** Add \u061c to the CONTROLS character class (one-line change at bead:2809). Arabic letters, emoji and ZWJ handling are unchanged.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G60_ArabicLetterMarkEscaped`. On e9eed92: FAIL (4 subtests: create, list, show, error) e.g. "AssertionError: '\u061c' unexpectedly found in 'Error: Issue not found: ex-\u061czz\n' : error printed U+061C raw instead of as \u061c"; Ran 1 test, FAILED (failures=4) With the diff: Ran 1 test in 0.600s OK

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Run against one copy with all four output-group diffs applied (G36, G59, G60, G61 in that order; patch -p1 applied each cleanly, no conflicts), Python 3.14.7, from the repo root with BEAD_UNDER_TEST set to that copy and scratch BEAD_HOME/TMPDIR: tests.test_commands 'Ran 97 tests in 153.538s OK'; tests.test_protocol 'Ran 54 tests in 153.301s OK'; tests.test_storage 'Ran 9 tests in 23.329s OK'; tests.test_replay 'Ran 60 tests in 121.485s OK'. No shipped test needs adapting.

```diff

+++ b/bead
@@ -2806,7 +2806,7 @@
     return parser
 
 
-CONTROLS = re.compile("[\x00-\x1f\x7f-\x9f\u2028\u2029\u200e\u200f\u202a-\u202e\u2066-\u2069]")
+CONTROLS = re.compile("[\x00-\x1f\x7f-\x9f\u061c\u2028\u2029\u200e\u200f\u202a-\u202e\u2066-\u2069]")
```
