#### G61 · Low · usability — `show` reports a closing commit already on origin/main as 'NOT yet on the default branch' when the local main is stale, because the local branch is consulted first and decides alone; a default branch with a slash (release/main) is looked up as `main`

**Mechanism.** merged_into_default (bead:1452-1470) builds names = [last path component of origin/HEAD (bead:1460, rsplit('/', 1)[-1]), 'main', 'master'] and, for each name, tries the short refs `name` then `origin/{name}` (bead:1463), returning the merge-base --is-ancestor result of the FIRST ref that exists (bead:1464-1469). In a normal clone the local `main` always exists, so origin/main is consulted only when there is no local main; a stale local main (the usual state when work happens in worktrees and is merged on the server) returns 1, i.e. merged=False. detail() stores it (bead:1517-1519) and cmd_show prints ' · NOT yet on the default branch' (bead:2300-2303). Separately, bead:1460 reduces origin/HEAD -> refs/remotes/origin/release/main to 'main'. The result is used only by `show`: no effect on readiness, ownership or sync.

**Reproduction.** In a support.Sandbox: bead init; bead create one; git worktree add ../wt -b feat; commit a file in wt; (in wt) bead close ID -r done; (in wt) git push origin feat:main; (in the main checkout) git fetch origin (local main left stale); bead show ID. Slash variant: push feat:refs/heads/release/main, fetch, git symbolic-ref refs/remotes/origin/HEAD refs/remotes/origin/release/main, bead show ID. Test: G61.py.

**Observed.** pristine e9eed92: show --json gave merged False although `git merge-base --is-ancestor <closing> refs/remotes/origin/main` succeeds; human output: ' closed on feat at 1630a6c8ef9e · NOT yet on the default branch'. Slash variant: merged False.

**Expected.** merged is True when the commit is on the default branch as either the remote-tracking ref or the local branch has it; False only when the default ref(s) that exist do not contain it; None ('unknown') when the commit is not known here. The default branch named by origin/HEAD is looked up by its full name.

**Proposed fix.** For the first default-branch name that has any ref, check both refs/remotes/origin/NAME and refs/heads/NAME (full ref names, so a tag called main cannot shadow the branch); True if either contains the commit, False if one answered 1, None if git does not know the commit (128). origin/HEAD's target is taken with removeprefix('refs/remotes/origin/') so release/main stays whole. Docstring updated (it said 'the local branch first').

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G61_MergedCheckSeesOriginMain` (2 tests). On e9eed92: FAILED (failures=2): "AssertionError: False is not True : a closing commit already on origin/main is reported as not merged: ✓ mg-und8 · one [P2 · CLOSED] ... closed on feat at 1630a6c8ef9e · NOT yet on the default branch" and "AssertionError: False is not True : a closing commit on origin/release/main (the default branch) is reported as not merged" With the diff: Ran 2 tests in 1.527s OK

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Run against one copy with all four output-group diffs applied (G36, G59, G60, G61 in that order; patch -p1 applied each cleanly, no conflicts), Python 3.14.7, from the repo root with BEAD_UNDER_TEST set to that copy and scratch BEAD_HOME/TMPDIR: tests.test_commands 'Ran 97 tests in 153.538s OK'; tests.test_protocol 'Ran 54 tests in 153.301s OK'; tests.test_storage 'Ran 9 tests in 23.329s OK'; tests.test_replay 'Ran 60 tests in 121.485s OK'. No shipped test needs adapting.

<details><summary>Proposed diff (G61, applies to e9eed92 with <code>patch -p1</code>)</summary>

```diff
--- a/bead
+++ b/bead
@@ -1450,23 +1450,25 @@
 
 
 def merged_into_default(commit: str) -> bool | None:
-    """Whether `commit` is reachable from the default branch as this checkout knows it (the local
-    branch first, then its remote-tracking ref); None when the commit or the branch is unknown here."""
+    """Whether `commit` is reachable from the default branch as this checkout knows it: True when either
+    the remote-tracking ref or the local branch contains it (a local branch in a worktree workflow is often
+    stale); None when the commit or the branch is unknown here."""
     if not commit:
         return None
     names = []
     head = run_git(["symbolic-ref", "-q", "refs/remotes/origin/HEAD"], check=False)
     if head.returncode == 0:
-        names.append(head.stdout.decode().strip().rsplit("/", 1)[-1])
+        names.append(head.stdout.decode().strip().removeprefix("refs/remotes/origin/"))
     names += ["main", "master"]
     for name in dict.fromkeys(names):
-        for ref in (name, f"origin/{name}"):
-            if run_git(["rev-parse", "--verify", "-q", ref], check=False).returncode:
-                continue
-            probe = run_git(["merge-base", "--is-ancestor", commit, ref], check=False)
-            if probe.returncode in (0, 1):
-                return probe.returncode == 0
-            return None  # the commit is not known here
+        refs = [ref for ref in (f"refs/remotes/origin/{name}", f"refs/heads/{name}")
+                if run_git(["rev-parse", "--verify", "-q", ref], check=False).returncode == 0]
+        if not refs:
+            continue
+        codes = [run_git(["merge-base", "--is-ancestor", commit, ref], check=False).returncode for ref in refs]
+        if 0 in codes:
+            return True
+        return False if 1 in codes else None  # None: the commit is not known here
     return None
```
</details>

#### G62 · Low · usability — In a checkout git refuses for dubious ownership (container mounts, shared machines, CI runners), bead says 'not inside a git repository (or set BEAD_WORKSPACE)' and drops git's reason and its safe.directory fix

**Mechanism.** find_workspace runs `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` (bead:196) and `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` (bead:214) with check=False; under dubious ownership both exit 128 with git's refusal and its fix (`git config --global --add safe.directory <path>`) on stderr, and `git config --get remote.origin.url` (bead:205) runs as if outside a repository (rc 1, empty), so no workspace id is derived. bead:215-216 then raise a fixed NO_WORKSPACE "not inside a git repository (or set BEAD_WORKSPACE)" without git's text. Following that hint (BEAD_WORKSPACE) leads to an empty tracker that cannot sync (verifier's evidence).

**Reproduction.** G62.py: Sandbox, Machine a with a scratch GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL, GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1 and GIT_TEST_ASSUME_DIFFERENT_OWNER=1 (git's own knob for the real ownership check; skipped if git does not honour it); `bead info --json`, `bead sync --json`, `bead info`; then the same under LANG=LC_ALL=de_DE.UTF-8.

**Observed.** AssertionError: 'safe.directory' not found in 'not inside a git repository (or set BEAD_WORKSPACE)' : info: git's reason and fix are dropped; human: 'Error: not inside a git repository (or set BEAD_WORKSPACE)\n'

**Expected.** The error carries git's reason, including its safe.directory command, in one readable line and in the user's language.

**Proposed fix.** When `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` fails, NO_WORKSPACE says "no usable git repository here: <git's stderr joined into one line> (outside a repository, set BEAD_WORKSPACE)"; the old text stays only when git printed nothing. No English matching, so it works under a translated git (the safe.directory command is never translated), and joining whitespace keeps visible() from escaping git's newline and tab. Patched: "Error: no usable git repository here: fatal: detected dubious ownership in repository at '.../a-main' To add an exception for this directory, call: git config --global --add safe.directory .../a-main (outside a repository, set BEAD_WORKSPACE)"; outside any repository: "Error: no usable git repository here: fatal: not a git repository (or any of the parent directories): .git (outside a repository, set BEAD_WORKSPACE)". No shipped test refers to NO_WORKSPACE or its text.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G62_DubiousOwnership` (3 tests). On e9eed92: FAILED (failures=3): "AssertionError: 'safe.directory' not found in 'not inside a git repository (or set BEAD_WORKSPACE)' : info: git's reason and fix are dropped: 'not inside a git repository (or set BEAD_WORKSPACE)'"; human: "... not found in 'Error: not inside a git repository (or set BEAD_WORKSPACE)\n'"; de_DE: same as the first With the diff: Ran 3 tests, OK (G62 copy and the combined git-env copy)

**Shipped suites with the diff.** Run against one copy with all six git-env diffs applied together (G17+G19+G20+G26+G27+G62, hand-merged: work-git-env/all/bead, diff work-git-env/git-env-combined.diff), from the repo root with BEAD_UNDER_TEST set: tests.test_protocol "Ran 54 tests in 364.958s OK"; tests.test_storage "Ran 9 tests in 25.254s OK"; tests.test_commands first run "Ran 97 tests in 467.734s FAILED (errors=1)": the error is Review6.test_remote_url_shapes_that_are_already_safe failing in its first line, support.Sandbox.clone's plain `git clone` of the fresh sandbox origin (exit 128) before any bead call, under load average ~168 from other runs; that test then passed 3/3 alone and a full rerun gave "Ran 97 tests in 259.754s OK". No shipped test needs adapting. Textual conflicts (checked pairwise with patch --dry-run): G17, G19, G20 and G62 stack cleanly (applied in that sequence without fuzz); G27 conflicts with G17, G19, G20 and G26 in either order, G26 conflicts with G19 and G27, and G20 does not apply after G26 (G26 does apply after G20): same env and `git init` lines, bead:1604-1625, so the combined copy was merged by hand. G17.diff is identical to hunk 1 of final/g01-g04.diff (apply only one); the rest of g01-g04.diff applies cleanly on top of the combined copy. Independent check (check-git-env): git-env-combined.diff applied to a fresh pristine copy reproduces work-git-env/all/bead byte for byte; a…

<details><summary>Proposed diff (G62, applies to e9eed92 with <code>patch -p1</code>)</summary>

```diff
--- a/bead
+++ b/bead
@@ -213,7 +213,11 @@
     if not wid:
         common = run_git(["rev-parse", "--path-format=absolute", "--git-common-dir"], check=False)
         if common.returncode:
-            raise BpError("NO_WORKSPACE", "not inside a git repository (or set BEAD_WORKSPACE)")
+            # git's own reason, in the user's language: no repository here, or one git refuses to use
+            # (dubious ownership, with the safe.directory command that fixes it). One line, so nothing is escaped.
+            said = " ".join(common.stderr.decode(errors="replace").split())
+            raise BpError("NO_WORKSPACE", f"no usable git repository here: {said} (outside a repository, set BEAD_WORKSPACE)"
+                          if said else "not inside a git repository (or set BEAD_WORKSPACE)")
         wid = "local-" + hashlib.sha256(os.path.realpath(common.stdout.decode().strip()).encode()).hexdigest()[:12]
     return Workspace(wid, name, origin or None, config)
```
</details>

#### G65 · Low · usability — An owner's close or delete that a takeover superseded answers '✓ Closed' (status closed, exit 0) although its own publish has just fetched the takeover and every machine ignores it

**Mechanism.** transition (bead:2266-2287) builds the answer from tx.state inside writing(), before publish_soon (2285); publish_soon's synchronise merges the takeover, and replay then lists the close under stale and keeps the new owner's claim (apply_event close, bead:1067-1071; REVIEWING section 2). Nothing re-reads the state after the publish, so the answer is the pre-publish one.

**Reproduction.** Machines a, b: a init, create X, sync; b sync; a claim X; b sync; b takeover X -r 'a looked dead'; a close X -r done --json; a show X.

**Observed.** a close: rc 0 '✓ Closed so-3wlr: x' ([{... "status": "closed" ...}] with --json); a show X: in_progress, claim owner codex:b-thread-0001@..., stale: ['close'].

**Expected.** The section 2 decision stands: the close is ignored and listed under stale, and (as the shipped test_takeover_fences_the_previous_owner pins) the command still exits 0. But its answer shows the issue as the remote now holds it and says the close was superseded.

**Proposed fix.** transition remembers the claim each owner's close/delete names; after a successful publish_soon it replays (view) and, when that close/delete appears under stale with the same claim, answers with the re-read records, each marked "superseded", and a text line '! X: another owner took over before this close reached the remote; it is ignored'. Exit status unchanged (0). A close whose publish failed keeps the 'recorded locally, not yet published' path.

**Tests.** `tests/test_review7.py`: `G65_SupersededOwnerCloseSaysSo`. On e9eed92: AssertionError: (0, 'closed') == (0, 'closed') : the close answered closed with exit 0, yet it was superseded and ignored With the diff: Ran 1 test OK (status in_progress, "superseded" set, exit 0)

**Shipped suites with the diff.** G63+G64+G65 applied together (patch -p1, no conflicts), from the repo root with scratch BEAD_HOME/TMPDIR: tests.test_protocol Ran 54 OK; tests.test_commands Ran 97 OK (G63+G64 alone also: test_replay 60 OK, test_storage 9 OK). A first version of this diff failed the command with a new SUPERSEDED code (exit 1); that broke test_commands.BpTest.test_takeover_fences_the_previous_owner, which pins exit 0 for the old owner's stale close, so the diff now keeps exit 0 and corrects only the answer. No shipped test needs adapting.

<details><summary>Proposed diff (G65, applies to e9eed92 with <code>patch -p1</code>)</summary>

```diff
--- a/bead
+++ b/bead
@@ -2265,7 +2265,7 @@
 
 def transition(op: str, verb: str):
     def command(args, ws, actor):
-        ended = False
+        ended, named = False, {}
         with writing(ws, actor) as tx:
             need_tracker(tx.state)
             me = actor_label(actor, tx.replica)
@@ -2277,13 +2277,24 @@
                                   "use `bead takeover` first, or --force")
                 data = {"reason": args.reason} if args.reason else {}
                 if op in ("close", "delete") and held and held["owner"] == me:
-                    data["claim"] = held["id"]  # ends exactly this claim, never a successor's
+                    data["claim"] = named[issue["uid"]] = held["id"]  # ends exactly this claim, never a successor's
                 ended = ended or bool(held)
                 tx.emit(op, issue["uid"], data)
             st = tx.state
             result = [brief(st, st.resolve(key)) for key in args.ids]
         warning = publish_soon(ws, args, actor) if ended else None
-        return result, [f"✓ {verb} {r['id']}: {r['title']}" for r in result] + ([warning] if warning else [])
+        notes = [warning] if warning else []
+        if named and not warning:  # published: answer with what the remote made of it
+            st = view(ws)
+            lost = {uid for uid, claim in named.items()
+                    if any(s["op"] == op and s.get("claim") == claim for s in st.issues[uid]["stale"])}
+            if lost:
+                result = [brief(st, st.resolve(key)) for key in args.ids]
+                for r in result:
+                    if st.resolve(r["id"])["uid"] in lost:
+                        r["superseded"] = f"another owner took over before this {op} reached the remote; it is ignored"
+                        notes.append(f"! {r['id']}: {r['superseded']} (see `bead show`, under stale)")
+        return result, [f"✓ {verb} {r['id']}: {r['title']}" for r in result] + notes
     return command
```
</details>

## Design observations

Not bugs, or not shown to be; kept apart from the findings above and from §2.

- **"Goes through" needs a definition.** F01's fix equates "a later command went through" with
  "its build succeeded" (§2's receipt rule). G08 shows the gap: build is before the push. G07 shows
  the other side: a local write that did go through does not retire the receipt. The rule would be
  easier to keep if it said "the remote accepted it, or it was a local write that was appended".
- **A retried close emits a second close event.** In the G12 schedules the retry appends another
  `close` (and `closed_at` moves). Harmless today; worth knowing for anyone reading `log`.
- **Per-call remotes persist.** `BEAD_REMOTE=x bead sync` once makes `x` the "last used" remote,
  which then outranks origin on later plain commands; README says `--remote` and `$BEAD_REMOTE`
  apply per call. Related to G25, not covered by its diff.
- **`init --tracked` with `BEAD_WORKSPACE` set still mints a new uuid** (bead:1965).
- **Mixed versions.** G14 and G15 change how existing events replay, without a format change: a
  crew running old and new bead disagrees on those edges until everyone upgrades. G15's diff fixes
  `create --parent` and renames against imports already applied, not an import that sorts after
  the colliding creates.
- **Two first-ever syncs on a fresh store** can fail once with `GIT_ERROR` "cannot mkdir … sync.git:
  File exists", contrary to the comment at bead:1620. Seen once by a verifier, not reproduced on
  demand, no test; G27's diff replaces that code.
- **A full disk under git surfaces as `GIT_ERROR`**, not `IO` (bpcakes; and in the investigators'
  disk-image runs a `claim` got `GIT_ERROR` "object … is unavailable" twice out of four). Nothing
  was half-applied in any of those runs.
- **Human output of git's multi-line stderr** inside `REMOTE_UNREACHABLE` shows `\x0a` escapes:
  `visible()` is doing its job on text that would read better split into lines.
- **stdout that cannot grow** (a full volume, `ulimit -f`) still ends in exit 120 and "Exception
  ignored while flushing sys.stdout": the stdout counterpart of F16, not fixed by G36's diff and not
  tested here. `bead CMD --help` into a closed pipe had the same exit 120; G36's diff covers that one.
- **Remote maintenance.** `git gc --prune=now` run *concurrently* with pushes corrupted a bare
  remote in 4 of 4 runs (the ref then names a missing commit or tree): git's documented hazard, not
  bead's. Run after every operation, or `repack`/`gc --auto` concurrently, it was harmless (verified
  below). G41 is how bead reports the corrupted case.
- **Shipped tests that misbehave under load.**
  `Protocol3.test_recovered_atomic_update_is_not_applied_again_after_a_later_observed_edit` mocks the
  global `time.sleep`; under load `subprocess.communicate(timeout=…)` calls it while `coordinated`
  holds the store lock, the mock runs `bead sync`, and that blocks on the lock forever (one such run
  hung for 2 h 53 min before I killed it). `ActualTransportReview.test_timed_out_accepted_release_returns_success`
  (0.1 s timeout) fails under load on pristine and patched alike. `test_compat`'s real-export test
  looks for `infra/compat_real.py` next to `BEAD_UNDER_TEST`, so it cannot run against a patched
  copy. `tests/__init__.py` leaves an empty `bp-tests-home-*` in `TMPDIR` on every import, even when
  `BEAD_HOME` is set.
- **`private_dir` re-modes a user-chosen `BEAD_HOME`.** An existing 0775 team directory given as
  `BEAD_HOME` becomes 0700 on the first write.

## Files bead creates, and their modes

Measured, not read from the code: a full two-machine sequence (init `--tracked --remote`, create,
comments, claim, release, takeover, close, a lost acknowledgement, a crash before push, a torn tail,
a foreign fork, `machines --name` as the first command, `export -o`, a SIGKILL between a temporary
write and its rename), every path `lstat`ed under umask 022, 002 and 077. e9eed92 only; the same
table with G47 applied, and the scripts, are in my scratch directory (`final/modes-table.md`).

| where | path | 022 | 002 | 077 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| store | `$BEAD_HOME`, `<ws>` (after any write) | `drwx------` | `drwx------` | `drwx------` |
| store | `$BEAD_HOME` when `machines --name` is the first command | `drwxr-xr-x` | `drwxrwxr-x` | `drwx------` |
| store | `<ws>/events`, `events/<m>`, `events/<m>/claims`, `<ws>/pending` | `drwxr-xr-x` | `drwxrwxr-x` | `drwx------` |
| store | segments `events/<m>/NNNNNN.jsonl` | `-rw-------` | `-rw-------` | `-rw-------` |
| store | attempt files `claims/<attempt>.json` | `-rw-------` | `-rw-------` | `-rw-------` |
| store | `pending/<attempt>.json`, `pending/<attempt>.command` | `-rw-------` | `-rw-------` | `-rw-------` |
| store | `meta.json`, `replica.json`, `aliases.json` | `-rw-------` | `-rw-------` | `-rw-------` |
| store | `<ws>/lock` | `-rw-r--r--` | `-rw-rw-r--` | `-rw-------` |
| store | `*.torn-<secs>` (repaired tail), `*.fork-<secs>` (damaged segment or attempt kept aside) | `-rw-r--r--` | `-rw-rw-r--` | `-rw-------` |
| store | `.<name>.<pid>.tmp` left by a kill or a failed write (claims/, pending/, events/) | `-rw-------` | `-rw-------` | `-rw-------` |
| store | `sync.git/` directories (hooks, info, objects/…, refs/…) | `drwxr-xr-x` | `drwxrwxr-x` | `drwx------` |
| store | `sync.git/HEAD`, `config`, `description`, `info/exclude`, `refs/beads/remote`, `FETCH_HEAD` (holds the remote URL) | `-rw-r--r--` | `-rw-rw-r--` | `-rw-------` |
| store | `sync.git/hooks/*.sample` (14) | `-rwxr-xr-x` | `-rwxrwxr-x` | `-rwx------` |
| store | `sync.git` loose objects (every event is in one), and packs after gc | `-r--r--r--` | `-r--r--r--` | `-r--------` |
| checkout | `.beadroll.json`, `export -o FILE` (and parent directories it creates) | `-rw-r--r--` | `-rw-rw-r--` | `-rw-------` |
| checkout | `.<name>.<pid>.tmp` left by a failed or killed `export -o` (a full or partial export) | `-rw-r--r--` | `-rw-rw-r--` | `-rw-------` |

Under umask 022, 289 of 316 store paths carry a group or other bit; under 077, none. Privacy holds
only because `$BEAD_HOME` and `<ws>` are 0700 after any write (G47). The checkout rows follow the
umask by decision (§2, F04); the temporary files there are G48.

## Verified properties

Each is a passing test class in `tests/test_review7.py` (`V1_…` to `V9_…`), run twice on e9eed92 by the agent that wrote it and again in the final run of the module. The sweep's property is above (`Sweep`).

**V1, run_git and push outcomes.**

- `V1_PushSideTranslation.test_git_keeps_the_rejection_tokens_bead_reads_untranslated`: Push-side tokens that Link.publish reads stay English under translated git. Tested with pure git in 6 locale settings (C, LANG=de_DE.UTF-8, LC_ALL=de_DE.UTF-8, LANG=fr_FR.UTF-8, LANG=fr_FR.ISO8859-1, LANGUAGE=de with LANG=en_US.UTF-8). The cases are a client-side non-fast-forward (stderr plus porcelain line), a pre-receive refusal ([remote rejected] (pre-receive hook declined)) and a server-side race ('remote: …
- `V1_PushSideTranslation.test_lost_races_are_recognised_and_retried_under_translated_git`: bead end to end under LANG=de_DE.UTF-8, LANG=fr_FR.UTF-8, and LANGUAGE=de with LANG=en_US.UTF-8. A pre-receive hook moves the ref once, so the sync loses a server-side race. The sync still returns rc=0 with attempts=2. The first publication runs with LC_ALL=C (lead observation 1). The ambient LC_ALL, LC_MESSAGES and LANGUAGE are blanked, and a subtest skips when git is not translated. Mutation check: with the …
- `V1_StalledReceiveTimeouts.test_stalled_receive_with_a_stderr_flood_returns_within_the_timeout`: A pre-receive hook writes 200 KiB to stderr, then sleeps 4 s. With BEAD_NET_TIMEOUT=1.5, sync returns REMOTE_UNREACHABLE in under 3.5 s. The receive side finishes on its own, and the next sync returns rc 0 with no pending ownership. I added one check: a second machine then sees the comment on the remote. The review measured about 2.33 s at a 2 s timeout.
- `V1_StalledReceiveTimeouts.test_claim_whose_ack_is_held_by_a_slow_post_receive_is_recovered`: A claim whose acknowledgement is held by a slow post-receive (4 s, after the ref update) at BEAD_NET_TIMEOUT=1.5 is recovered: rc 0, claim generation 1, no pending ownership. The test waits for the hook to finish before cleanup.
- `V1_RunGitTimeoutHygiene.test_timeouts_leak_nothing_and_reap_as_documented`: 20 in-process run_git calls per mode, timeout=0.2, against a git alias whose helper sleeps 3 s. Each call raises REMOTE_UNREACHABLE, and each mode finishes in under 6 s. The /dev/fd count is unchanged and the test process has no zombie children. With reap=True no helper survives; with reap=False helpers survive, by design (F15). The review measured 4.08 s and 4.10 s, fds 4->4, 0 zombies, 0 and 13 leftovers. Changes …

**V2, receipts and pending attempts.**

- `V2_ReceiptsArePerActor.test_other_actor_on_the_same_machine_never_drops_a_receipt`: Receipts are per actor. On the same machine, another thread's claim that crashes after build (rc 98) and its successful claim (rc 0) both leave A's settled receipt for its lost-ack release in place, and A's retry `release X` is recognised. The test checks: 1 .command left; retry rc 0; X open with no claim; exactly 1 release event; Y claimed by thread 'other'.
- `V2_InFlightAttemptAcrossActors.test_another_actor_fences_an_in_flight_release_during_the_unlocked_sleep`: (e) A's release is in flight (stalled pre-receive hook, BEAD_NET_TIMEOUT=1). During A's unlocked sleep between rounds, actor B on the same machine fences A's attempt and drops it with its receipt (pending is empty after B). A's next round rebuilds and releases exactly once. The test checks: A rc 0; B's claim rc 0; no pending files; X open, no claim, 1 release on m and on a fresh peer; Y held by actor-b; remote …
- `V2_InFlightAttemptAcrossActors.test_in_flight_release_lands_before_another_actors_command`: (e), other order: A's in-flight push lands first. B's merge confirms A's attempt and keeps A's receipt (pending after B is exactly one .command). A's next round returns its result. The test checks: A rc 0; X open; 1 release on m and the peer; Y held by actor-b; remote history claim, claim, claim, with no fence.
- `V2_ConfirmedTwinKeepsTheRetryRecognised.test_identical_pending_twin_confirms_and_the_retry_is_recognised`: Q03 shape (the repro_d control, undamaged twin). An in-process release crashes at drop_pending after local confirmation and leaves a pending .json twin, byte-identical to the confirmed claims file, plus its .command receipt. The retry `release X` exits 0, pending is empty afterwards, X is open, and there is exactly 1 release event.

**V3, decoding and validation.**

- `V3_NonUtf8IdentityAndText.test_non_utf8_identity_and_text_are_validation_and_write_nothing`: Non-UTF-8 identity or text in argv or the environment (--actor, BEAD_THREAD on claim and on update --claim, BEAD_AGENT, BEAD_ACTOR, --author) gives a structured VALIDATION error and writes nothing. F07 holds for argv/env. The test checks 6 cases: rc 1, error == {code: VALIDATION, message: 'text is not valid UTF-8'}, no traceback, export byte-identical afterwards, pending_ownership == [].
- `V3_ImportShapeErrors.test_import_shape_errors_are_validation`: Import records with the wrong container type for labels ('a,b', [1]) or dependencies (dict, str), or with a wrong-typed comment id (1.5) or text (5), are rejected with VALIDATION and no traceback. All 6 cases. Afterwards info integrity == [] and list total == 0.
- `V3_ForeignFolderDefaultQuotePath.test_non_utf8_ref_folder_does_not_crash_sync_under_default_quotepath`: Under git's default core.quotePath (empty GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL, GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1), a foreign coordination-ref folder named events/caf\xe9/ or events/line<U+2028>sep/ does not crash sync. Sync returns rc 0 with no traceback, and the local events folders are unchanged (the folder is skipped). A separate one-off check, not part of the file, confirmed the fixture reaches ls-tree: with core.quotePath=false the same …

**V4, dangling dependencies.**

- `V4_DanglingReplayDeterminism.test_unique_key_histories_replay_identically_under_any_delivery_order`: Replay is deterministic for dangling histories. The same events give the same projection (edges by key plus unresolved dangling keys) under any whole-journal delivery order, and replay leaves nothing pending and flags no integrity problems. The test runs 1000 seeds x 4 shuffled orders on three replicas, with imports carrying dangling keys, key- and uid-based dep_removes, explicit dep_adds and random pairwise syncs. …
- `V4_DanglingReplayDeterminism.test_twin_key_histories_replay_identically_under_any_delivery_order`: The same determinism with twin keys, where a key may be minted on two replicas and one copy is renamed. The test runs 1000 seeds x 4 delivery orders. On the pristine bead that comes to about 10,350 events, with 929 key-based and 502 uid-based removals, and 564 seeds mint a key twice. The review measured 10,019 events, 551 seeds with renames and 0 nondeterministic. The mutation check fails this test too.
- `V4_DanglingOrdersThatHold.test_key_retraction_that_sorts_first_holds`: End to end on two Machines. m1 imports ex-a, which depends on ex-b; ex-b exists nowhere yet, so the dependency is a dangling entry. m1 has not seen ex-b, and the test asserts that precondition. m1 then removes the dependency by key before m2 imports ex-b (which depends on ex-a). After settling, the two exports are equal, ex-a has no edge on either machine, and dep cycles reports 0. The review recorded 3/3 order …
- `V4_DanglingOrdersThatHold.test_uid_retraction_after_the_target_arrived_holds`: Once the target's create has arrived, the dangling entry becomes an edge, and m1 sees ex-a -> ex-b (blocks). A uid-based dep remove by m1 then removes the edge on both machines, and the exports converge. The review recorded 3/3 passing.
- `V4_DanglingOrdersThatHold.test_concurrent_explicit_add_survives_a_key_retraction`: Add-wins. m2 sees ex-b arrive, removes the imported edge and adds it back explicitly. Concurrently, m1, which has not seen ex-b (the test asserts this), removes the dependency by key. After settling, ex-a -> ex-b (blocks) remains on both machines. The review recorded 3/3 passing.

**V5, file modes and side files.**

- `V5_DataFileModes.test_data_files_are_0600_and_checkout_files_follow_umask`: Data files bead writes into its own store are 0600 under umask 022, 002 and 077. This covers segments, claims/*.json, pending/*.json and *.command (checked after both a crash before push (rc 98) and a lost ack (rc 99)), meta.json, replica.json and aliases.json. BEAD_HOME and <ws> are 0700. The checkout files .beadroll.json and export -o follow the umask (0644, 0664 or 0600).
- `V5_Retighten.test_a_write_retightens_a_relaxed_store`: A write (create) chmods BEAD_HOME and <ws> back to 0700. A read (list) leaves them alone: 0755/0755, then 0755/0755 after list, then 0700/0700 after create.
- `V5_Retighten.test_existing_group_directory_becomes_owner_only`: An existing 0775 BEAD_HOME (a team directory) is silently made 0700 by init. This is a design observation.
- `V5_GitDirInHook.test_sync_repo_lands_in_the_store_with_git_dir_set`: With GIT_DIR set to either the relative '.git' or an absolute path, sync returns rc 0 and sync.git/HEAD lands in the store, not the checkout. The checkout's core.bare stays 'false' before and after, and git status returns rc 0.
- `V5_SideFilesIgnored.test_leftover_side_files_are_never_read_as_journal`: Side files are never read as segments or attempts. This covers .X.<pid>.tmp, *.torn-* and *.fork-* in events/<m>/, and .tmp and *.fork-* in claims/. After writing them: sync integrity 0, info integrity [], segments 1, and export byte-identical.
- `V5_KillAtRename.test_kill_at_rename_keeps_targets_whole`: A sitecustomize on PYTHONPATH sends SIGKILL at os.replace. Export -o killed this way returns rc -9, the target keeps 'previous export' and a .issues.jsonl.<pid>.tmp is left over. A claim killed at its claims/ rename returns rc -9 and leaves a claims/.tmp plus 2 pending files. Retrying the claim returns rc 0 with integrity [] and pending_ownership [], and the owner is the claiming thread. The test skips if python3 …
- `V5_SigningConfigIsInert.test_global_commit_signing_does_not_affect_claims`: A scratch GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL sets commit.gpgsign=true and a gpg.program that logs and exits 2. Under it, claim and sync both return rc 0, the gpg log is never created, and the claim owner is correct (git 2.55). A separate scratch check confirmed this fake gpg makes a porcelain `git commit` fail, while `commit-tree` never calls it. That shows the test can tell the two cases apart.

**V6, local state and IO errors.**

- `V6_UserSuppliedPaths.test_oserror_on_a_user_supplied_input_path_is_io_and_writes_nothing`: OSError on user-supplied input paths (ENOENT, EISDIR, EACCES, ELOOP, ENAMETOOLONG, ENOTDIR) x 5 commands (update/create --description-file, comments add -f, import, update --claim --description-file) is IO exit 1, no traceback, event count unchanged, no claim (30/30)
- `V6_UserSuppliedPaths.test_export_output_path_errors_are_io_and_missing_parents_are_created`: export -o into EISDIR/EACCES/ENAMETOOLONG/ENOTDIR paths is IO, and so is /dev/stdout (that case runs only when /dev is not writable); missing parent dirs are created and the export exits 0 (the export -o half of the same verified property)
- `V6_StoreDamage.test_store_damage_is_answered_with_a_structured_code`: 6 store damages (lock dir, lock 000, segment 000, segment dir, pending/ as a file, claims 0500) x 5 commands (show, create, claim, sync, comments add): no traceback, and any failure is IO, GIT_ERROR or REMOTE_UNREACHABLE (the review ran 80 cases with 0 tracebacks)
- `V6_FileSizeLimit.test_file_size_limit_is_io_and_the_next_write_and_sync_are_clean`: under ulimit -f 200, create with a 300 KB description and a sync that pulls a 300 KB segment are both IO; the next create and sync exit 0, integrity is [], and the pulled issue is present
- `V6_DescriptorExhaustion.test_descriptor_exhaustion_never_drops_a_pending_attempt`: after a lost-ack update --claim --status closed, show/claim other/sync under ulimit -n 8, 10, 11 give no traceback and leave the pending .json and .command in place; the retry exits 0 and the issue is closed
- `V6_IdentityAndConfigShapes.test_identity_and_tracked_config_shapes_are_validated`: replica.json with an integer alias or extra keys still lets create/machines/claims succeed; bad .beadroll.json refs (refs/heads/x, refs/beads/../x, refs/beads/x.lock, 'refs/beads/a b') and workspaces ('.', '..', 101 chars, 'wö', 'w1\n') are CONFIG; unknown keys are accepted

**V7, remote URLs.**

- `V7_OriginsWithAUserOrSchemeAreOneWorkspace.test_shapes_with_a_user_or_scheme_give_one_workspace_everywhere`: Origins with a user part or a scheme (scp git@host:..., ssh://, https://, git://) give one workspace id, and info reports the origin as the remote, in a clone, its linked worktree, a subdirectory and a second clone (16 info calls, the one id 987c37c1f56322a0 asserted exactly).
- `V7_OriginsWithAUserOrSchemeAreOneWorkspace.test_scp_origin_with_a_user_syncs_over_ssh`: Control from the same property: with git@myhost:origin.git reached through a fake GIT_SSH_COMMAND that runs the remote command in the sandbox (scratch GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL, GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1), init then sync gives rc 0 and pushed 1, refs/beads/journal reaches the bare origin, and the clone, its worktree and a second clone all report id 606ef04ef5c6313e with the origin as the remote.
- `V7_PasswordShapesFollowGit.test_git_confirmed_password_shapes_are_dropped`: without_password table from the review: 8 shapes lose their password (user:pw, p%40ss, upper-case HTTPS, :TOKEN, https TOKEN@, IPv6 with port, ssh user:pw, persistent-https), and 7 stay unchanged (ssh ports and users, IPv6, scp with a user, ssh://git%40corp@, '@' in local and file:// paths).
- `V7_PasswordShapesFollowGit.test_http_secrets_git_sends_are_the_ones_bead_drops`: The oracle half of the same property, run inside the test: a 127.0.0.1 http.server that answers 401 and records Authorization, plus git ls-remote with a scratch HOME and config, no proxies and no prompts. Git sends the secret for alice:secret, alice:p%40ss (sent as p@ss), HTTP:// upper-case, :TOKEN and TOKEN@. With bead's rewritten URL git sends no secret and still sends the user alice where the URL had one.
- `V7_PasswordShapesFollowGit.test_ssh_logins_bead_keeps_are_what_git_hands_ssh`: The ssh half of the oracle, through a logging GIT_SSH_COMMAND that exits 1. Git hands ssh 'git:secret@host.example', and after bead's rewrite 'git@host.example' with no secret. It hands 'git@corp@host.example' for ssh://git%40corp@, the host 'alice' for scp alice:secret@host:path, and '-p 2222 git@host.example' for a port. bead leaves those and scp git@host unchanged, which matches git.
- `V7_NormalizeRemote.test_spellings_of_one_remote_share_an_id_and_ports_separate`: normalize_remote reduces 6 spellings of one repository (scp, ssh://, https with .git and a trailing slash, an https user part, host case, git://, git+ssh://) to 1 string, and keeps 3 port variants as 3 strings.

**V8, terminal, remote maintenance, Python 3.12.**

- `V8_PtyOutputMatchesPipeOutput.test_pty_output_matches_pipe_output_and_escapes_hostile_text`: Under a PTY, human output equals the pipe output modulo CRLF, no raw C0/C1/bidi/LS/PS character reaches the terminal, errors go only to stderr, and exit codes match. The review measured 75 runs: rc 75/75, stderr 75/75, stdout 69/75, with the 6 differences being ENV-2.
- `V8_SyncUnderAControllingTerminalCannotPrompt.test_sync_under_a_controlling_terminal_cannot_prompt`: sync under a controlling terminal cannot prompt: git and ssh run in their own session, so a transport that wants /dev/tty fails fast (review: rc 1 after 0.44 s, no prompt).
- `V8_HangupOfTheControllingTerminal.test_hangup_of_the_controlling_terminal_ends_bead_quietly`: Closing the controlling terminal mid-output ends bead quietly by SIGHUP with no traceback (review: rc -SIGHUP after reading 1 KB of `list --all --limit 0` over 40 issues with 1500-char titles).
- `V8_RemoteMaintenance.test_gc_prune_now_aggressive_after_every_remote_operation`: Running gc --prune=now --aggressive on the bare remote after every sync/claim/release (not concurrently) leaves convergence and ownership intact. Review: in every mode, exports and claims identical, owners equal to the model, integrity [], waiting 0, pending [], fsck 0.
- `V8_RemoteMaintenance.test_repack_and_auto_gc_running_concurrently_with_pushes_and_fetches`: repack -adf + prune-packed and gc --auto (gc.auto=1) looping concurrently with pushes and fetches cause no maintenance error, no unexpected bead error and no divergence. Review: 4740 and 8529 runs, 0 errors, model 17/17.
- `V8_PruneOnThePrivateSyncRepo.test_prune_now_on_the_private_sync_repo_during_syncs_is_transient`: gc --prune=now looping on a machine's own sync.git during its syncs causes only transient GIT_ERROR or REMOTE_UNREACHABLE. Nothing is left pending and the next syncs converge (review: 89 GIT_ERROR and 2 REMOTE_UNREACHABLE, all transient).
- `V8_Python312.test_storage_module_passes_with_bead_itself_running_on_312`: On CPython 3.12 the suite passes with both the runner and every bead subprocess on 3.12 (review: 225 tests OK across six modules, test_storage 9 OK in 11.8 s).
- `V8_RecoveryFromAPrunedRemote.test_resetting_the_ref_to_the_last_valid_commit_converges_without_loss`: Recovery from a remote corrupted by gc --prune=now: resetting refs/beads/journal to the last valid commit lets all machines converge with no local data lost, and the claimant republishes the acknowledged claim from the lost commit (review: exports identical, same confirmed claims on a, b and c, integrity [], fsck clean, no claims possible during the outage).

**V9, the git environment.**

- `V9_HarmlessGlobalGitSettings.test_signing_push_and_fetch_settings_are_harmless`: 0: global git settings reach sync.git without harm in a two-machine round (identical exports and shows, ALREADY_CLAIMED on the competing claim, status closed). This part: commit.gpgSign with ssh format and a missing key, push.gpgSign=if-asked, push.default=nothing, transfer/receive.fsckObjects, fetch.prune+pruneTags
- `V9_HarmlessGlobalGitSettings.test_protocol_and_repository_settings_are_harmless`: 0 (continued): protocol.version=0, protocol.version=1, safe.bareRepository=explicit, core.fsmonitor=true
- `V9_HarmlessGlobalGitSettings.test_format_and_housekeeping_settings_are_harmless`: 0 (continued): init.defaultRefFormat=reftable, gc.auto=1 with autoDetach=false, core.autocrlf+safecrlf, color.ui=always
- `V9_CheckoutHooksDoNotRunOnBeadsPush.test_checkout_and_husky_pre_push_hooks_never_run`: 1: a refusing .git/hooks/pre-push and a refusing husky pre-push (core.hooksPath=.husky) never run on bead sync or claim; the claim succeeds (in_progress)
- `V9_CallerGitEnvironment.test_hook_and_alias_git_environment_keeps_workspace_and_claims`: 2: a caller's GIT_DIR, GIT_DIR+GIT_WORK_TREE, GIT_INDEX_FILE+GIT_PREFIX, GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS, fixed author/committer dates, or GIT_TRACE=1 leave the workspace the same, and claim and release rc 0 (6/6)
- `V9_EveryCheckoutShapeIsOneWorkspace.test_checkout_shapes_of_one_repository_share_the_workspace`: 3: a subdirectory, a linked worktree, a detached worktree, a bare clone, a shallow clone, an unborn HEAD with the same origin, and a submodule checkout all resolve to the same untracked, URL-derived workspace and read the same issue (7/7)
- `V9_TransportAndUrlSettings.test_git_ssh_command_in_the_environment_is_honoured`: 4a: bead sync honours a GIT_SSH_COMMAND that is already in the environment; the wrapper is invoked for both git-upload-pack and git-receive-pack, and the journal reaches origin. No real ssh runs: the URL is ssh://fakehost<sandbox path>, a fake ssh first on PATH and a global core.sshCommand both refuse, and the wrapper runs the remote command locally
- `V9_TransportAndUrlSettings.test_global_push_instead_of_is_honoured`: 4b: a global url.<primary>.pushInsteadOf=<read-only mirror> is honoured. bead sync rc 0 (the review measured 0.4 s; the test asserts under 30 s), and the journal lands on the primary, not the mirror
- `V9_TransportAndUrlSettings.test_relative_origin_resolves_like_git`: 4c: with origin ../origin.git, the top and src/ (which holds a decoy origin.git directory) give the same workspace and git fetch rc 0. In a linked worktree one level deeper, and in its subdirectory, git fetch gives rc 128 and bead's workspace differs from the checkout's

| §5 claims: superseded pre-pass | ran (partly) | takeover × every boundary in the sweep; G65 (stale close answer) |

| §5 journal integrity: kill -9 at every boundary, rotation, merge rule | ran (partly) | kill during attempt writes and confirmation in the sweep; torn tail, foreign fork in the modes run; G52, G63, G64 |
| §5 fields, labels, dependencies | ran (dangling only) | G14, G15, G33, G42-G46 |
| §5 readiness against `br` on real trackers | ran via the shipped test | Mac: all match (compat OK); linux-1: 48 of 52 match, 3 `blocked` differences not diagnosed, 1 import refused |
| §5 sync protocol: slow push, retry exhaustion, refusals | ran | G16, G18, G19, G58; timeouts with stalled and flooding hooks (no hang, no zombie) |

| §5 performance and scale (10k-200k events) | not run | — |
| §5 CLI: `--json`, exit codes, envelopes | ran (partly) | G36, G37, G56, G59, G60; PTY comparison of 25 commands × 3 locales |
| §6.1 randomised model | ran (shipped) | `test_stress` on Mac 3.14, linux-1 3.12.3, arch 3.14, Mac 3.12.10 |
| §6.2 fault injection: kill -9, crash hooks, disk full, read-only store, clock skew | ran except clock skew | sweep; full and read-only HFS+ images (Mac, by an investigator); clock skew not run |

## How this review was run, and side effects

**Method.** I read REVIEWING.md, README.md, `bead` in full, `tests/support.py` and `test_protocol`'s
fault helpers, wrote the sweep driver and ran it myself, and confirmed four findings directly (G12,
G17, G18, G37). The focus list of the brief was then split into nine areas, each given to an
investigating agent inside this Claude Code session; every reported finding went to a separate
verifying agent that reproduced it from scratch and was told to refute it (63 of 63 reproduced, 0
refuted), plus a completeness critic. A second round of agents (one per area, each followed by an
independent checker) turned every confirmed finding into the clean diff and failing test above; a
third turned verified properties into passing tests. G63, G64 and G65 are mine, from the second
round's reports and one check of my own. In all 104 agent runs (73 investigating and verifying, 22 finalising and checking, 9 for the
verified-property tests), all inside this session; no `codex exec` or separate `claude` sessions were
started.

**Side effects.**

- Mac: everything under this session's scratch directory
  (`/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-aa-Documents-beadroll/51e3ce5f-…/scratchpad/`), including
  sandboxes, scratch `BEAD_HOME`s, patched copies of `bead`, logs and scripts. `BEAD_HOME` was set
  for every bead run; `~/.local/state/beadroll` was not used (its mtime is unchanged). bead never ran
  with its cwd inside a real repository. In the repository: `tests/test_review7.py` and this report
  are new; running the tests from the repository root wrote `tests/__pycache__/test_review7.*.pyc`
  (ignored by git). `./check.sh` itself reads `~/Documents/*/.beads/issues.jsonl` read-only in
  `test_compat`, as it always does.
- One investigating agent ran `pkill -f "./check.sh"` to stop its own run and also killed a sibling
  agent's `check.sh` (this review's, no one else's); that run was repeated. A verifier's
  `test_protocol` run deadlocked (the `time.sleep` mock above) and ran 2 h 53 min until I killed it
  and its child `bead sync`, after checking from their environment that both were this review's.
  Agents briefly ran HTTP servers on 127.0.0.1 ephemeral ports (credential and 403 oracles), attached
  and detached small HFS+ disk images inside scratch (deleted afterwards), ran `br` 0.5.7 only in
  scratch repositories with `HOME` pointed at scratch, read a few keys of the real `~/.gitconfig`
  with `git config --get-regexp` (read-only), and once created and deleted a stray `/tmp/null`.
- linux-1: `/tmp/bead-review7.XN7lOq` (8.6 MB left: the repository snapshot, sweep results, logs).
  The shipped real-export test and one direct run of `infra/compat_real.py` read
  `~/Documents/*/.beads/issues.jsonl` read-only and worked in temporary repositories; the directory
  that direct run left was deleted. A queued shell of mine whose `pgrep -f` matched itself was killed.
- linux-2: `/tmp/bead-review7.rH67XR` (3.4 MB: snapshot, sweep results, `test_stress` output).
- linux-3: `/tmp/bead-review7.o2TNhq` (1.4 MB of text outputs left). Its disk was already
  full from other work on that machine; when I noticed, I stopped my probe and removed my 186 MB of
  sandboxes. One `pkill -f` of mine matched its own shell and ended that command.
- No pushes to GitHub or any network remote; no change to ssh, git, firewall or shell configuration
  on any machine; no `br`/`bv` in a real repository.
- One action was refused by the session's permission policy (reading private tracker content on
  linux-1 to diagnose the `blocked` differences); it was not attempted another way.
